Answered in writing: (8) a `P0002` from a Settlement writer cannot be reached once the plan check has passed under the locks, so the service reports every `P0002` as not found (the draft or Settlement); (9) the confirm's internal invariant refusals (the exact-records closure check, the snapshot sums, 23514) are reported as a stale draft; they cannot be reached by a draft the plan check accepted, and the refusal writes nothing; (10) more than one current physical move-out cannot be created (the move-out record's own checks keep one current record), so that refusal stays defensive, as in `app.start_final_settlement`; (11) a deferred check tying every closure and every Deposit application to a CONFIRMED decision would refuse the owner-written closures of the earlier tests (8a-1, 8e-1); only the confirm writes them, so it stays a slice 12 item; (12) an active account with a non-human identity cannot be created, so the confirm's 42501 for it is defensive; (13) the receipt reversal wait of the decision trigger stays defense in depth: the plan check's pending-draft issue refuses first; (14) a two-connection test of the Lease row position is left optional, as the plan says; (15) for the owner: the confirm is one statement under the shared 3-second statement timeout of the Deposit services (`DEPOSIT_QUERY_TIMEOUT_MS`); a timeout rolls everything back and is reported as an unresolved outcome to replay; a much larger Settlement than the tests' may need its own timeout (slice 12 item, owner decision).
- **V1-056 sub-slice 8e-3 review round 2 (independent and security, 2026-10-06):** no blocker; security no should-fix; independent two should-fix, both fixed (tests only): a confirm by an approver who did not prepare the draft, with the decision, `closed_by`, the snapshots' `included_by`, the audit actor, the Credit and Deposit applications and the Refunds naming the approver and the closure snapshot naming the preparer; the current physical move-out proven by a move-out corrected after the Settlement started (the snapshot names the correction, `move_out_date_snapshot` keeps the start-time date). Notes taken: the Late-payment Charge snapshot UPDATE and DELETE refusals are tested; a snapshot insert no longer relies on Charges of earlier modules; wording (an unknown Property; the second deadlock pair in choice (1) above; the traceability count). For the owner (adds to answer 15): each snapshot row re-runs the whole closure reconciliation at commit, so the confirm's work grows faster than the Lease's history; measure with a long Lease in slice 12 before choosing one check per Settlement or a confirm timeout. Answered: a server whose default isolation is not READ COMMITTED would see every confirm refused as a conflict (the service never changes the default).
- **V1-056 sub-slice 8e-3 review round 3 (independent and security, 2026-10-06):** no blocker and no should-fix, so 8e-3 is committed under the standing round-3 rule. Security found nothing; the independent notes are documents (the rule-ID headings, the docs/004 wording of the deferred check) or tests for slice 12 (the insert check's own message, failing cases of the line-snapshot and Charge-snapshot checks, `included_by` of a Charge snapshot under a second approver). After round 3 only documents changed; the committed migration, code and tests are exactly what U3 and P5 ran.
- **Owner follow-up to Q-2026-10-06-34 (2026-10-06, after the sub-slice 8e-3 report):** leave it out, as recommended; the answer also covers next month's Rent Invoice that the hand-back resolution VOIDs when the Customer leaves between the 25th and the month end (slice 5, coverage `NEXT_MONTH`); a month VOIDed for any other reason still blocks. `app.final_settlement_unbilled_rent_months` already leaves out every month whose Rent Invoice a hand-back resolution VOIDed, whatever its coverage, so no code changed. Two tests on hand-back Leases: next month VOIDed by the resolution, with the termination dated in that month so the billing rule counts it as billable (the early final month), does not block; the hand-back month and next month VOIDed by a plain staff VOID instead still block.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 8f, Credit Refund to the Invoice Customer who is not the payer (BR-CREDIT-014, BR-AUTH-010, BR-REFUND-002, BR-REFUND-003, BR-REFUND-009, BR-REFUND-011, BR-REFUND-014, BR-PAY-042; owner answer to Q-2026-10-05-19; migration `20261030_95`):** a Customer Credit that a credit Adjustment released and whose Customer is not the payer of its source Payment (a third-party Payment since migration 67, or a wrong-Unit move to another Customer since slice 7) is refunded to that Customer. Staff with `refunds.create` record a `REFUND` draft of variant `CREDIT_CUSTOMER` (`app.record_credit_customer_refund_draft`, `RefundService.record_credit_customer_draft`) with a Refund method and a reference note; a `financial.approve` holder confirms it through the existing Refund draft confirmation, which calls the internal writer `app.post_credit_customer_refund` (no grant). The Refund source check lifts the payer rule only for such a Credit, paid to its own Customer, with a matching draft pending or confirmed by the same approver; a deferred check lets the Refund commit only with that draft CONFIRMED by the same approver under `financial.approve` and its details row. At confirmation every SQLSTATE 23514 is now reported as a conflict (the plan): the command rolled back and the draft stays pending, so "outcome unresolved; replay" was wrong for it. Choices, within the plan and docs/004: (1) only Credits whose Customer is not the payer use the new variant; a Credit whose Customer is the payer keeps the ordinary `USD` Credit Refund draft, which already pays that Customer, and the new variant refuses it (`P5206`, a conflict); (2) the ordinary `USD` Credit Refund draft of such a Credit still records (refusing it would refuse input accepted before) and is refused at confirmation as a conflict; (3) the Refund row records `authorization_reference` `refunds.create` with `processed_by` the approver, as every confirmed Refund draft (slice 3a-1; the slice 12 owner item on approver naming covers it); (4) a draft whose amount is no longer available and an inactive method are refused with `P5202` (invalid, 422 over HTTP), the same as the `USD` variant; slice 11c maps the stale Refund draft refusals to a conflict with the other Refund draft routes (owner answer on business-state refusals, plan slice 11c); (5) no new SQLSTATE for the confirmation's 23514 (the plan maps that code); (6) no route records the draft yet (slice 11c); the existing confirm route confirms it. Ledger, Refund corrections and the slice 7 one-call Credit reversal refusal are unchanged.
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 8f):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261030_95` (26 assertions in 14 modules); only the expected revision changed. Narrowed expectations, test names kept, because a check violation at Refund draft confirmation is now a conflict: `test_refund_service_migration.py::test_refund_service_concurrent_commands_replay_once_or_fail_closed` lets the losing worker also fail with `RefundConflictError` (still no Refund for it), and `test_credit_refund_api_migration.py::test_credit_refund_http_real_competing_refunds_cannot_double_consume_credit` also accepts 409 for the losing request (still exactly one 201 and one Refund). The new PostgreSQL module `test_credit_customer_refund_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 8f review round 1 (independent and security, 2026-10-06):** no blocker; security no should-fix; independent one should-fix (plan notes for slices 11c and 11e), taken. Fixes before round 2: the writer sets its own deferred check back to DEFERRED before its insert (as the 8d writer); a unique violation (23505) at Refund draft confirmation is also a conflict, because a Refund id or `REF-` number another writer took first (for example a Reservation Refund draft's client-chosen `outcome_id` or `refund_number` equal to the derived id) would otherwise be reported as "replay", which can never succeed (security N2; the preparer withdraws the draft and records it again with another key); a test of each clause of the two checks (a decision by another approver or not CONFIRMED, the same approver without the details row); stale wording (docs/004 DB-PAY-008, the slice 7 test comment, the plan's slice 9 bullets), the rule IDs of the migration docstring and this log's heading. Answered: the Final Settlement preview sees a pending `CREDIT_CUSTOMER` draft through its `source_kind` and `source_id` (both reviewers read it; a test is a slice 12 item); an HTTP confirmation test waits for slice 11c with the record route; the `P5206` re-check at confirmation cannot fail today (the Credit's Customer, the payer and the release rows never change) and stays as defense in depth; the draft lookup scans the Property's REFUND drafts (fine at V1 scale; slice 12); the Refund resource embeds the payer's source Payment (memo, channel reference) also when the recipient is not the payer, and the audit event takes the Lease from the source Payment (slices 11c and 12); the losing worker of `test_refund_service_migration.py::test_refund_service_concurrent_commands_replay_once_or_fail_closed` may also see P5202 if its confirmation starts after the winner committed, which was so before 8f (slice 12). Owner question, not blocking 8f (security N1, an earlier path): Q-2026-10-06-35.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 8f review round 2 (independent and security, 2026-10-06):** no blocker; security no should-fix; independent one should-fix, fixed (tests only): the round-1 test of the check clauses proved the deferred check's details clause only, so it now also writes the Refund while its draft waits with the details row and no decision, with another person's CONFIRMED decision (the test uses the preparer) and with a REJECTED decision (each refused at commit, 42501), asserts each refusal's message, and ends with the real confirmation after an earlier `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE` in its transaction (it succeeds because the writer keeps its own check deferred; without that reset it would be refused). `authorization_reference='financial.approve'` cannot be broken alone, because `financial_draft_decision_shape` forces it on every CONFIRMED decision. Answered: the confirmation sets `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE` (not only its own check), as `app.create_usd_refund` already does on every USD confirmation; the confirmation is a top-level command committed right after, so no later statement of the caller is affected. Notes taken: the review record's counts; the slice 12 item on derived-id clashes names 8f and says its 23505 mapping has only a mocked test.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 8f review round 3 (independent and security, 2026-10-06):** no blocker and no should-fix, so 8f is committed under the standing round-3 rule. Notes, all slice 12 test or hardening items in the plan: direct tests of each draft-match clause and of the release clause; refusal messages in the earlier direct-write test; the deferred check's 42501 would reach the caller as an authorization error (404), not a conflict, if a later change broke the confirmation's write order (unreachable today). The committed migration, code and tests are exactly what U4 and P3 ran.
- **Owner answers to the sub-slice 8f report (2026-10-06):** (1) keep the mapping of SQLSTATE 23505 to a conflict at Refund draft confirmation: a replay that can never succeed must not be offered; (2) Q-2026-10-06-35: yes, the tightening is approved: the one-call Credit reversal is refused for every Credit that 8f covers (an adjustment Credit released to a Customer who did not make the Payment), with tests, following BR-CREDIT-014 and the owner answers to slice 7; built as sub-slice 8g; (3) the 8g report says how staff can fix such a Credit when the credit Adjustment itself was a mistake, and a draft-based way goes to the slice 10 plan if none exists; (4) lint, format and type checks run one per tool call, never joined (ops/V1_055_RUN_RULES.md).
- **V1-056 sub-slice 8g, one-call reversal refused for a Credit released to another Customer (BR-CREDIT-014, BR-CREDIT-008, BR-AUTH-010; owner answer to Q-2026-10-06-35, approved tightening; migration `20261031_96`):** a new BEFORE INSERT trigger `customer_credit_reversals_other_customer` (function `app.validate_credit_customer_credit_reversal`, no grant) refuses the reversal of every Credit for which `app.credit_customer_refund_basis` is not NULL, for every writer, with the slice 7 SQLSTATE `P4904`; `CustomerCreditService` reports it as a conflict with one message for both refusals ("This Credit stays with its Customer; apply it to that Customer's obligations or refund it to that Customer"). Choices: (1) a new trigger rather than a change of the slice 7 function, so the slice 7 refusal and its message stay as they were; its name sorts after the slice 7 trigger and before the Credit reversal checks, so a Credit that both refuse gets the slice 7 message; (2) no lock: the basis reads rows that never change; (3) the same SQLSTATE as slice 7, as the owner's question proposed. Consequence, recorded in the plan's slice 12 item on the lasting block: a third-party Payment whose credit Adjustment released such a Credit can no longer be reversed (for example a returned transfer), because a Payment reversal needs every Credit of the Payment reversed and, after 8g, no path reverses such a Credit (a confirmed `CREDIT_CUSTOMER` Refund of it also keeps it from being reversed); the Payment stays blocked until a controlled path exists. Before 8g staff could reverse the Credit first. Fixing a mistaken credit Adjustment: no draft-based way exists. One way of ordinary commands without a draft works in general while the Credit is still available: a one-time Miscellaneous Charge to the same Customer for the amount, as BR-INV-044 says for a missed amount on a fully paid line, then the Credit applied to it (`credits.apply`); the amount is then billed as a Miscellaneous Charge, not in its first category. A second way without a draft works only in part: reverse the replacement allocation, record a debit Adjustment on the line, allocate the Payment to the line again and apply the Credit; it needs a replacement allocation (none exists when the Adjustment released the whole allocation, and then the debit Adjustment is refused on the fully paid line, P4509) and an allocation the one-call reversal accepts (a moved `WRONG_UNIT_MOVE` replacement is refused, P4904, and a new move draft is refused while the Payment has a Credit that is not reversed). Both ways are refused for a Lease charge once its Final Settlement is closed (8e-1). A reversal draft for a mistaken credit Adjustment is added to the slice 10 plan.
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 8g):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261031_96` (26 assertions in 14 modules); only the expected revision changed. The new PostgreSQL module `test_credit_customer_credit_reversal_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`. No earlier test reverses with success a Credit that 8g refuses; the slice 7 tests that try it with a moved Credit still get the slice 7 refusal (read-only search of every test that reverses a Credit).
- **Owner answers to Q-2026-10-06-36 (2026-10-06), post-settlement corrections (V1-056 slice 10):** (1) yes to the four correction kinds (extra charge, Refund, returned Payment, Adjustment reversal); a wrong charge that is still unpaid must have a clear fix, and if none of the four kinds covers it a fifth kind removes the wrong amount with a credit Adjustment, as a draft (design choice: built as the fifth kind `CHARGE_REDUCTION`, so that the `REFUND` kind always pays a Refund); every kind needs a reason; Staff prepare it and only a `financial.approve` holder confirms it. (2) The new DB-SETTLE-005 link columns and the Deposit application reversal table are approved; docs/004 is updated to match. (3) Approved tightening: after closure the one-call reversal of a Payment allocation or a Credit application that existed at closure is refused; fixes go through the corrections. (4) Approved tightening: after closure the wrong-Unit Payment move of such an allocation is refused, and the design note says how staff fix a real wrong-Unit case after closure; a forfeiture reversal after closure is allowed only inside a correction, which follows DL-032 (owner answer 3 to Q-2026-10-05-23 and Q-2026-10-05-24): the Deposit first pays this Lease's debt that is still open, through a Deposit application inside the correction, and only the rest is refunded. (5) The returned-Payment draft also works with no Settlement and ends the lasting Payment-reversal blocks of slice 7 and sub-slice 8g; tested with a returned third-party Payment whose adjustment Credit was released to another Customer. (6) No Late-payment Charge on a correction Invoice. The extra charge goes to the Customer of the obligation it corrects, or to the Primary Tenant when it corrects nothing on an Invoice; an existing one-time category; Due Date = issue date + Property due-days with the existing override. Agent check against the earlier rules before use, as the owner asked: a Signer's own debt stays on that Signer's ledger (owner answer to Q-2026-10-05-21, BR-SETTLE-016: the extra charge for an obligation on that Signer's Invoice goes to that Signer, as an Adjustment follows its obligation's Customer under BR-INV-045), other Lease debt goes to the Primary Tenant (BR-SETTLE-016 and the billing functions), a missed amount is billed as a one-time Miscellaneous or Damage Charge (BR-INV-044), and a one-time charge's Due Date is the issue date plus the Property's days with the authorized override (DL-069); no difference found. (7) `invoices.adjust` prepares the Adjustment reversal before closure and `final_settlements.prepare` after closure; only a `financial.approve` holder confirms it in both cases.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10a, post-settlement correction record (BR-SETTLE-012, BR-SETTLE-013, BR-AUTH-010; DB-SETTLE-003, DB-SETTLE-005; owner answers to Q-2026-10-06-36; migration `20261101_97`):** `app.settlement_corrections` (the header: closed Settlement, reason, approver, `financial.approve`, the `SETTLEMENT_CORRECTION` draft as `correlation_id`) and `app.settlement_correction_effects` (one typed link per row to each record a correction writes) are append-only and no application role may read or write them. The internal `app.begin_settlement_correction` checks the approver and `financial.approve`, takes the draft lock and the Settlement's draft lock, needs the Settlement closed (55000 otherwise, a conflict), records the CONFIRMED decision and then writes the header; `app.link_settlement_correction_effect` links a record before the writer writes it. A deferred check lets a header commit only with its draft confirmed by the same approver (42501) and at least one effect. The 8e-1 refusal and the 8e-2 refusal let a record through only when it is linked to a correction of the same Settlement confirmed in the same transaction (`app.settlement_correction_linked`), never through a session setting. No variant writer exists yet, so no correction can be written outside tests. Choices: (1) every link is unique: a record is the effect of at most one correction; (2) the context of each linked record is checked by the variant writer that writes it (10b to 10g), not by one table check; whether a wrong-Unit move or a returned Payment may link records of another Lease, as DB-SETTLE-005's context sentence does not allow today, is Q-2026-10-06-37 point 5; the closed Settlement refusals pass only records of the Settlement's own Lease; (3) "the same transaction" is exact: the draft's CONFIRMED decision stores the top-level transaction id (`pg_current_xact_id()`, the same inside an exception block) in its result, and the effect check, the link check and the deferred check compare it with the current one and also need the decision written after the current transaction started (review round 2: after a logical restore into a new cluster a stored transaction id can be handed out again) (review round 1, blocker B1: the first build compared the header time with the transaction start, which under READ COMMITTED let a transaction that started earlier treat another's committed correction as its own; a header column would also work but would change DB-SETTLE-003); (4) no time limit is tested by checking that the header's only time checks are "not before the closure" and "not later than the clock plus 5 seconds" (every writer stamps the database clock, so "years after closure" cannot be run); (5) the shared step does not replay: each variant writer replays an exact repeat before calling it (the draft is then already decided, 55000); (6) a variant writer sets no constraint IMMEDIATE before every linked record is written, so it writes its own records instead of calling functions that set `ALL IMMEDIATE` (for example `app.reverse_credit_application`).
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10a):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261101_97` (26 assertions in 14 modules); only the expected revision changed. The new PostgreSQL module `test_settlement_correction_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10a reviews (independent and security, 2026-10-06):** round 1: one blocker (the first "same transaction" rule compared the header time with the transaction start, which under READ COMMITTED let a transaction that started earlier treat another's committed correction as its own; fixed with the transaction id stored in the CONFIRMED decision) and four should-fix (header and committed-link tests, the stale check and replay in the writer notes, the rule that a writer writes every linked record in its final state before it returns), all fixed; round 2: two should-fix (a two-connection test of the blocker fix; the decision's time bound against a transaction id handed out again after a logical restore), both fixed; round 3: no should-fix, so 10a is committed under the standing round-3 rule. Notes for 10b (a test of the `decided_at` bound; a database check that refuses a commit while a linked Invoice is still DRAFT) and 11e (leave `transaction_id` out of draft responses) are in the plan; a server clock stepped backwards fails a correction closed (runbook, slice 12).
- **Owner answers to Q-2026-10-06-37 (2026-10-07), slice 10 follow-up points:** (1) yes: `FORFEITURE_REVERSAL` and `PAYMENT_MOVE` are correction kinds (variants of the `SETTLEMENT_CORRECTION` draft kind, prepared under `final_settlements.prepare` with a reason and confirmed only by a `financial.approve` holder; sub-slices 10d and 10g). (2) yes: a new append-only table `payment_returns` records a returned Payment that has no closed Settlement (one row per returned Payment: the Payment, the draft, the reason, the approver, `financial.approve`, and the Settlement correction when there is one); docs/004 gains it (DB-PAY-009); built in sub-slice 10e. (3) yes: a `payments.reverse` holder prepares it as a new draft kind `PAYMENT_RETURN`, and only a `financial.approve` holder confirms it (with a closed Settlement it is the `RETURNED_PAYMENT` variant under `final_settlements.prepare`). (4) yes: DB-SETTLE-005 gains the link `authorized_nonrecurring_obligation_id`, the one-time charge record of an extra charge (sub-slice 10b). (5) yes, exactly two exceptions to the rule that every record linked to a correction belongs to the Settlement's Lease: the wrong-Unit move may pay another Lease's Invoice, and a returned Payment may reverse its records on other Leases. One condition: neither may change a record that another Lease's closed Settlement relied on, unless that change is also a confirmed correction of that Settlement; this is tested. If it is hard to build, the case is written in ops/V1_056_QUESTIONS.md with a recommendation and the work stops (sub-slices 10e and 10g). docs/004 DB-SETTLE-005 and DB-PAY-009 are updated to match.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10b, post-settlement extra charge (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-012, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-INV-044, BR-AUTH-010; DB-SETTLE-003, DB-SETTLE-005; owner answer 6 to Q-2026-10-06-36 and owner answer 4 to Q-2026-10-06-37; migration `20261102_98`):** a missed amount after closure is billed by an `EXTRA_CHARGE` correction. Staff record it under `final_settlements.prepare` with a reason (`app.record_settlement_extra_charge_draft`, `SettlementCorrectionService.record_extra_charge`); it has no effect until a `financial.approve` holder confirms it (`app.confirm_settlement_extra_charge`), which in one transaction records the decision and the correction header (10a), then writes, each linked before it is written, a Miscellaneous or Damage one-time charge record and its Invoice with the Lease and one line, issued in the same call with the one-time charge Due Date (the issue date plus the Property's days, or the override with its reason). The Customer is the Customer of the corrected Invoice when the draft names one, otherwise the Primary Tenant; a Damage charge always goes to the Primary Tenant; no Late-payment Charge follows (the 8e-1 refusal of Late-payment Decisions stays). DB-SETTLE-005 gains the link `authorized_nonrecurring_obligation_id`, the 8e-1 refusal lets such a linked record through, and a deferred check refuses the commit while an Invoice linked to a correction is not issued (10a security review round 2 note). A Damage extra charge follows the Damage rules of BR-MOVEOUT-006 and DB-INV-005 (one current, unbilled finding; the Primary Tenant), but it is prepared under `final_settlements.prepare` and authorized by the `financial.approve` holder who confirms it, not under `damage_charges.bill`: the approvers of every correction are the Settlement's (owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-06-36, design note point 3; review round 3 note). Choices: (1) the reason is at most 500 characters, because the one-time charge record keeps it; (2) the corrected obligation is an issued, non-VOID Invoice of the Settlement's Lease (the preview's definition of a Settlement obligation), not a closure snapshot row; (3) the one-time charge record is authorized by the approver under `financial.approve`, the capability that authorized it; (4) the writer writes its own records, because `app.create_invoice_draft` and `app.correct_invoice_draft` set `ALL IMMEDIATE` while the Invoice is still DRAFT (review round 1 corrected this wording), and runs `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE` itself once every linked record is written (full run P1: the Invoice validators are not definer functions and read tables the financial role cannot read, so they failed at COMMIT); (5) a Due Date override before the issue date is a conflict (55000) at record and at confirm, and the draft stays pending; (6) a draft whose sources changed (the closure, the Primary Tenant, the corrected Invoice, the finding) is stale (P5S02) and stays pending; two confirms on one Lease are serialized by the locks, so the second sees the billed finding as a changed source, and a unique violation stays a conflict (review round 1); (7) the service is a new module `settlement_correction.py` that reuses the Final Settlement error mapping; the routes stay in slice 11e, so docs/005 is unchanged; (8) after review round 1: the records get fresh ids, because a client chooses one-time charge, Invoice and audit ids elsewhere and could take an id derived from the draft first, and an exact repeat finds them through the correction's links; the commit check is positive (it refuses an Invoice that is not issued, also when a caller makes it run before the Invoice is written); the confirm runs only at READ COMMITTED, locks the Lease row (FOR SHARE) after the Settlement draft lock so no superseding inspection commits in between, stamps its records after the approval, and never returns the decision's transaction id. The commit check refuses nothing that any role could do before: the link functions have no grant and no correction existed before this migration, so it is not a tightening of reachable behavior (review round 1 note); it is the check the 10a design note (approved for 10b) asks of the first writer that links an Invoice.
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10b):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261102_98` (26 assertions in 14 modules); only the expected revision changed. `test_an_invoice_draft_moves_onto_the_closed_lease_only_inside_its_own_correction` (sub-slice 10a) keeps its name and its purpose (the UPDATE path of the 8e-1 refusal passes only a draft linked to a correction confirmed in the same transaction). Under the new commit check a correction never keeps a linked DRAFT Invoice, so two expectations changed, each with a comment: the committed-link half now expects the commit refusal (23514) before the later unlinked change is refused as before (P5S01); the same-transaction half now expects the refusal of the new check (23514, which `app.correct_invoice_draft` runs at once after the guarded UPDATE passed) instead of a moved DRAFT, and the draft is unchanged. Its traceability note (sub-slice 10a, left as written because the manifest is append-only) still says such a draft "can" be corrected onto the closed Lease; the test docstring now says that it passes the 8e-1 refusal and is then refused by the commit check (review round 2). The new PostgreSQL module `test_settlement_extra_charge_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`.
- **Owner answers to the sub-slice 10b report (2026-10-07):** (1) accepted: both changed expectations in the 10a test follow from the commit check the design note asked for. (2) Accepted: the commit check refuses nothing anyone could do before, so it needed no stop; such checks keep being named in the slice report; a new check that could refuse something staff can do today needs the owner's answer first. (3) Accepted: a Damage extra charge after closure is approved under `financial.approve`, like every correction; BR-MOVEOUT-006 (docs/002) gains an "Amended by" line that says so.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10c-1a, post-settlement charge reduction (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-012, BR-INV-045, BR-AUTH-010; DB-SETTLE-003, DB-SETTLE-005; owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-06-36; migration `20261103_99`):** a wrong charge that is still unpaid after closure has a clear fix: a `CHARGE_REDUCTION` correction. Staff record it under `final_settlements.prepare` with a reason (`app.record_settlement_charge_reduction_draft`, `SettlementCorrectionService.record_charge_reduction`); only a `financial.approve` holder confirms it (`app.confirm_settlement_charge_reduction`), which in one transaction records the decision and the correction header (10a) and writes, linked before it is written, a credit Adjustment of the wrong Invoice line on that Invoice's Customer (BR-INV-045). Sub-slice 10c is split (no owner answer needed): 10c-1a this reduction, 10c-1b `REFUND` of lines paid by Payments or Credits, 10c-2 lines paid by a Deposit application; a wrong Late-payment Charge after closure is Q-2026-10-07-38. Choices: (1) the reduced obligation is an issued, non-VOID Invoice of the Settlement's Lease (as 10b choice 2) and one of its lines; (2) the amount is at most the line's unpaid rest: the recorder refuses a reduction that would release paid value (55000), because the design note makes that a `REFUND`; at the confirm any change of the line since the draft (for example a Payment that would make the reduction release paid value) makes the draft stale (P5S02), so the limit holds under the locks (review round 1 wording); (3) an Invoice that a Deposit application paid stays refused (the 8a-1 refusal, 55000), also for an unpaid line of it, until 10c-2; (4) the Adjustment is authorized by the approver under `financial.approve` (as 10b choice 3) with `source_reference` `settlement-correction:<correction id>`, and the reason is at most 500 characters (the limit of `app.adjust_invoice`); (5) the writer writes the Adjustment itself and refreshes the Invoice status, version and time as `app.adjust_invoice` does, because that command records `invoices.adjust`, its own replay row and correlation; (6) a draft whose line changed since it was recorded (its obligation or paid value, the Invoice's issue state or Deposit applications, the closure) is stale (P5S02) and stays pending; (7) lock order: the draft lock, the Settlement's draft lock, the Invoice `FOR UPDATE`, then the Settlement row in `app.begin_settlement_correction` (the 8e-1 refusal's order: Invoice, then Settlement row). The migration only adds functions: no table, trigger or earlier function changes and no new check on anything staff can do today (the 8e-1 refusal already lets a linked Adjustment through, 10a).
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-1a):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261103_99` (26 assertions in 14 modules); only the expected revision changed. The new PostgreSQL module `test_settlement_charge_reduction_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`.
- **Owner answers to Q-2026-10-07-38 and Q-2026-10-07-39 (2026-10-07), post-settlement Refunds and Late-payment Charges:** Q-38: yes; a new sub-slice 10c-3 fixes a wrong Late-payment Charge after closure with a Late-payment Charge correction linked to the Settlement, prepared under `final_settlements.prepare` and confirmed only by a `financial.approve` holder; for a paid Charge it works like a credit Adjustment of an Invoice line: the released value follows BR-CREDIT-014, so Credit released to a Customer who did not make the Payment is refunded to that Customer (docs/002 BR-SETTLE-013, BR-LATE-005 and BR-CREDIT-014 gain "Amended by" lines). Q-39 (1): yes; a new append-only table `settlement_correction_refund_details`, like 8d's `final_settlement_refund_details`, keeps each Refund a correction pays, so one correction can pay several Refunds, each with its own method, reference note and recipient, and Staff never split a correction by hand; docs/004 gains the table. Q-39 (2): approved tightening: the one-call reversal of the leftover ordinary Credit application, written when a `REFUND` correction releases only part of a Settlement Credit application, is refused; it is still money the closed Settlement relied on (owner answers to Q-2026-10-06-32 and Q-2026-10-06-36). Also accepted for now: until sub-slice 10h, a one-call allocation reversal followed by a `CHARGE_REDUCTION` can remove a charge that was paid at closure without a `REFUND` record (10c-1a security review round 2), because no money can leave without an approved Refund draft and nothing is live; 10h must close it with a test.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10c-1b, post-settlement Refund of a paid wrong charge (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-012, BR-CREDIT-014, BR-CREDIT-008, BR-REFUND-014, BR-INV-045, BR-AUTH-010; DB-SETTLE-003, DB-SETTLE-005, DB-REFUND-001; owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-06-36 and the owner answers to Q-2026-10-07-39; migration `20261104_100`):** a wrong charge that was paid at closure is fixed by a `REFUND` correction. Staff record it under `final_settlements.prepare` with a reason, one Invoice line, the amount, a Refund method and a reference note (`app.record_settlement_refund_draft`, `SettlementCorrectionService.record_refund`); only a `financial.approve` holder confirms it (`app.confirm_settlement_refund`), which in one transaction records the decision and the correction header (10a) and writes, each linked before it is written, the release of the paid part as a credit Adjustment releases it (migration 67, newest first), the credit Adjustment, and one Credit Refund per released source to that Credit's Customer, each with its own `settlement_correction_refund_details` row. Choices: (1) the draft holds one method and one note (Q-39 option (a)); every Refund of the correction keeps them in its own details row, and the Refund row carries the recipient; every released value belongs to the Invoice Customer (BR-CREDIT-014 for a released allocation, BR-CREDIT-008 for a Credit application's Credit), so all Refunds of one correction go to that Customer; (2) the amount is at most the line's charge and must release paid value: removing only unpaid value is a `CHARGE_REDUCTION` (55000), and an unpaid part of the amount is removed with the release (design note); (3) one Refund per released source, not per Credit, so each Refund matches one release record; (4) the draft stores the sources it will release and the source revision covers the line and every source that pays it; any change is stale (P5S02) and the draft stays pending; an unknown or inactive Refund method is a conflict (55000) at record and at confirm, as the Final Settlement draft; (5) the writer writes the release itself instead of calling the migration 67 functions, because those give fresh ids inside and a Settlement Credit application's reversal passes the 8e-2 refusal only when linked first; the release records keep the migration 67 reason and authorization references (`payments.allocations.reverse`, `credits.applications.reverse`, `payments.allocate`, `credits.apply`, `credits.recognize`), which those tables' checks fix; the Adjustment is authorized under `financial.approve` (10c-1a choice 4); (6) the Refund row records `refunds.create` with `processed_by` the approver, as every confirmed Refund draft, and a time after its Credit's latest event; (7) lock order: the draft lock, the Settlement's draft lock, each source Payment and then each source Credit in id order (the order of the allocation, Credit and Refund writers), the Invoice, then the Settlement row; (8) the released Credits have no DB-SETTLE-005 link: the linked Adjustment's `invoice_credit_excess_releases` rows name them; (9) the Refund correction paths (a verified return and its reversal) stay open for these Credit Refunds as for any Credit Refund: a returned Refund puts the value back on the Credit, not on the Settlement's obligations; (10) the approved tightening of Q-39 answer 2 is a new trigger (`a0_customer_credit_application_reversals_settlement_remainder`), not a change of the 8e-2 function, with the 8e-2 SQLSTATE and message; it walks back through every earlier re-post. Relaxing patches: the general Refund source check and the deferred `refunds_credit_customer_confirmed` check also accept the correction's own Credit Refund to a Customer other than the payer (`app.settlement_correction_refund_allowed`). New check named for the owner (10b answer 2): only the approved remainder refusal; it refuses nothing staff can do today, because a re-posted remainder of a Settlement Credit application exists only through a correction. An Invoice that a Deposit application paid stays refused until 10c-2.
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-1b):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261104_100` (26 assertions in 14 modules); only the expected revision changed. The new PostgreSQL module `test_settlement_refund_correction_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`.
- **Owner answers to Q-2026-10-07-40 and the "For the owner" points of the sub-slice 10c-1b review record (2026-10-07):** Q-40: option (a), as an owner exception like Q-2026-10-05-31: one test that a second 1-cent Refund from the same Credit is refused once the correction's own Refunds use up what it released to that Credit (the cumulative term of `app.settlement_correction_refund_allowed`), the backend unit suite and the full PostgreSQL suite once each, then one review round limited to the new test and the review record; 10c-1b is committed only if that round has no blocker and no should-fix. The other points: (1) accepted: a correction draft holds one Refund method and one reference note, stored in each Refund's own details row, and every Refund of the correction goes to the Invoice Customer; (2) accepted: the released Customer Credits have no correction link; the linked Adjustment's release records and the correction's audit event name them; (3) accepted: a deadlock between a correction confirm and a one-call Adjustment or VOID of the same Invoice ends as a conflict and the draft stays pending; (4) yes, correction Refunds follow the 8d rule: approved tightening: a Credit Refund paid by a post-settlement correction is corrected only through another post-settlement correction, not by the one-call verified return (`app.verify_usd_refund_return`), as 8d made a Settlement Deposit Refund correctable only through a correction, because a one-call return would otherwise create Credit that pays debt without an approver; not built in 10c-1b (choice 9 of the 10c-1b entry describes 10c-1b as built); it is built with a test in sub-slice 10c-2 or, at the latest, 10h (plan); (5) yes, both: a re-posted leftover of anything that existed at closure (a Payment allocation or a Credit application) counts as existing at closure; 10c-1b already refuses the one-call reversal of a Settlement Credit application's re-posted leftover, and 10h applies the rule to every closure-era allocation and Credit application.
- **Owner answers to Q-2026-10-07-41 (2026-10-07), a Refund of a Deposit-paid charge after closure (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2):** (1) option (a): Deposit value that a correction releases first pays this Lease's open debt through Deposit applications linked to the correction, and only the rest is refunded; it follows every Settlement rule already given: Customer Credit before Deposits (Q-2026-10-05-29), the Security Deposit before the remaining Pet Deposit (Q-2026-10-05-30), the Pet Deposit choice rules (Q-2026-10-05-20, Q-2026-10-05-25) and the kept-Deposit cases (Q-2026-10-05-24); the step is built once and reused by 10d (`FORFEITURE_REVERSAL`); if these rules conflict, the work stops and asks the owner. (2) Yes: the release takes the newest records first across Deposit applications, Payment allocations and Credit applications, as migration 67 does. (3) Yes: `settlement_correction_refund_details` gains two nullable columns (`deposit_id`, `signer_agreement`), docs/004 is updated, and the 8d Deposit Refund source check is relaxed only for a confirmed correction's own Refund, with a test that it accepts nothing else. (4) Yes, with one change: the new Refund in a returned-Refund correction is optional; if the Customer cannot be reached yet, the correction records the verified return only, and the returned amount stays as that Customer's Credit until a later correction refunds it; both the one-call return and its one-call reversal stay refused for a correction's Refund; if this does not fit the design, the work stops and asks the owner.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-1, Deposit application reversals (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-012, BR-SETTLE-009, BR-LEDGER-003, BR-AUTH-010; DB-DEP-006, DB-SETTLE-005, DB-DER-004, DB-DER-010; owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-06-36; migration `20261105_101`):** the append-only table `app.deposit_application_reversals` (DB-DEP-006) records the reversal of a Final Settlement Deposit application, written only inside a post-settlement correction of the application's Settlement confirmed in the same transaction, by that correction's approver under `financial.approve` (BEFORE INSERT check, 23514), with the Deposit and target locks and the chronology of the Deposit application (later than the latest event of the target and of the Deposit, not before the closure or the correction; review round 1); no role may read or write it, and no writer exists yet (10c-2a-3 and 10e write it). DB-SETTLE-005 gains the link `deposit_application_reversal_id`. Every reader counts only applications not reversed: the Deposit held balance and snapshot, the Invoice line value paid by Deposits as of a time, the Late-payment Charge application summary and the 8a-1 refusal of changes to a Deposit-paid obligation; the latest event time of an allocation target counts the reversal. The Customer Ledger shows `DEPOSIT_APPLICATION_REVERSED` (order 76, the two `DEPOSIT_APPLIED` components with the opposite effect) and the Deposit history shows `APPLICATION_REVERSAL_INVOICE_LINE` or `APPLICATION_REVERSAL_LATE_PAYMENT_CHARGE`. Choices: (1) one reversal per application, as DB-DEP-006 has no amount; a partial release reverses the whole application and re-posts the rest, as migration 67 does (10c-2a-2 or 10c-2a-3); (2) the reason keeps the application's limit (1 to 1,000 characters); (3) the 8a-1 refusal is relaxed only for an obligation whose Deposit applications are all reversed; after closure the 8e-1 refusal still stops every change not linked to a correction; (4) `app.deposit_has_active_application` already read the reversal table once it exists, so it is not changed; (5) changed in review round 1: the reversed line's Invoice status and version are refreshed at commit by the table's own deferred trigger, as the Deposit application's own trigger does, instead of by each writer. New check named for the owner (10b answer 2): only the BEFORE INSERT check of the new table, which refuses nothing anyone could do before. Review round 1 (independent and security): no blocker; the should-fix findings (the reversal's locks and chronology, an untested Late-payment Charge branch of the relaxed 8a-1 refusal, the list of later writers) are fixed, with tests. Round 2: one should-fix (the target-bound test was masked by the correction bound), fixed. Round 3 (independent and security): no blocker and no should-fix, so 10c-2a-1 is committed under the standing round-3 rule.
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-1):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261105_101` (26 assertions in 14 modules); only the expected revision changed. Narrowed expectations, test names kept, because the ledger has one more event kind: `test_customer_ledger.py` expects 25 event kinds (was 24) and TEST-FE-564 in `CustomerAccountLedger.test.tsx` (it lists every ledger event label) expects 26 rows (25 events and the header; was 25). The new PostgreSQL module `test_deposit_application_reversal_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`.
- **Owner answers to Q-2026-10-07-42 (2026-10-07), Customer Credit before Deposits after closure and a returned Deposit Refund (V1-056 sub-slices 10c-2a-2, 10c-2a-3 and 10c-2b):** (1) yes, option (a): before released Deposit value pays open debt, the correction first applies each Customer's own Credit to that Customer's own open debt on this Lease, as the Settlement draft does (owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-05-29); Credit never pays another Customer's debt; the correction writes these Credit applications itself, linked to the correction and recorded as Credit applications of that Settlement (so their one-call reversal is refused, as sub-slice 8e-2), following BR-CREDIT-006, and reuses the 8e-2 Credit application writer with a correction link if it can. (2) Yes, option (a), an approved change of the committed sub-slice 10c-1b: every `REFUND` correction first uses the Credit it releases against that Credit's own Customer's open debt on this Lease and refunds only the rest; a Customer with no open debt on this Lease (for example a third-party payer) gets the Refund as today; 10c-1b is changed in sub-slice 10c-2a-3, with tests for both cases. (3) Yes, option (a): a returned Deposit Refund goes back to the Deposit, not to Customer Credit; the returned amount stays held by the Deposit until a later correction, which first pays this Lease's open debt in the DL-032 order and then refunds the rest; the owner answer 4 to Q-2026-10-07-41 (the returned amount stays as that Customer's Credit) applies to a returned Credit Refund only.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-2, the shared DL-032 step after closure (DL-032, BR-SETTLE-004, BR-SETTLE-005, BR-SETTLE-006, BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-017, BR-CREDIT-006, BR-CREDIT-008, BR-CREDIT-014, BR-DEP-027, BR-REFUND-014, BR-REFUND-015, BR-AUTH-010; owner answers 1 and 3 to Q-2026-10-07-41 and 1 and 2 to Q-2026-10-07-42; migration `20261106_102`):** value a post-settlement correction releases, or a Deposit holds again after closure, is used under the Settlement rules before anything is refunded. A correction draft that runs the step keeps `plan` (the Settlement plan format), `plan_overlay` (what the correction itself changes before the step) and `plan_result`; the internal check `app.settlement_correction_plan_result` reads the closed Settlement as the Settlement preview does with the overlay applied, and the internal `app.post_settlement_correction_plan` runs inside the correction after the variant's release: the check again on the real state (a different result is stale, P5S02), then the Customer Credit applications (the 8e-2 writer with a correction link), the Deposit applications and the Deposit Refunds, each linked to the correction before it is written with a fresh id. The 8e-2 Credit application checks, the Deposit application check and the 8d Deposit Refund source check accept a correction's own record only (relaxing); `settlement_correction_refund_details` gains `deposit_id` and `signer_agreement`. No variant uses the step yet (10c-2a-3, 10c-2b and 10d will), so no role can reach it. Choices: (1) the plan has the Settlement plan's format and checks (copied, not shared: the 8c check needs a ready preview and an open Settlement, and is not changed); (2) the overlay lets a variant record the draft before its release; a Deposit in the overlay counts as following DL-032 because the variant's release guarantees it, and the confirm checks the real state; (3) of the preview's blocking issues only a waiting draft on a Settlement source refuses the step (the 8r rule and the Settlement's pending-draft rule); the other issues describe the closure, which the 8e-1 refusals keep unchanged; (4) the step covers every DL-032 Deposit that holds money, not only the one the correction releases (BR-SETTLE-008: every remaining Deposit balance is applied or refunded; owner answer 3 to Q-2026-10-07-42: a later correction pays the debt and refunds the rest of a returned amount); (5) Customer Credit before Deposits (Q-42 answer 1, "before released Deposit value pays open debt") applies only when a Deposit holds money; this differs from the Settlement draft, where each Customer's Credit must pay that Customer's open debt even when no Deposit pays (owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-05-29), because an unconditional rule would force a Credit-only correction to apply the Credit of Customers it does not concern, beyond Q-42 answer 2 (named for the owner after review round 1); released Credit pays its own Customer's open debt on the Lease before any Refund (Q-42 answer 2) applies to each Credit the overlay names, whether or not a Deposit holds money, and it counts all of that Credit's available value, also value it held before the correction (named for the owner after review round 1); (6) one Refund method, note, recipient and agreement per correction plan (owner answer 3 to Q-2026-10-07-41, as the method and note of 10c-1b); (7) the 8e-2 writer is reused (owner answer 1 to Q-42): it reads a correction's step from its `plan`, resolves the Credit key through `app.settlement_correction_credit_id` (a key may name a Payment allocation the correction releases, whose value becomes the Invoice Customer's Credit, BR-CREDIT-014) and gives the record a fresh id linked to the correction (sub-slice 10b rule); its Settlement path is unchanged; (8) a correction's Deposit application takes the Settlement's next order number, and its stage order is checked within the correction; (9) a correction's Deposit Refund records `financial.approve` as a Settlement Deposit Refund does; (10) the step defers its own link keys again, because an earlier writer in the same transaction may have set every constraint IMMEDIATE (full run P1); (11) the receipt reversal recorder is not changed: a waiting correction draft is protected by the pending-draft rule at the step and by the stale check, so no tightening is needed; (12, review round 1) a correction's Deposit application must match a Deposit step of the correction's plan (Deposit, stage, target and amount), each step at most once, as its Credit applications and Deposit Refunds do; (13, review round 1) `settlement_correction_refund_details` is unique on the correction and the Deposit (each Deposit refunded at most once per correction, as 8d once per Settlement), and its deferred check also needs the details to name the Refund's own Deposit, and no Deposit for a Credit Refund; (14, review round 1) the step itself checks READ COMMITTED and takes its locks again (held already when the variant took them), and the lock order is the Settlement confirm's: the draft and Settlement draft locks, the Lease row `FOR UPDATE` (added after review round 2, owner answer to Q-2026-10-07-43: the move-out inspection command that supersedes an inspection locks the same row `FOR UPDATE`, so no line stops being pet damage while the step runs), the steps' Payments and Credits, the Lease's Charges and Invoices, the Settlement row, then the Deposits; (15, review round 1) the step compares the whole recorded result; (16, review round 1, accepted race) the Credit-before-Deposits rule reads every available Credit of the Lease's Customers but locks only the Credits the steps name (as the Settlement confirm): a Credit recognized by a concurrent one-call command after the step's check is not used first; nothing is overpaid. New checks named for the owner (10b answer 2): none refuses anything anyone could do before; the relaxed checks still refuse every record that is not a correction's own.
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-2):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261106_102` (26 assertions in 14 modules); only the expected revision changed. The new PostgreSQL module `test_settlement_correction_plan_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`.
- **Owner answers to Q-2026-10-07-43 (2026-10-07), sub-slice 10c-2a-2 after review round 2:** option (a), as an owner exception like Q-2026-10-05-31 and Q-2026-10-07-40: (1) the step must hold a lock that really stops the move-out inspection from being superseded while it runs; the command that supersedes an inspection (`app.move_out_inspection_record`, and its insert trigger) locks the Lease row `FOR UPDATE` and the step's lock function did not, so the step now locks the Lease row `FOR UPDATE` right after the two draft locks, as the Settlement confirm (the lock order of the 8e-3 plan note), with a test that an inspection correction waits while the step runs and that the Pet Deposit cannot pay a line that is no longer pet damage; (2) tests of the step's two guards inside a live correction (a person other than the approver; the READ COMMITTED refusal); (3) tests of the BR-DEP-027 branches (the staff reason refused at an unpaid-Initial termination; a Pet Deposit Refund while other debt stays open, accepted by the plan check and the Deposit Refund source check) and the BR-CREDIT-006 branches (the Rent-first refusal; the proposal and designation bases the correction's Credit check requires); then the backend unit suite and the full PostgreSQL suite once each, one more independent and security round on the three fixes and the code they touch, and the commit only if that round has no blocker and no should-fix. The owner's answers to the three named choices: choice 5, first part, confirmed: Customer Credit before Deposits after closure applies only when a Deposit holds money, because the rule protects Deposit money and a correction must not apply the Credit of Customers it does not concern; choice 5, second part, confirmed: "released Credit first" counts the whole Credit of that Customer, including value it held before the correction, so a Customer is never refunded while that Customer owes money on this Lease that the Credit could pay (Q-2026-10-05-29); choice 16 accepted as a known limit (below).
- **Known limit (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-2, owner answer to Q-2026-10-07-43):** the Credit-before-Deposits rule of the post-settlement correction step reads every available Credit of the Lease's Customers but locks only the Credits its steps name, as the Settlement confirm does. A Credit recognized by another one-call command right after the step's check is not used first. Nothing is overpaid, and that Credit stays with the Customer.
- **Owner answers to Q-2026-10-07-44 (2026-10-07), sub-slice 10c-2a-2 after review round 3:** (1) option (a): the two text findings are fixed (the comment before `app.lock_settlement_correction_plan` and the plan note now say that 10c-1b as committed takes no Lease lock and that every later correction type takes the Lease row `FOR UPDATE` in the 8e-3 order; the inspection test's docstring and traceability entry no longer cite BR-DEP-027); a read-only search found no PostgreSQL test that reads the changed comment, docstring or traceability entry (the comment is outside every function body), so no PostgreSQL run; static checks and the backend unit suite once, then the commit. (2) Approved tightening: once a Lease's Final Settlement is closed, a supersede of its move-out inspection is refused; a wrong pet-damage line after closure is fixed only with a post-settlement correction; the plan names the correction kind staff use, and if no kind fits the question goes to the owner with a recommendation; built in a small sub-slice after 10c-2a-3, with tests. (3) A correction that uses the rest of the Pet Deposit for other debt at a BR-DEP-027 termination needs a new Customer choice recorded for that correction; it never reuses the choice given for the Settlement; without a new choice the rest of the Pet Deposit is not used for other debt (one agreement per correction, as the owner answer 3 to Q-2026-10-07-41); the variant recorders (10c-2a-3, 10d) ask for it. (4) When 10c-1b uses the shared step in 10c-2a-3, it takes the same Lease lock in the 8e-3 order, with a test, and the comment and the plan note are updated.
- **Owner answer to Q-2026-10-07-45 (2026-10-07), a wrong pet classification of a Damage line after closure:** option (a): a new post-settlement correction kind for a Damage line whose pet classification is wrong after closure; the charge amount stays the same and the closed inspection record is not changed; the correction records the corrected classification and the reason; it covers both directions (a line wrongly marked as pet damage, and a line that should have been pet damage); it re-runs the shared DL-032 step after closure with all Settlement rules (Q-2026-10-05-24, Q-2026-10-05-29, Q-2026-10-05-30) and owner answer 3 to Q-2026-10-07-44 (a new Customer choice recorded for this correction); it is prepared under `final_settlements.prepare` and confirmed only by a `financial.approve` holder, as every correction; built after sub-slice 10c-2a-4 and before 10h, with tests for both directions. The owner also said to continue with 10c-2a-3 while this kind waits for its sub-slice.
- **Owner answer to Q-2026-10-07-46 (2026-10-07), the 8a-1 refusal and the newest-first release (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-3):** option (a), a relaxing change: the 8a-1 refusal of an Invoice Adjustment while a Deposit application on that Invoice is active lets through only an Adjustment linked to a post-settlement correction of that Invoice's own Settlement, confirmed in the same transaction by that correction's approver; every unlinked Adjustment stays refused, as in 8a-1; the release stays newest first (owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-07-41); after such an Adjustment no line may be paid more than its new amount; Deposit value is freed only by the correction's own Deposit application reversal, following the plan, and never turns into Customer Credit by itself; if the database does not already enforce this on the new path, a check is added; tests for an older Deposit application on the same line that the release does not reach, a Deposit application on another line of the same Invoice, and an unlinked Adjustment on such an Invoice still refused.
- **Owner answer to Q-2026-10-07-47 (2026-10-07), the 10c-1b tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-3):** option (a), an owner exception to the narrowing rule for six tests only: the six tests of `test_settlement_refund_correction_migration.py` that confirm Refund corrections on a Settlement closed with the test-only `close()` helper are rebuilt on a real closure (the 8e-3 confirm, as the 10c-2a-2 tests do); each keeps its name and proves the same 10c-1b behaviour; no assertion about that behaviour is removed or loosened; only the setup, amounts and source lists change; each changed test is listed in docs/010 with what changed and why; the independent reviewer checks each rebuilt test against its old version; new correction tests never use the test-only close helper; the slice 12 list gains a check of every other test that uses it and leaves a state a real closure cannot leave.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-3, Refund of a Deposit-paid charge after closure (DL-032, BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-013, BR-CREDIT-006, BR-CREDIT-008, BR-CREDIT-014, BR-DEP-027, BR-REFUND-014, BR-REFUND-015, BR-AUTH-010; owner answers 1 and 2 to Q-2026-10-07-41, 1 and 2 to Q-2026-10-07-42, 3 and 4 to Q-2026-10-07-44, and the answers to Q-2026-10-07-46 and Q-2026-10-07-47; migration `20261107_103`):** the `REFUND` correction of sub-slice 10c-1b is extended in place (same signatures) and now also corrects a line a Deposit application paid. The release takes whole sources newest first across Deposit applications, Payment allocations and Credit applications, only until the paid part is covered (Q-41 answer 2); a released Deposit application is reversed whole (sub-slice 10c-2a-1) and its value goes back to its Deposit, never to Customer Credit; the rest of a partly released Deposit application is paid again only as a step of the correction's plan; the re-posted rests of Payment allocations and Credit applications keep every other line's amounts, so no other already-paid line changes how it is paid. Every `REFUND` correction then runs the shared DL-032 step of sub-slice 10c-2a-2: the draft request carries `plan` (staff choose every step; every Deposit Refund step uses the draft's method and note), the recorder builds the overlay of its own release and stores the check's result, and the confirm writes the release, runs the step (Credit first, Deposit applications, Deposit Refunds) and only then writes one Credit Refund per released Payment allocation or Credit application for the value left in that Credit, at most what the correction released to it (Q-42 answer 2: a Customer with open debt on the Lease gets back only the rest; a Customer with none gets the Refund as before; a correction may end with no Refund). The confirm takes the Lease row `FOR UPDATE` right after the two draft locks, before the source Payments, in the 8e-3 order (Q-44 answer 4). A remaining Pet Deposit step with the Customer's choice needs a choice text other than the one recorded at the Settlement (Q-44 answer 3); at the BR-DEP-027 terminations the step still refuses the staff basis. Relaxing (Q-46): the 8a-1 refusal of an Invoice Adjustment lets through only an Adjustment linked to a correction of its Invoice's own closed Settlement, confirmed in the same transaction by that correction's approver under `financial.approve` (internal `app.settlement_correction_adjustment_allowed`); every unlinked Adjustment stays refused. No new check was needed for "no line paid more than its new amount": `app.validate_invoice_adjustment_effect` already refuses at commit a line whose outstanding value at the Adjustment's time, Deposit applications not reversed included, is below zero (a test shows it on the new path). Choices: (1) the release reads one list of sources ordered by their recorded time, newest first, with the source id as the tie-break; (2) a Deposit application is never split: it is reversed whole and its unreleased rest is a plain open obligation for the step, so staff choose where the Deposit value goes under the Settlement rules (Credit first; DL-032 order); (3) the empty plan is valid only when the step has nothing to pay (no Deposit holds money and no released Credit's Customer owes on the Lease), as 10c-1b behaved; otherwise the step refuses the draft (55000); (4) the `CHARGE_REDUCTION` recorder (10c-1a) still refuses a Deposit-paid Invoice; (5, review rounds 1 and 2, security) after the draft locks and the Lease row the confirm takes every other lock through the step's lock function (except the Refund method, `FOR SHARE`, last, as sub-slice 8d and the step take it), which for a `REFUND` draft (patched in migration `20261107_103`, same signature) takes the released sources' Payments and Credits in its single id-ordered Payment and Credit passes together with the Credit steps' (a separate pass could lock Payments out of id order and deadlock with another Lease's correction or Settlement confirm that names a Credit of the same Customer) and the corrected Invoice `FOR UPDATE` in its id-ordered Invoice pass after the Late-payment Charges (an Invoice locked before the Charges could deadlock with a one-call writer that locks a Charge and then its source Invoice); the comment before the lock function in migration `20261106_102` ("then its own sources ... then calls this") is a historical migration and is not edited; (6, review round 1) a remaining Pet Deposit choice is also refused when its text equals the choice of an earlier confirmed correction of the same Settlement (one agreement per correction); known limits for the owner: the database compares the recorded text only, so a changed text passes and the staff remain responsible for recording a real new choice; the comparison runs when the draft is recorded, not again at confirm (a second draft recorded with the same text before the first is confirmed becomes stale once the first correction's Pet Deposit step changes the Settlement's open total and closing balances, P5S02, and is then recorded and checked again); (7, review round 1, answered) the Q-46 exception does not check the Adjustment type or the correction kind: owner answer to Q-46 lets through any Adjustment linked to a confirmed correction of the Invoice's own Settlement, only the `REFUND` correction reaches it today (the `CHARGE_REDUCTION` recorder refuses a Deposit-paid Invoice, `EXTRA_CHARGE` writes no Adjustment), a debit Adjustment of a paid line stays refused (BR-INV-044), and each later correction kind states in its own design whether it relies on it; (8, review round 1, answered) a `REFUND` draft recorded before migration `20261107_103` has no `plan`; its confirm fails with 22023 and writes nothing; no persistent database holds such a draft (V1-056 is unpublished). New checks named for the owner (10b answer 2): none refuses anything anyone could do before, except the approved change of Q-42 answer 2 (a `REFUND` correction whose released Credit's Customer still owes on the Lease needs a plan that pays that debt first).
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-3):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261107_103` (26 assertions in 14 modules); only the expected revision changed. The new PostgreSQL module `test_settlement_deposit_refund_correction_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`; its tests close every Settlement through the real confirm. Narrowed expectation, test name kept: `test_refund_correction_record_and_confirm_send_the_stored_correction` (`test_settlement_correction.py`) also expects the empty `plan` in the request, because the request now carries the DL-032 plan. In `test_settlement_correction_plan_migration.py` (sub-slice 10c-2a-2 round-3 hardening carried here, no expectation weakened): `blocked_by` now also needs the waiter, blocked by the holder, to hold or wait for a lock on `app.leases` (any lock on that relation) and waits up to 10 seconds (was 2), and the stale test asserts that its changes cover every recorded result field; the helper `failure` now runs the deferred checks as a commit would (`SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE`) and then rolls back, also when the action unexpectedly succeeds, so a failing test leaves nothing behind (review round 1 of 10c-2a-3; the first version rolled back before the deferred checks and failed one refusal expected at commit in P4). Owner exception to the narrowing rule (owner answer to Q-2026-10-07-47), six tests of `test_settlement_refund_correction_migration.py` rebuilt on a real closure (the 8e-3 confirm instead of the test-only `close()` helper, which left both Deposits holding money and the Customer owing 25,000, a state in which every Refund correction would now be refused under Q-42 answer 2 and BR-SETTLE-008); each keeps its name and proves the same 10c-1b behaviour: (1) `test_a_refund_correction_releases_a_paid_wrong_charge_and_refunds_it_to_the_invoice_customer`: the Lease starts this month (no unbilled Rent month blocks the closure) and the normal plan is confirmed, so both Deposits end empty and the Customer owes nothing; the "nothing paid would be released" refusal (`NOTHING_RELEASED`) moved to the end of the test and now uses a 1,500 charge issued after closure by an `EXTRA_CHARGE` correction, because after a real closure no Settlement line is unpaid (owner-accepted change beyond setup, 2026-10-07); (2) `test_one_refund_correction_pays_a_payment_and_a_settlement_credit_back_in_two_refunds`: a two-Signer Lease; the Settlement Credit application is written by the real confirm (the second Signer's Credit pays 400 of that Signer's 1,000 line) instead of by hand, the Pet Deposit pays the Primary Tenant's Late-payment Charge and Rent, and the Signer pays the 600 left after closure; the correction of 800 releases the Payment's 600 and 200 of the Settlement Credit application and refunds 600 and 200 to the Signer, who owes nothing else on the Lease (were 12,300, 12,100 and 200 to the Primary Tenant); (3) `test_a_third_party_payment_is_refunded_to_the_invoice_customer_not_the_payer`: real closure; the third party pays a 1,500 charge issued after closure; Refund 500 and re-posted allocation 1,000 (were 5,000 and 7,500 on the other Damage line); (4) `test_a_changed_line_is_stale_an_inactive_method_conflicts_and_a_deposit_paid_invoice_waits`: real closure; narrowed expectation (owner-accepted, 2026-10-07): a Refund correction of the Deposit-paid line is no longer refused by the 8a-1 refusal ("A Deposit application paid this obligation at Final Settlement"), because 10c-2a-3 now corrects Deposit-paid lines as approved; without a plan for the released Deposit value it is refused by the DL-032 step ("Every remaining Deposit balance must be applied or refunded"); the stale, method, isolation and key checks run on a 1,500 charge issued after closure and paid by the Customer, amounts 500, 499 and 700 (were 2,000, 1,999 and 3,000 on the other Damage line); (5) `test_a_credit_released_before_closure_to_the_invoice_customer_is_refunded_to_that_customer` and (6) `test_a_second_refund_of_the_same_credit_beyond_what_the_correction_released_is_refused`: the Customer pays the rest of the Pet-damage line before closure, so the Credit application is that line's newest source, and the Settlement refunds both Deposits; the corrections now Refund 100 (were 12,200 and 12,300, whose Adjustments also removed the unpaid rest of 12,100 and 12,200); the 100 released from that Credit application is unchanged. The seventh test that uses `close()` (`test_the_relaxed_refund_checks_hold_inside_a_hand_built_correction`) builds its correction by hand through the internal functions and is not changed (slice 12 item). Coverage moved (owner answer to Q-2026-10-08-48): at HEAD, tests 5 and 6 were the only tests that confirmed a `REFUND` correction whose amount included an unpaid part; after the rebuild they correct a fully paid line, so that case is proven again by two new tests in the new module (a charge partly paid after closure and refunded for more than the paid part; a Deposit-paid line that still owes, refunded the same way), each amount checked to the cent.
- **Owner answer to Q-2026-10-08-48 (2026-10-08), sub-slice 10c-2a-3 after review round 3:** option (a), as an owner exception like Q-2026-10-07-43. (1) Each rebuilt 10c-1b test must still prove the same behaviour (Q-47 answer); tests 5 and 6 lost the check of a Refund that includes an unpaid part, so that coverage comes back before the commit: two new tests, a charge partly paid after closure and refunded for more than the paid part, and a Deposit-paid line that still owes, refunded the same way, each amount checked to the cent, including how much Deposit value is released; the six rebuilt tests stay as they are. (2) The two plan notes for later correction types and the cheap round-3 notes are fixed. (3) Each check runs once; then one limited review (independent and security) of the new tests and the changed text; commit 10c-2a-3 only if it finds no blocker and no should-fix, otherwise stop and report. (4) Cause C of the 10c-2a-3 runs (a host clock ahead of the Docker VM clock) is accepted; full PostgreSQL runs keep the Mac awake with `caffeinate` until the slice 12 item (lease signature fixtures on database time) is done. (5) After 10c-2a-3 is committed, 10c-2a-4 continues under the same rules.
- **Owner answer to Q-2026-10-08-49 (2026-10-08), inspection supersede after closure (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-4):** option (a). (1) Once a Lease's Final Settlement is closed, every supersede of a move-out inspection on that Lease is refused; the refusal is a conflict, like the other refusals after closure. (2) Damage found after closure is recorded as a new, separate inspection that supersedes nothing; the inspection the closed Settlement relied on never changes. (3) A wrong finding on the inspection the Settlement relied on is fixed only with a post-settlement correction (sub-slice 10c-2a-5 for a wrong pet classification). (4) Known limit: a mistake in an inspection recorded after closure cannot be fixed by a supersede either. The wrong finding is simply not billed, because billing it still needs an `EXTRA_CHARGE` correction confirmed by a `financial.approve` holder. (5) Before code, a read-only check that nothing assumes one chain of inspections per Lease, in particular the readers that decide current findings and pet damage for the Settlement and for corrections; if one does, the question goes to the owner and the work stops. Done 2026-10-08, none found: "current" is decided per inspection (an inspection that no other inspection supersedes) by the Damage source check (migration 65), `app.invoice_line_is_pet_damage` (migration 86; used by the Settlement preview, the Settlement Deposit Refund source, the Settlement correction plan check and step, migrations 89, 91 and 102), the extra charge plan and source state (migration 98) and the inspection snapshot and page read (migration 60); the inspection service and the frontend pass only inspection and finding ids. (6) The committed 10b test `test_a_damage_extra_charge_bills_a_current_unbilled_finding_and_a_stale_draft_waits` changes its set-up so that the damage found after closure is a separate inspection; it keeps its name and must still prove the same behaviour; a part that can no longer happen after closure is named here and in the report; the independent reviewer compares it with its version at HEAD and confirms it was not weakened.
- **Owner answer to Q-2026-10-08-50 (2026-10-08), the 10c-2a-2 inspection test (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-4):** option (a), as an owner exception like Q-2026-10-07-47, for this one test only. (1) `test_an_inspection_correction_waits_for_the_step_and_its_line_is_then_no_pet_damage` keeps its lock part on the closed Settlement: it shows that the superseding command waits for the step's Lease lock and is then refused (P5S01), and that the line stays pet damage and is paid from the Pet Deposit. (2) The test keeps its name; its docstring and this log say that its last words describe the behaviour before 10c-2a-4, and why. (3) The step's own re-check of pet damage stays as a second guard; if no command can reach it any more, this log says so. (4) The independent reviewer compares the test with its version at HEAD and confirms that the lock is still proved. (5) The 10b test change is accepted as described in Q-2026-10-08-50 (the closure-time inspection superseded before closure, the damage found after closure a separate inspection, every assertion as at HEAD). (6) New run rule: long documents, the questions file included, are edited only with exact-text edits, also when adding at the end (ops/V1_055_RUN_RULES.md).
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-4, inspection supersede refused after closure (BR-MOVEOUT-006, BR-SETTLE-012, BR-SETTLE-013; DB-MOVEOUT-001; owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-07-44 and the owner answers to Q-2026-10-08-49 and Q-2026-10-08-50; approved tightening; migration `20261108_104`):** once a Lease's Final Settlement is closed, a supersede of any move-out inspection on that Lease is refused (P5S01, the code of the other refusals after closure, mapped to a conflict by the inspection service) before anything is written; the inspection the closed Settlement relied on never changes, so no line it paid changes its pet classification. The refusal is in the inspection command (`app.move_out_inspection_record`) and in its insert trigger (`app.validate_move_out_inspection_source`), both patched in place (same signatures; owner, security, settings and grants kept). Choices: (1) both read the Lease's Final Settlement row `FOR SHARE` (as the 8e-1 refusal reads it) after the Lease row lock; the Settlement confirm, the only writer of `closed_at`, locks the Lease row `FOR UPDATE` before it closes the Settlement (review round 1 corrected this wording), so a supersede that waited while the Settlement closed reads the closure at READ COMMITTED (tested); a supersede runs only at READ COMMITTED (55000 otherwise, before and after closure; owner answer to Q-2026-10-08-51, which replaced this choice's earlier claim that at REPEATABLE READ the row lock fails with 40001; tested, including a REPEATABLE READ transaction that started before the Settlement existed); (2) an inspection that supersedes nothing is not checked: damage found after closure is a new inspection, and "current" stays per inspection (no reader assumes one chain per Lease, see the Q-49 entry); (3) the exact-repeat replay of a command stays first and writes nothing, so a supersede committed before closure still replays its saved response; (4) the refusal follows the existing supersede source checks (unknown source P5M01, changed source P5M03); (5) the service message tells staff to record later damage as a new inspection; there is no inspection HTTP route yet (V1-055 Slice D), so docs/005 is unchanged. Known limit (owner answer to Q-49, point 4): an inspection recorded after closure cannot be superseded either; its wrong finding is simply not billed, because billing it needs an `EXTRA_CHARGE` correction confirmed by a `financial.approve` holder.
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-4):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261108_104` (26 assertions in 14 modules); only the expected revision changed. The new PostgreSQL module `test_inspection_supersede_after_closure_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`. (1) `test_a_damage_extra_charge_bills_a_current_unbilled_finding_and_a_stale_draft_waits` (sub-slice 10b; owner answers to Q-49 and Q-50): set-up only, name kept, every assertion as at HEAD. The closure-time inspection is now superseded before the Settlement closes (an inspection with no findings), so its billed finding is still refused as "not on the current inspection" after closure; the damage found after closure is then a separate inspection that supersedes nothing (was: a supersede of the closure-time inspection after closure).
