The part that can no longer happen after closure is recording later damage by a supersede; the new module tests its refusal. (2) `test_an_inspection_correction_waits_for_the_step_and_its_line_is_then_no_pet_damage` (sub-slice 10c-2a-2; owner exception for this one test, answer to Q-50): the lock part is kept (the step holds the Lease row lock on the closed Settlement and the superseding command waits); review round 1 made its wait check stricter, not weaker: the new helper `blocked_on_lease_row` runs `blocked_by` and also needs the waiter to hold the tuple lock of an `app.leases` row and none of an `app.final_settlements` row, because `blocked_by` alone accepts any lock on `app.leases` (every inspection command holds one) and the step also holds the Settlement row; the end changed: once the holder rolls back, the command reads the closed Settlement and is refused (P5S01) with nothing written (was: written), the line stays pet damage (was: no longer pet damage), and the step pays it from the Pet Deposit as pet damage, the Deposit application checked to the cent (was: refused, "The Pet Deposit first pays only valid pet damage"); its name is kept as the run rule asks, and its last words describe the behaviour before 10c-2a-4, when the waiting supersede was written after the step. (3) The step's own re-check of pet damage stays as a second guard (owner answer to Q-50, point 3). No command can reach it any more: the recorder's plan check already refuses a Pet Deposit pet-damage step on a line that is not pet damage (tested in the 10c-2a-2 refusal test), and the step's re-check differs only when the line's classification changed between the draft and the confirm; `app.invoice_line_is_pet_damage` reads only immutable records apart from whether the inspection is superseded, and after closure that can no longer change. Until sub-slice 10c-2a-5 adds the pet classification correction (whose record the reader will read first), only a correction built by hand by the table owner could reach it. No test reaches it through the step any more: the 10c-2a-2 test was the only one (review round 2 notes, both reviewers); the same check is still tested where the recorder calls it (the 10c-2a-2 plan refusal test), and the Deposit application trigger is a third guard. Sub-slice 10c-2a-5, which makes the step's re-check reachable again, tests it through the step. (4) A unit test shows the service maps P5S01 to a conflict (`test_move_out_inspection.py`). (5) After the owner answer to Q-2026-10-08-51 (review round 1), the new module has seven tests: `test_a_supersede_at_repeatable_read_cannot_miss_a_closure` now covers the gap itself (a REPEATABLE READ transaction that started before the Settlement existed is refused, 55000, when it tries the supersede after closure; the earlier version of this uncommitted test expected 40001 from a snapshot that already held the Settlement row); `test_a_supersede_outside_read_committed_is_refused_also_before_closure` is new; the trigger test also refuses a direct superseding row at REPEATABLE READ; the lock-wait tests use `blocked_on_lease_row`; the before-closure test replays a supersede after closure. A read-only search found no existing test that supersedes outside READ COMMITTED, so no other expectation changed. The service mapping is unchanged for 55000 (the service always writes at READ COMMITTED).
- **Owner answer to Q-2026-10-08-51 (2026-10-08), a supersede in a REPEATABLE READ transaction (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-4, review round 1):** option (a); the further tightening is approved. Any inspection supersede outside READ COMMITTED is refused with 55000, before and after closure, as the Settlement confirm and the correction confirms already require. Reason (both round-1 reviewers): a REPEATABLE READ or SERIALIZABLE transaction whose snapshot is older than the Settlement's start does not see the Settlement row, so the closed-Settlement read finds nothing and the Lease row lock raises nothing (the start and the confirm lock the Lease row but never change it); the design's "at REPEATABLE READ the row lock fails with 40001" held only when the Settlement row was in the snapshot. No screen or service path changes (the service always writes at READ COMMITTED); an existing test that supersedes outside READ COMMITTED changes only that expectation and is named in the report; a new test covers the gap itself (a REPEATABLE READ transaction that started before the Settlement existed is refused when it tries the supersede after closure); the design note is corrected.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-5, pet classification correction after closure (DL-032, BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-012, BR-SETTLE-013, BR-DEP-027, BR-AUTH-010; DB-SETTLE-005, DB-SETTLE-007 (new); owner answer to Q-2026-10-07-45, answer 3 to Q-2026-10-07-44; migration `20261109_105`):** a Damage line whose pet classification is wrong after closure, in either direction, is fixed by the correction variant `PET_DAMAGE_RECLASSIFICATION` (the Q-45 option name), one line per draft, recorded under `final_settlements.prepare` with a reason and an evidence reference (`app.record_settlement_pet_classification_draft`, `SettlementCorrectionService.record_pet_classification`) and confirmed only by a `financial.approve` holder (`app.confirm_settlement_pet_classification`). The charge amount stays the same and the closed inspection is not changed: the confirm writes, each linked before it is written, a classification record (DB-SETTLE-007, new append-only table; DB-SETTLE-005 link `settlement_pet_classification_id`), the reversal of the line's Deposit applications where the corrected classification changes which Deposit should pay, and then the shared DL-032 step after closure with the draft's plan. `app.invoice_line_is_pet_damage` reads the line's latest classification record before the inspection rule. Choices: (1) which applications are reversed, read from the owner's words "where the new classification changes which Deposit should pay": to not pet damage, the line's Pet-Deposit-first applications (the Pet Deposit may then pay the line only at step 5, by a staff reason or, at a BR-DEP-027 termination, a Customer choice given for this correction); to pet damage, the line's Security Deposit applications, but only while a Pet Deposit of the Lease holds money (otherwise the Security Deposit still is the Deposit that pays, and only the record is written); a Pet Deposit remainder application stays (the same Deposit pays); each reversal is whole (10c-2a-1); (2) the draft's plan is checked at record under the corrected classification through a new overlay key of the step's check (`pet_damage`; a relaxing change of the internal `app.settlement_correction_plan_result`), and at the step the record exists; (3) the Settlement rules apply unchanged: at a normal end the Pet Deposit's value may not be refunded while the line it can pay at step 5 still owes, so staff give a reason and the remaining Pet Deposit pays it; at a BR-DEP-027 termination without a new Customer choice the remaining Pet Deposit pays no other debt (owner answer 3 to Q-2026-10-07-44), so its value is refunded and the line stays open debt (review round 1 corrected this reason; in general a Deposit refunded at closure is not taken back, the Q-45 text the owner accepted); (4) the lock function (same signature) takes the line's Invoice `FOR UPDATE` in its Invoice pass; the confirm takes each plan Refund method `FOR SHARE` last; (5) the source revision covers the closure, the line's Invoice and what the line owes, its Deposit applications and reversals, its classification records and the Lease's Deposits' held values; (6) a second correction may correct the same line again (the latest record decides; review round 1: a new record must be dated later than the line's latest one, and the confirm dates it so); the same classification is refused (55000); (7) routes stay in slice 11e, so docs/005 is unchanged. Review round 1 (independent and security) also: (8) the insert check binds a record to its own correction and to exactly what its `PET_DAMAGE_RECLASSIFICATION` draft prepared (line, classification, evidence, reason), as the step's checks do; (9) only a Pet Deposit on the DL-032 path counts as holding money for the to-pet-damage reversal; (10) a line billed from an inspection superseded before closure is not eligible, so the owner's open point on carrying a finding forward (Q-2026-10-07-45, "Also") stays open; (11) the confirm checks the Customer choice again under the Settlement's draft lock, so of two drafts recorded while both were pending with the same choice only the first confirms (the second is refused, 55000, and stays pending). The committed `REFUND` confirm (10c-2a-3) has the same gap; it is not changed here (an owner item).
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-5):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261109_105` (26 assertions in 14 modules); only the expected revision changed. The new PostgreSQL module `test_settlement_pet_classification_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`; it includes the test carried from the 10c-2a-4 review (the step's own pet-damage re-check refusing through the step after a later correction changed the line's classification). Three unit tests in `test_settlement_correction.py` cover the new command and service. No committed test changed its expectations.
- **Owner answers to the sub-slice 10c-2a-5 report (2026-10-08; Q-2026-10-08-52):** (1) Approved tightening: every post-settlement correction confirm that uses a Customer choice (the remaining Pet Deposit at a BR-DEP-027 termination, `pet_remainder` with basis `CUSTOMER_CHOICE`) checks that choice again at the confirm, as the `PET_DAMAGE_RECLASSIFICATION` confirm does (choice (11) above), including the committed `REFUND` confirm of sub-slice 10c-2a-3. The check moves into the shared step (`app.post_settlement_correction_plan`), with a test, in a small sub-slice (10c-2a-6) right after 10c-2a-5 is committed. (2) The binding of the 10c-2a-1 Deposit application reversal check to the correction draft's list of reversals (`reversed_applications`, `released_sources`) joins the slice 12 list. (4) Not a stop: a Damage line billed from a finding of an inspection superseded before closure has no pet classification fix after closure (choice (10) above); the case and the recommended fix path are Q-2026-10-08-53, placed in the plan before 10h; the owner answers it before 10h. (5) Accepted: the to-pet-damage reversal (a Security Deposit application reversed while a Pet Deposit on the DL-032 path holds money) is reachable today only from a hand-built state; whichever of sub-slices 10c-2b and 10d first returns value to a Pet Deposit adds a test of that reversal on a real path, not a hand-built state (plan). (6) Confirmed: choice (1) above, which Deposit applications are reversed, in both directions; and choice (3) at a normal end: a line moved to not pet damage is paid by the remaining Pet Deposit with a staff reason (DL-032 step 5), because no Deposit is refunded while the line still owes.
- **Known limit (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-5, owner answer 3 to its report):** a correction that uses the remaining Pet Deposit at a BR-DEP-027 termination needs a new Customer choice (owner answer 3 to Q-2026-10-07-44). The system compares the choice as exact text with the Settlement's choice and every earlier correction's choice, and refuses only an exact repeat; a reworded earlier consent passes. The system cannot prove that a Customer gave a choice again, so the `financial.approve` holder who confirms the correction is the real check.
- **Owner answer to Q-2026-10-08-54 (2026-10-08), the Customer choice re-check in the shared step (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-6):** option (a). (1) The shared step (`app.post_settlement_correction_plan`) keeps the full re-check: it refuses the Final Settlement's own choice text and any earlier confirmed correction's choice, as the recorders and the 10c-2a-5 confirm do. (2) The committed 10c-2a-2 test `test_a_br_dep_027_termination_after_closure` builds a correction by hand whose plan reused the Final Settlement's own choice text, which the re-check refuses; only that test's choice text changes to a new one, given for that correction (owner answer 3 to Q-2026-10-07-44); its name and every assertion stay the same, and the change is listed in the 10c-2a-6 updated-tests entry. (3) The independent reviewer compares that test with its version at HEAD and confirms it was not weakened.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-6, the Customer choice checked again in the shared step (BR-DEP-027, BR-SETTLE-013, BR-AUTH-010; owner answer 3 to Q-2026-10-07-44, owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-08-52 (approved tightening) and the answer to Q-2026-10-08-54; migration `20261110_106`):** the shared DL-032 step after closure (`app.post_settlement_correction_plan`, patched in place through `pg_get_functiondef`, so owner, SECURITY DEFINER, settings and privileges stay) refuses (55000, the recorders' messages) a `CUSTOMER_CHOICE` text for the remaining Pet Deposit that equals the confirmed Final Settlement's choice or the choice of another confirmed correction of the same Settlement. It covers every variant that runs the step: the committed `REFUND` confirm (10c-2a-3), the `PET_DAMAGE_RECLASSIFICATION` confirm (10c-2a-5) and every later one. Choices: (1) the check runs right after the step's locks (every variant takes the Settlement's draft lock before the step, so two confirms on one Settlement serialize) and before the step reads the plan and compares the result, so a reused choice is reported as such, not as stale by the step's own comparison (a variant's earlier stale checks, such as the `REFUND` confirm's source digest and release, still come first; review round 2); (2) the current draft's own CONFIRMED decision, written by `app.begin_settlement_correction` before the step, is left out by its id; (3) the refusal rolls the whole confirm back, so the draft stays pending and nothing is written; (4) the 10c-2a-5 confirm keeps its own re-check, which runs first with the same messages (removing it would change committed code and the source-order pin of its least-privilege test); (5) no service logic changes (55000 is already a conflict); the `confirm_refund` docstring names the refusal; no route (slice 11e), so docs/005 is unchanged. The text comparison stays a known limit (the entry above).
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2a-6):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261110_106` (26 assertions in 14 modules); only the expected revision changed. - **Owner answers to Q-2026-10-08-55 (2026-10-08), the returned-Refund correction after closure (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2b):** (1) yes: a returned Credit Refund's Credit first pays its own Customer's open debt on this Lease, as in every Refund correction (owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-07-42); this holds whether or not the same correction pays a new Refund; only the rest is refunded, or stays as that Customer's Credit. (2) Yes: Credit left by a return-only correction is that Customer's Credit, as in owner answer 4 to Q-2026-10-07-41; a later correction may refund it, and the ordinary Credit paths stay as they are. (3) Yes: a returned Deposit Refund goes back to the Deposit; when the same correction pays a new Refund, the Deposit value first pays this Lease's open debt in the DL-032 order, with all Settlement rules, and only the rest is refunded. (4) Yes: the variant names are `REFUND_RETURN` (a returned Refund, with or without a new Refund) and `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` (the refund-only form), variants of the `SETTLEMENT_CORRECTION` draft kind. (5) Approved tightening: after closure the receipt reversal draft of a Lease Deposit is refused; a Deposit receipt found wrong after closure, for example a bounced Payment, is fixed only through a post-settlement correction, whose kind the plan names; if none fits, the question goes to the owner first. (6) Approved tightening: after closure the one-call verified return of a Pet Refund-review Refund and the one-call reversal of such a return are refused, as owner answer 4 to the sub-slice 10c-1b review record did for a correction's Credit Refund; such a return is handled by the returned Deposit Refund correction of sub-slice 10c-2b-2 and tested there.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2b-1, a returned Credit Refund of a post-settlement correction (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-CREDIT-006, BR-CREDIT-008, BR-CREDIT-014, BR-REFUND-014, DL-032, BR-AUTH-010; owner answer 4 to the sub-slice 10c-1b review record, answer 4 to Q-2026-10-07-41, answers 1 and 2 to Q-2026-10-07-42 and answers 1, 2 and 4 to Q-2026-10-08-55; migration `20261111_107`):** approved tightening: a Refund that a post-settlement correction paid is corrected only through another post-settlement correction; the one-call verified return and its one-call reversal refuse it (P5202; their routes answer 422, docs/005 API-066) and the Refund correction check refuses every other row for it (23514), accepting only the correction's own verified return with its draft's Refund, amount, return date and references, one per correction. The new variant `REFUND_RETURN` records that such a Credit Refund's money came back: Staff record it under `final_settlements.prepare` with a reason, the returned amount, the return date, the return method reference, the evidence, the DL-032 plan and, optionally, a new Refund's method and reference note (`app.record_settlement_refund_return_draft`, `SettlementCorrectionService.record_refund_return`); only a `financial.approve` holder confirms it (`app.confirm_settlement_refund_return`, `confirm_refund_return`). The confirm writes the verified return under `financial.approve`, linked to the correction first, so the value goes back to the Credit; the shared DL-032 step then lets that Credit pay its own Customer's open debt on the Lease first, whether or not a new Refund is paid; only the rest is refunded to the same Customer, or stays as that Customer's Credit. Choices for the owner: (1) the new Refund is what is left in the Credit after the step, at most the returned amount, the rule of the `REFUND` confirm; a Credit that held other value may so pay the debt from that value and refund the whole returned amount; (2) the verified return carries `financial.approve` as its authorization reference and the draft's reason, and is dated at the confirm, after the Refund, its corrections and the Credit's latest event; (3) in this sub-slice only a correction's Credit Refund can be returned: a Settlement Deposit Refund, a correction's Deposit Refund and a Pet Refund-review Refund are refused (55000) until sub-slice 10c-2b-2; (4) one Refund method and note per correction, so a Deposit Refund step needs the new Refund's method and note, and a return-only draft has no Deposit Refund step; (5) the return date may not be before the Refund or later than the recording time; (6) a return recorded by a correction is never reversed: a mistaken return has no fix yet (owner answer 4 to Q-2026-10-07-41: it needs a later correction kind). The relaxed Credit Refund check of sub-slice 10c-1b also counts what the correction's own returns gave back to the Credit, so the new Refund may go to a Customer who did not make the Payment (BR-CREDIT-014), and no more. The lock function takes the returned Refund's Credit and its Payment in its id-ordered passes.

- **V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2b-3, the refund-only form after a return-only correction (BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-013, BR-CREDIT-006, BR-CREDIT-008, BR-CREDIT-014, BR-REFUND-014, BR-REFUND-015, DL-032, BR-AUTH-010; owner answer 3 to Q-2026-10-07-42, answers 1, 2 and 4 to Q-2026-10-08-55, answer 2 to Q-2026-10-08-59; migration `20261114_110`):** the new variant `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` refunds value that an earlier return-only `REFUND_RETURN` correction of the same Final Settlement left, linked to that correction. Staff record it under `final_settlements.prepare` with a reason, the earlier correction, the new Refund's method and reference note (both required) and the DL-032 plan (`app.record_settlement_held_value_refund_draft`, `SettlementCorrectionService.record_held_value_refund`); only a `financial.approve` holder confirms it (`app.confirm_settlement_held_value_refund`, `confirm_held_value_refund`). A held Credit first pays its own Customer's open debt on this Lease through the shared step (owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-08-55), and only the rest is refunded to that Customer; held Deposit money first pays this Lease's open debt in the DL-032 order, and the plan's Deposit Refund steps refund the rest (recipient and agreement as BR-REFUND-015). Only a Deposit on the DL-032 path may be refunded so (55000 otherwise; Q-2026-10-09-61 stays open for the others). Choices for the owner: (1) what the earlier correction left in a Credit is its return less the Credit applications its own step and every linked held-value correction wrote from that Credit and less their Refunds from it; the new Refund is what is left in the Credit after the step, at most that value (the rule of choice (1) of 10c-2b-1); value the Customer used through the ordinary Credit paths only lowers the Credit's available value; (2) a held-value correction, of either form, applies or refunds every held DL-032 Deposit balance, not only the linked correction's amount, because the shared step requires every such balance to be applied or refunded (BR-SETTLE-008): the Deposit form applies or refunds every held Deposit balance, and a Credit form recorded while a Deposit holds returned money (owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-08-59) also applies or refunds that Deposit money; the confirm's audit event lists every Deposit balance it settled (`settled_deposits`); (3) the link to the earlier correction is the draft's `held_from_settlement_correction_id` and the confirm's audit event; no table or column is added; (4) after review round 1 (owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-09-63): an earlier correction is named only while it still has value left, and the Credit or the Deposit must still hold value (55000); the record-time refusal of a Credit correction that would write nothing (55000) stays as a backstop that these checks now come before. Also after review round 1: a Deposit correction's draft records the Deposit's owner, and its audit event names the recipient of the Deposit Refund the step wrote, or the Deposit's owner when nothing was refunded; one correction may still carry two Refund recipients (the Credit's Customer and a Deposit Refund to an agreed Signer), as `REFUND_RETURN`; the new Refund's method and note are required even when nothing is refunded, as the `REFUND` correction; the human-approver check of the confirm is defence in depth (a service identity is refused first, P4506), as in the other variants.
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2b-3):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261114_110` (26 assertions in 14 modules and the 10c-2b-1 module's head assertion); only the expected revision changed. The new PostgreSQL module `test_settlement_held_value_refund_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`; three unit tests in `test_settlement_correction.py` cover the new command and service. No other committed test changed its expectations: a read-only search of every backend test found that the tests reading the lock function's source count Payment and Credit lock passes, which the patch does not add, and that the direct calls of the relaxed Credit Refund check use other variants, for which the new term is 0. Review rounds 1 and 2 add to the new module (9 tests): the cap binding where the Credit holds more than the earlier correction left (two return-only corrections of one Refund), on the payer's Credit after the earlier correction's own step paid debt, and on a third-party Credit with a linked 1-cent Refund refused by the relaxed check; this correction's own step not lowering the cap (owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-09-63); naming an earlier correction again after its value is gone refused with no money or record changed (owner answer 1); a Credit correction with an empty Credit refused although its plan has a Deposit Refund step; the Q-2026-10-08-59 answer 2 path refunding the Credit and the held Deposit money in one correction; a return-only correction of another Settlement (P0002); the database guards (22023, 42501, P5205 and the replay, P4506, READ COMMITTED); the Lease and Credit row locks, and the Credit lock taken before the stale check (the lock function's patch); the audit `customer_id` and `settled_deposits` of a Deposit correction. The 10c-2b-1 NOWAIT check of the Credit row (`test_a_returned_refund_correction_refuses_wrong_input_and_a_stale_draft_waits`) would also pass without its lock patch, because the Refund insert locks the Credit too; it goes to the slice 12 list.
- **Owner answers to Q-2026-10-09-63 (2026-10-09), the refund-only form after review round 1 (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2b-3):** (1) Option (a): a `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` correction may name an earlier return-only `REFUND_RETURN` correction only while that correction still has value left; it is checked at record (55000, "Nothing the earlier correction returned is left to refund"); at the confirm a draft whose earlier correction's value changed after it was recorded is stale (P5S02, its source digest holds the value left, the Credit's or Deposit's value and the linked corrections) and stays pending, and the same check runs again as a backstop; naming the same correction again after its value is gone changes no money and no record. How the value left is counted (never below 0): for a Credit, the earlier correction's verified return to that Credit, less the Credit applications from that Credit written by the earlier correction's own step and by every confirmed `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` correction linked to it, and less the Refunds from that Credit of those linked corrections; at the confirm this correction's own step applications and Refund are left out, so they do not lower the cap of its own Refund (answer 2); the Credit must also still hold value (55000, "The Credit holds no returned value to refund", also when the plan has other steps). For a Deposit, the earlier correction's verified return to that Deposit, less the Deposit applications and the Refunds from that Deposit written by every confirmed `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` correction linked to it (a return-only Deposit correction runs no step); the Deposit must also still hold money. Only records of corrections linked to the named correction lower its value: Deposit money that another correction's step settles (a held-value correction linked to another earlier correction, or a `REFUND` or `REFUND_RETURN` correction with a new Refund, each of which must apply or refund every held DL-032 Deposit balance, BR-SETTLE-008) lowers only what the Deposit holds, and the audit event of the correction that settled it lists every Deposit balance it settled. (2) Kept as built, as approved for sub-slice 10c-2b-1 (docs/010 choice (1)): Credit spent through the ordinary Credit paths (a one-call Credit application, an ordinary Credit Refund draft) lowers only the Credit's available value, and the available value still caps every Refund. **Known limit:** the value an earlier correction left can be more than the Customer can still get back (in a Credit for value spent through the ordinary Credit paths, and, by the counting of answer 1, in a Deposit for money another correction settled); no Refund is ever more than the Credit or the Deposit holds. Updated by sub-slice 10c-2b-4 (owner answer to Q-2026-10-09-64): a held-value correction now uses, in each Deposit, only what its own earlier correction left there, so in a Deposit this limit stays only for money that a correction naming no earlier correction settled (see the 10c-2b-4 entry); the Credit part is unchanged.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10c-3a, an unpaid Late-payment Charge after closure (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-LATE-005, BR-AUTH-010; owner answer to Q-2026-10-07-38, recommendation (a); migration `20261115_111`):** 10c-3 is split (a split needs no owner answer): 3a an unpaid Charge, 3b a paid one. The `CHARGE_REDUCTION` correction also reduces a wrong Late-payment Charge of an Invoice of the Settlement's Lease that is still unpaid: Staff record it under `final_settlements.prepare` with a reason, the Charge and the amount (`app.record_settlement_late_charge_reduction_draft`, `SettlementCorrectionService.record_late_charge_reduction`; the draft's `target_kind` is `LATE_PAYMENT_CHARGE`); only a `financial.approve` holder confirms it (`app.confirm_settlement_late_charge_reduction`, `confirm_late_charge_reduction`; human approver, READ COMMITTED, stale P5S02, replay). The confirm links and writes one Late-payment Charge correction with authorization reference `financial.approve` and the draft's reason, so the Charge's obligation drops and its history keeps the original amount (BR-LATE-005). Refused at record, as the Invoice-line form (at the confirm such a change makes the draft stale, P5S02, and the same checks run again as a backstop): more than the unpaid rest (the current obligation less the active Payment, Credit and Deposit applications), a Charge that a Deposit application paid (8a-1; the paid case is 10c-3b), a reversed Charge and a Charge on a VOID Invoice (55000); a Charge of another Lease (P0002). The Invoice-line confirm now refuses a draft with a `target_kind` (not found), and the Charge confirm refuses an Invoice-line draft. Choices for the owner: (1) removing the whole current obligation writes a `CHARGE_REVERSAL` (the Charge is then terminal; the Charge correction rules allow a `REDUCTION` only to a result above zero), any smaller amount a `REDUCTION`; (2) the confirm locks the Lease row, then the Charge and its Invoice (the order of the allocation writers), before the Settlement row; (3) the source digest holds the Charge's Invoice VOID state, not its status, so a Payment of the Rent line does not make the draft stale. After review round 1: the Charge form counts only Deposit applications not reversed (the 8a-1 trigger as patched in 10c-2a-1), while the Invoice-line form still refuses an Invoice with any Deposit application; the difference cannot arise for a Charge yet. The new confirm checks Deposit applications before the unpaid-rest check, which is what the 8a-1 note asked of a Charge correction that becomes a correction effect; the one-call writer `app.correct_late_payment_charge` is unchanged and stays refused after closure (P4608 or P5S01). The VOID refusal is a backstop (an Invoice is not voided after closure, and the Charge correction row's own check refuses a VOID Invoice). **Known gap until sub-slice 10h, as accepted for the Invoice-line form under Q-2026-10-07-39:** a one-call reversal of an allocation that paid the Charge at closure, followed by this reduction, removes Charge value the closed Settlement relied on without a `REFUND` record (no money leaves: the value stays with the Payment); 10h closes it and its test covers the Late-payment Charge path too.
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-3a):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261115_111` (26 assertions in 14 modules and the 10c-2b-1 module's head assertion); only the expected revision changed. The new PostgreSQL module `test_settlement_late_charge_reduction_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`; three unit tests in `test_settlement_correction.py` cover the new command and service. No other committed test changed its expectations: a read-only search of every backend test found no test that reads the patched Invoice-line confirm's source except the 10c-1a check that its functions use no `interval` or `age(` (still true), and the 10c-1a forward-only test upgrades only to its own revision.
- **Owner answer of 2026-10-09 to the Deposit counting reported after sub-slice 10c-2b-3 (Q-2026-10-09-64; approved tightening):** the stricter count. A Deposit correction that names an earlier correction may use at most the value that correction has left; this limit covers the Lease debt it pays and the Refund together. To use more held money, staff prepare another correction that names the other earlier correction. Built as a small sub-slice before 10c-3b (10c-2b-4) with a new migration; no committed migration changes. The committed test that shows the old limit is changed so that it proves the new refusal and keeps its name; the independent reviewer compares it with its version at HEAD. The known-limit entry of owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-09-63 is updated by the 10c-2b-4 entry. The owner asks whether the Credit side counts the debt it pays in the same way, to be reported and not changed.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2b-4, the stricter Deposit count of the refund-only form (BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-013, DL-032, BR-AUTH-010; owner answer to Q-2026-10-09-64; migration `20261116_112`):** a `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` correction uses, in each Deposit, at most what its earlier correction left there (`app.settlement_held_deposit_value`), the Lease debt it pays and the Refund together. A Deposit the earlier correction returned nothing to gives it nothing: a Credit correction (its earlier correction returned a Credit Refund) uses no Deposit money, and Deposit money that another earlier correction returned stays held until staff prepare a correction that names that correction. As built after owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-09-65 (option (b), "at most"): the correction may leave part of the value its earlier correction left in the Deposit; that part stays held for a later correction that names the same earlier correction; but a correction whose limit reaches held money must use some of it. BR-SETTLE-008 and the DL-032 order stay as they are for every correction, also one that leaves value held (for example no Deposit Refund while debt the Deposit could pay stays open). Refusals (55000): more than the limit in any Deposit, "A held-value correction uses at most what its earlier correction left in each Deposit"; none of that value used, the existing message "Every remaining Deposit balance must be applied or refunded" (kept, so the committed no-plan refusal of the Deposit form is unchanged). (Before the owner answer the correction had to use exactly the smaller of the value left and what the Deposit holds; review round 2 found a case where two corrections then each stay refused.) This replaces, for this variant, choice (2) of sub-slice 10c-2b-3: a held-value correction no longer applies or refunds every held DL-032 Deposit balance, and a Credit correction no longer settles held Deposit money (which owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-08-59 had allowed). Built as exact-text patches of four functions (no new function, table or column; no service change): the shared plan check takes an optional overlay key `held_left` (a Deposit id to cents; malformed, or together with `hold_deposits` true, 22023) and checks the limit in place of the every-balance rule; the recorder stores it in the draft's `plan_overlay` (`{Deposit: value left}` for the Deposit form, `{}` for the Credit form); the shared step passes it for this variant (a draft without it: `{}`, no Deposit money); the confirm treats a draft whose stored limit differs from the recomputed one as stale (P5S02), so a draft recorded under `20261114_110` stays pending. The other step rules stay (DL-032 order, Credit before Deposits, pet damage first, no Deposit Refund while debt the Deposit could pay stays open, the Security Deposit before the remaining Pet Deposit). Because other held money is no longer used in the same correction, these rules now order the corrections: while the Security Deposit holds money, a correction limited to the Pet Deposit cannot use the remaining Pet Deposit (step 5) for other debt; and, added after review round 1 (the security reviewer's finding), while a Pet Deposit keeps held money that the correction may not use and pet damage stays open after the Pet Deposit's step, no Security Deposit step pays that pet damage (55000, "The Pet Deposit pays open pet damage before any other use or Refund"; narrowed after review round 2 so that the Security Deposit may still pay other debt; owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-09-65: owner answer 3 (ii) to Q-2026-10-05-20 and the 10c-2b-2a hold rule only stop the Security Deposit from paying pet damage instead of the Pet Deposit, and never stopped it from paying other debt), so the correction that names the Pet Deposit's earlier correction pays it; a Security Deposit Refund while that debt stays open is refused by the existing rule. Pet damage paid by the Pet Deposit itself (step 3) and a Pet Refund where the rules allow it are not held back. The case of Q-2026-10-09-65 (both Deposits hold other corrections' money, pet damage and other debt open) is settled through several corrections in either order, because a correction may leave part of its value held. Each Customer's Credit still pays that Customer's open debt first whenever any Deposit holds money, also money this correction may not use. **Known limit, updated (owner instruction):** the value a return-only correction left in a Deposit can still be more than the Deposit holds, but only when a correction that names no earlier correction settled that money: a `REFUND` correction, a `REFUND_RETURN` correction with a new Refund and a `PET_DAMAGE_RECLASSIFICATION` correction still apply or refund every held DL-032 Deposit balance (BR-SETTLE-008), and a return-only Credit correction still applies held Deposit money that open debt can take (owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-08-59). Naming that earlier correction is then refused (55000, "The Deposit holds no returned money to refund"). After review round 1: when such a correction settled the money and a later return-only correction brings money back to the same Deposit, a correction that names the first one may use the new money (the smaller of its value left and what the Deposit holds), and naming the later one is then refused for the same reason. The settled money is a linked effect of the correction that settled it; no Refund is ever more than the Deposit holds. **Owner check, the Credit side (reported, not changed):** the Credit form does not count the debt it pays in the same way. Its new Refund is at most what the earlier correction left, but this held-value correction's own step applications from the Credit are left out of that cap (owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-09-63, kept as built), so the debt it pays and its Refund together may exceed what the earlier correction left (the committed test `test_a_held_credit_first_pays_its_customer_s_debt_and_only_the_rest_is_refunded`: 1,800 left, 1,500 paid to debt and 500 refunded); what the earlier correction left is lowered only by its own step's applications from the Credit and by the applications and Refunds of the other corrections linked to it. Owner answer 3 to Q-2026-10-09-65: kept as built, because a Customer's Credit pays that Customer's own debt first, whatever its source (owner answers to Q-2026-10-05-29 and Q-2026-10-08-55). **Known limit, Credit side (owner answer 3):** a held-value correction's own Refund counts against the value its earlier correction left, but the debt that correction pays from the Credit does not; the debt paid by the earlier correction's own step and by the held-value corrections confirmed before it does lower what is left for later corrections.
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2b-4):** owner instruction: the committed test that showed the old limit, `test_a_credit_return_while_a_deposit_holds_money_is_refunded_with_that_money` (`test_settlement_held_value_refund_migration.py`), keeps its name and now proves the new refusal: a Credit correction linked to the Credit's return-only correction with the Pet Deposit's Refund step is refused and writes nothing (it was the refunded case); without that step it is recorded (it was the every-balance refusal) and refunds only the Credit's 400 while the Pet Deposit keeps its 2,000; a correction linked to the Deposit's return-only correction refunds the 2,000 with the same audit check as before; naming that correction again is refused because it has nothing left (it was "The Deposit holds no returned money to refund", which the new module now shows on its remaining path). Its later checks are unchanged; its traceability note follows. The head-revision assertions now expect `20261116_112` (26 assertions in 14 modules and the 10c-2b-1 module's head assertion); only the expected revision changed. The new PostgreSQL module `test_settlement_held_deposit_limit_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`. No other committed test changed its expectations: a read-only search of every backend test found that the module's Deposit-form tests use exactly the value left and keep the every-balance message, that the plan-check tests of 10c-2a-2 and 10c-2b-2a pass no `held_left`, and that the source checks of the step (the choice re-check order, no `interval` or `age(`) still hold.
- **Owner answers of 2026-10-09 for sub-slice 10c-3b (Q-2026-10-09-66), given before its design:** (1) Deposit applications on a paid Late-payment Charge follow the owner answer to Q-2026-10-07-46, the same way as for Adjustments; if that does not fit Charges, the reason and a recommendation go to the questions file and the work stops. (2) Where the Credit freed from a paid Charge is recorded is a technical choice: an additive or relaxing migration only; every money movement links to the correction that caused it; the choice is written in the design and in docs/004 and checked by the reviewers; a choice that would change who gets the money, or the order the money is used in, needs the owner first.
- **Owner answers to Q-2026-10-09-65 (2026-10-09), sub-slice 10c-2b-4 after review round 2:** (1) option (b): "at most" is what the owner meant; a held-value correction may leave part of the value that its named correction left in a Deposit, held for a later correction that names the same earlier correction; it must still use some value (the committed no-plan test does not change); BR-SETTLE-008 and the DL-032 order stay exactly as they are for every correction, also one that leaves value held; tests: the Q-65 case settled through more than one correction in both orders, each end result in the DL-032 order, and a correction that leaves value held still cannot pay a Refund that BR-SETTLE-008 refuses. (2) The narrow guard is agreed, for the reason written in the 10c-2b-4 entry. (3) The Credit side is kept as built; its known limit is written in the 10c-2b-4 entry (as the code counts it: the earlier correction's own step and the linked corrections confirmed before also lower what is left). (4) The read-only findings for 10c-3b are accepted: `invoice_credit_excess_releases` is relaxed with exactly one of its two links (the Adjustment or the Late-payment Charge correction); 10c-3b is split into Payment and Credit sources first, then Deposit-paid Charges.
- **Owner answers to Q-2026-10-09-67 (2026-10-09), sub-slice 10c-2b-4 after review round 3:** (1) option (a), the owner exception of Q-2026-10-07-44: the four stale texts of the old exact-use rule (the new test module's docstring and three plan sentences) and the loose sentence of this log on `hold_deposits` are fixed as text only, checked with git diff, then the three static checks and one full unit run, and 10c-2b-4 is committed if they are clean (no PostgreSQL run: no PostgreSQL test reads these lines). (2) The Q-65 test in both orders, on the plan check called directly, is accepted for now; a real-path version with real corrections recorded and confirmed is a task in the plan to be done before 10h. (3) The Credit-side wording of the 10c-2b-4 entry is right because it matches the code; the wording of owner answer 3 to Q-2026-10-09-65 was not exact; nothing else changes. (4) After the commit, the 10c-3b pre-code design is written in the plan and committed, with no code; then the work stops for a fresh session.
- **Owner approval of 2026-10-09, sub-slice 10c-3b-1 design gap (Q-2026-10-09-68, asked in the session before code):** the shared plan check `app.settlement_correction_plan_result` refused a released Payment allocation in its Credit overlay unless the allocation was on an Invoice of the Lease (a Late-payment Charge allocation has no Invoice), so every Charge `REFUND` with released Payment value would have been refused; the 10c-3b design did not list this reader. Approved: one exact-text patch lets the overlay also name an allocation on a Late-payment Charge of an Invoice of the Lease, as Credit of the Charge's Customer (BR-CREDIT-014). Who gets the money and the order it is used in do not change.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10c-3b-1, Refund of a Late-payment Charge paid by Payments or Credits after closure (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-LATE-005, BR-CREDIT-006, BR-CREDIT-008, BR-CREDIT-014, BR-REFUND-014, DL-032, BR-AUTH-010; owner answers to Q-2026-10-07-38 (b), Q-2026-10-07-41 answer 2, Q-2026-10-07-42 answers 1 and 2, Q-2026-10-09-66, answer 4 to Q-2026-10-09-65 and Q-2026-10-09-68; migration `20261117_113`):** the `REFUND` correction also fixes a wrong Late-payment Charge of an Invoice of the Settlement's Lease that Payment allocations or Credit applications paid. Staff record it under `final_settlements.prepare` with a reason, the Charge, the amount, a Refund method, a reference note and the DL-032 plan (`app.record_settlement_late_charge_refund_draft`, `SettlementCorrectionService.record_late_charge_refund`; the draft's `target_kind` is `LATE_PAYMENT_CHARGE` and its `refund` object also stores the Charge's source Invoice); only a `financial.approve` holder confirms it (`app.confirm_settlement_late_charge_refund`, `confirm_late_charge_refund`; human approver, READ COMMITTED, stale P5S02, replay). In one transaction the confirm reverses whole sources newest first until the paid part is covered (Q-41 answer 2), writes one linked Late-payment Charge correction (`REDUCTION`, or `CHARGE_REVERSAL` for the whole current obligation, the 10c-3a rule), re-posts each reversed source's unreleased rest to the same Charge, recognizes released Payment value as Credit of the Charge's Customer (BR-CREDIT-014, also when that Customer did not pay) and gives released Credit value back to its Credit, runs the shared DL-032 step (released Credit first pays its own Customer's open debt on the Lease, Q-42 answer 2) and pays one Credit Refund per released source of what is left in its Credit, at most what was released to it, to that Credit's Customer with the draft's method and note. Refused at record (and, after a change, stale at the confirm): an open Settlement (55000); a Charge not of the Settlement's Lease (P0002); a reversed Charge or one on a VOID Invoice (55000); a Charge a Deposit application paid (55000, until sub-slice 10c-3b-2); more than the current obligation (55000, "The Refund correction exceeds the Late-payment Charge"); an amount that releases no paid value (55000, the Invoice-line message: that is a `CHARGE_REDUCTION`); an inactive Refund method (55000). The Invoice-line `REFUND` confirm now refuses a draft with a `target_kind` (not found), on the replay path and the main path, and the Charge confirm refuses an Invoice-line draft. **The release record (owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-09-66, a technical choice):** `app.invoice_credit_excess_releases` is relaxed, never rebuilt: `invoice_adjustment_id` becomes nullable and a new nullable `late_payment_charge_correction_id` (FK, ON DELETE RESTRICT, indexed) is added; a release row names exactly one of the two, the reason "Invoice adjustment credit excess" with an Adjustment or "Late-payment Charge correction credit excess" with a Charge correction; every existing row keeps its Adjustment. Every money movement links to the correction: the reversals, the Charge correction, the re-posted rests and the Refunds are DB-SETTLE-005 effects, each release row names the Charge correction and the released Credit names its release row. Patched readers (exact-text patches, owner, SECURITY DEFINER, settings and grants kept): the release validator (a Charge branch: the reversed source targets that Charge with the same reason, the release at most what it applied), the Credit recognition validator (finds the Charge's Customer through the Charge correction), the relaxed Credit Refund check and the plan's Credit key (join the release rows through the Charge correction effect too), the two audit labels of re-posted allocations and Credit applications (the release row's reason), the lock function (a Charge `REFUND` draft adds its Charge to the Charge pass FOR UPDATE; its source Invoice joins the Invoice pass through the `refund` object) and, approved under Q-2026-10-09-68, the shared plan check's Credit overlay. Choices for the owner: (1) the reversals, the Charge correction, each re-post and each released Credit are stamped after the Charge's latest event (and, for a Credit's record, the Credit's latest event), so the Charge correction follows every allocation and Credit application on the Charge (its serializer; 10c-3a security note 3); (2) the source digest holds the Settlement, the Charge (current obligation, corrections, active applications, VOID state) and every source that pays it with its Credit, as the Invoice-line form; (3) the released Credit's `customer_authorization_reference` is `late-payment-charge-correction:` and the Charge correction id. Not changed: the 10c-3a `CHARGE_REDUCTION` Charge form and the one-call writer `app.correct_late_payment_charge` (refused after closure). **Known gap until sub-slice 10h, as for 10c-3a:** a one-call reversal of an allocation that paid the Charge at closure.
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-3b-1):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261117_113` (26 assertions in 14 modules and the 10c-2b-1 module's head assertion); only the expected revision changed. The new PostgreSQL module `test_settlement_late_charge_refund_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`; three unit tests in `test_settlement_correction.py` cover the new command and service. No other committed test changed its expectations: a read-only search of every backend test found that the source checks of the patched functions in other modules (the lock order of the Invoice-line `REFUND` confirm, one Payment pass and one Credit pass in the lock function, no `interval` other than the listed ones and no `age(`) still hold, every forward-only test upgrades only to its own revision, and no test reads the release table's structure.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10c-3b-2, Refund of a Deposit-paid Late-payment Charge after closure (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-008, BR-LATE-005, BR-CREDIT-014, BR-REFUND-014, DL-032, BR-AUTH-010; owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-09-66, the owner answer to Q-2026-10-07-46, Q-2026-10-07-41 answers 1 and 2, Q-2026-10-07-42 answers 1 and 2; migration `20261118_114`):** the Late-payment Charge `REFUND` correction of sub-slice 10c-3b-1 also fixes a Charge that a Deposit application paid. The release still takes whole sources newest first across Deposit applications, Payment allocations and Credit applications, only until the paid part is covered (Q-41 answer 2). A released Deposit application is reversed whole by a Deposit application reversal (sub-slice 10c-2a-1) linked to the correction, with the draft's reason, authorization `financial.approve` and the approver, dated after the Charge's latest event and the Deposit's latest balance event; its value goes back to its Deposit and never becomes Customer Credit; its unreleased rest is a plain open obligation of the Charge that only the shared DL-032 step pays again. The draft's released sources keep a Deposit application's whole amount (`application_usd_cents`) beside the released part, as the Invoice-line form, so the step's overlay gives the Deposit all of it. The confirmed audit lists the Deposit application reversals. Relaxing (the Q-46 answer for a Charge, owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-09-66): the 8a-1 refusal of a Late-payment Charge correction while a Deposit application on the Charge stands (P4603) lets through only a Charge correction linked to a correction of its Charge's own closed Settlement (the Settlement of the Lease of the Charge's source Invoice), confirmed in the same transaction by that correction's approver under `financial.approve` (internal `app.settlement_correction_charge_correction_allowed`, the twin of `app.settlement_correction_adjustment_allowed`); every unlinked Charge correction stays refused (after closure the 8e-1 refusal answers first, P5S01). No new check was needed for "no Charge paid more than its new amount": the Charge correction row's own deferred check refuses at commit a result below the Charge's active applications, Deposit applications not reversed included (a test shows it on the linked path). Choices for the owner: (1) as for an Invoice Adjustment (choice (7) of sub-slice 10c-2a-3), the exception does not check the Charge correction kind or the correction variant; only the Charge `REFUND` confirm reaches it today (the 10c-3a `CHARGE_REDUCTION` Charge form still refuses a Deposit-paid Charge, and the one-call writer `app.correct_late_payment_charge` is refused after closure), and each later correction kind states in its own design whether it relies on it; (2) a Refund of a Deposit-paid Charge whose plan leaves the returned Deposit money unused is refused at record (BR-SETTLE-008, "Every remaining Deposit balance must be applied or refunded"), and no Deposit Refund is written while the Lease still has open debt the Deposit could pay; (3) a correction may end with no Refund at all when the returned Deposit money pays open debt; (4, security review round 1) as choice (2) of sub-slice 10c-2a-3, a Deposit application is never split: it is reversed whole and its unreleased rest is a plain open obligation of the Charge for the step, and under Credit before Deposits (owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-07-42) the Charge Customer's available Credit pays that rest first, so a correction may refund more of a Deposit than it released (the mixed-Charge test releases 1,000 of a 3,000 Pet Deposit application; the released Payment Credit pays the Charge's 2,000 rest and the key card, and the Pet Deposit's 3,000 are refunded); every write stays under `financial.approve`, the Deposit Refund recipient passes BR-REFUND-015, each Credit Refund goes to its Credit's Customer, and Deposit value never becomes Customer Credit. Not changed: the 10c-3a `CHARGE_REDUCTION` Charge form and the one-call writer; so (review round 3), as for an Invoice line (choice (4) of sub-slice 10c-2a-3), the unpaid rest of a Charge that a Deposit application partly paid cannot be reduced on its own after closure (the reduction refuses a Deposit-paid Charge and a `REFUND` must release paid value); a `REFUND` whose amount covers that rest and some paid value removes both. **Known gap until sub-slice 10h, as for 10c-3a and 10c-3b-1:** a one-call reversal of an allocation that paid the Charge at closure.
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-3b-2):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261118_114` (26 assertions in 14 modules and the 10c-2b-1 module's head assertion); only the expected revision changed. Changed committed test (one expectation, test name kept, a short comment; run rule for historical tests): `test_the_refund_is_refused_for_unpaid_too_large_deposit_paid_or_other_lease_charges` in `test_settlement_late_charge_refund_migration.py` expected the 10c-3b-1 refusal of a Deposit-paid Charge (55000, "A Deposit application paid this obligation at Final Settlement"), which its design said lasts until sub-slice 10c-3b-2; the Deposit application is now released, the Pet Deposit holds the money again, and the same draft with an empty plan is refused by the shared step (55000, "Every remaining Deposit balance must be applied or refunded", BR-SETTLE-008); the test's docstring, the module docstring and its traceability note say so (text). The new PostgreSQL module `test_settlement_late_charge_deposit_refund_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`. No other committed test changed its expectations: a read-only search of every backend test found the two tests that expect P4603 for a Charge correction on a Deposit-paid Charge still true (`test_deposit_application_reversal_migration.py` writes the linked row with an actor who is not the correction's approver; `test_final_settlement_migration.py` writes an unlinked row), the 10c-3a test of a Deposit-paid Charge reduction unchanged (the form is unchanged), the 10c-3b-1 head-level source checks of the patched confirm and plan still true (no `age(`, no interval other than one microsecond), and every forward-only test upgrading only to its own revision.
- **Owner answers to the sub-slice 10c-3b-2 report (2026-10-10; Q-2026-10-10-69):** (1) the reading of the standing round-3 rule is right: P2 ran after the last code and test change and only text changed after it, so P2 counts as the full PostgreSQL run before round 3; (2) choice (4) of the 10c-3b-2 entry is accepted: a partly released Deposit application is reversed whole and the Customer's own Credit pays the rest first (Credit before Deposits); (3) the optional allow-function hardening (the correction's draft names the obligation, the row's correlation is the correction) stays on the slice 12 list, and any sub-slice that adds a new writer that links an Adjustment or a Late-payment Charge correction must do this hardening first; (4) the owner asked whether staff can still reduce the unpaid rest of a partly Deposit-paid Charge after closure: no path reduces only that rest (both `CHARGE_REDUCTION` forms refuse a Deposit-paid obligation; a `REFUND` must release at least 1 cent of paid value, which then leaves the obligation for good), written as Q-2026-10-10-70 with recommendation (a), a relaxing reduction within the unpaid rest after the hardening, for the owner to answer before 10h; (5) the changed 10c-3b-1 test `test_the_refund_is_refused_for_unpaid_too_large_deposit_paid_or_other_lease_charges` was compared read-only with its version at 0139619 (the same as HEAD 2b5f7c9, which the round-1 reviewer compared): one expected message, a comment and two docstrings changed, every other assertion kept; not weakened (recorded in the 10c-3b-2 review record); (6) the 10d design is accepted with its three points, and the plan's 10h entry closes the 6b forfeiture reversal draft after closure.
- **Owner answers of 2026-10-10 in the session, before sub-slice 10d code (Q-2026-10-10-71):** (1) no test fixture has a kept Deposit on a Lease with a second signed Signer, so 10d checks the recipient rules on the real kept-Deposit path only by refusals (a recipient who is not a Signer; an agreement for the Primary Tenant); the positive agreed-Signer Deposit Refund stays covered by the shared-step test on `two_signer_case`, and a positive agreed-Signer Refund test on a kept Deposit, with a two-Signer fixture, joins the plan's real-path task before 10h; (2) the 10d recorder and confirm refuse (55000) a reversal while another forfeiture of the same Deposit stays active, because the reversal would not put the Deposit on the DL-032 path; decided with Q-2026-10-09-61 before 10h.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10d, a forfeiture reversal after closure (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-DEP-019, BR-DEP-022, BR-DEP-023, BR-DEP-026, DL-032, BR-REFUND-014, BR-REFUND-015, BR-CREDIT-006, BR-AUTH-010; owner answer 4 to Q-2026-10-06-36, answer 1 to Q-2026-10-06-37, answers 1 and 3 to Q-2026-10-07-41, answers 1 to 3 to Q-2026-10-07-42, Q-2026-10-05-24, Q-2026-10-10-71; migration `20261119_115`):** the post-settlement correction variant `FORFEITURE_REVERSAL` reverses a confirmed Lease Deposit forfeiture of a Deposit of the Settlement's Lease once the Settlement is closed. Staff record it under `final_settlements.prepare` with a reason, the forfeiture, an evidence reference, the correction's Refund method and reference note and the DL-032 plan (`app.record_settlement_forfeiture_reversal_draft`, `SettlementCorrectionService.record_forfeiture_reversal`); only a `financial.approve` holder confirms it (`app.confirm_settlement_forfeiture_reversal`, `confirm_forfeiture_reversal`; human approver, READ COMMITTED, stale P5S02 with the draft left pending, replay). In one transaction the confirm takes the 8e-3 locks (the draft lock, the Settlement's draft lock, the Lease row, the shared lock function with every Deposit of the Lease, then the forfeiture row), checks the source digest and the recorder's check again, takes the Refund methods FOR SHARE, writes the decision and the header, links and writes the forfeiture reversal through the existing reversal function (the approver as actor, `deposits.correct`, the draft's reason and evidence reference, the command record keyed by the draft and the `deposit.disposition.reversed` audit event, as the 6b confirm writes it), and runs the shared DL-032 step on the real state: each Customer's Credit first pays that Customer's own open debt (Q-42 answer 1), the Deposit pays the Lease's open debt in the DL-032 order and only the rest is refunded to the Primary Tenant or an agreed signed Signer (BR-SETTLE-008, BR-REFUND-015); a correction may end with no Refund when the debt takes all of it, and the debt left stays on its Customer's ledger (BR-SETTLE-016). The recorder checks the plan with the forfeited amount added to the Deposit whatever its path; at the confirm the reversal puts the Deposit on the DL-032 path (Q-24) before the step checks the real state against the recorded result. Refused at record: an open Settlement (55000); a forfeiture that is not one of a Deposit of the Settlement's Lease, a Reservation forfeiture or an unknown one (P0002, "Lease Deposit forfeiture was not found"); a forfeiture already reversed (55000); another forfeiture of the same Deposit that stays active (55000, owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-10-71); an inactive Refund method (55000); a Deposit Refund step with another method or note (22023); a waiting draft on a Settlement source, such as a 6b reversal draft of the forfeiture (55000, the shared check); the plan rules of the shared check. The source digest holds the Settlement, the forfeiture and its reversals, the Deposit (held value, latest balance event, path, the other active forfeitures) and the Deposit's waiting drafts other than correction drafts; so a 6b draft recorded or confirmed after the correction draft makes the correction draft stale at its confirm (P5S02, not the record-time 55000), and a change of debt or Credit makes it stale through the shared step's comparison. Choices for the owner: (1) the reversal row and its audit name the approver and `deposits.correct`, as the 6b confirm does (slice 12 owner item on approver naming); the authority is the linked correction (`final_settlements.prepare` prepares, `financial.approve` confirms) and the correction's audit evidence names `financial.approve`; (2) the reversal row and its audit event are stamped with the confirm's statement time, so they are dated just before the correction's decision and header that link them, as in 6b; reports read them through the link; the reversal audit's resulting state is the Deposit snapshot before the step applies or refunds the money; (3) no new table, column or patch: the existing reversal function, link and shared step serve unchanged. Design test items not built as written: the positive Deposit Refund to another signed Signer with the written agreement (no fixture has a kept Deposit on a Lease with a second signed Signer; owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-10-71: refusals only now, the positive test in the plan's real-path task before 10h); a pending forfeiture draft of the Deposit (a forfeiture draft cannot be recorded after closure since sub-slice 10c-2b-2a; a pending 6b reversal draft is tested). **Known gap until sub-slice 10h:** the 6b draft still reverses a closed Settlement's forfeiture without the step; 10h closes it (owner answer 6 to Q-2026-10-10-69).
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10d):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261119_115` (26 assertions in 14 modules and the 10c-2b-1 module's head assertion); only the expected revision changed. The new PostgreSQL module `test_settlement_forfeiture_reversal_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`; three unit tests in `test_settlement_correction.py` cover the new command and service. No other committed test changed: a read-only search of every backend test found no other test that lists the app functions, their grants or the correction variants at head, and every forward-only test upgrades only to its own revision.
- **Owner answers of 2026-10-10 to Q-2026-10-08-53, Q-2026-10-08-57, Q-2026-10-09-61 and Q-2026-10-10-70 (before V1-056 sub-slice 10e):** (1) Q-53, recommendation (a): a small relaxing sub-slice (10c-2a-7) lets the pet classification correction (`PET_DAMAGE_RECLASSIFICATION`) accept a Damage line billed from a finding of a move-out inspection of the Lease that was superseded before closure; it allows only the change to pet damage. (2) Q-57, recommendation (a): a `RETURN_REVERSAL` correction (10e-3) undoes what a wrong return paid (the earlier correction's Credit applications and Deposit applications paid from the returned value are reversed as linked records) and reverses the return (a linked `CORRECTION_REVERSAL` row); any part already refunded again becomes an `EXTRA_CHARGE` to that Customer in the same correction. (3) Q-61, option pay back, before and after closure (10c-2b-5): the returned money of a Pet Refund-review Refund had already left the Pet Deposit before the termination, so it is the Customer's money and is not kept under BR-DEP-022. Before closure the Final Settlement treats the returned amount as DL-032 value (no forfeiture is required for it; it first pays the Lease's open debt in the DL-032 order; the rest is refunded by the Settlement's Deposit Refund step); after closure a `REFUND_RETURN` with a new Refund and the `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` form accept such a Pet Deposit for the returned amount only, the open debt first; the rest of the Deposit stays kept; no correction links a forfeiture. The owner asked whether the 10d refusal of a reversal while another forfeiture of the same Deposit stays active is still needed: it is needed until 10c-2b-5 is built and is kept afterwards as a guard (implementer note under Q-61); whether the forfeiture must leave the returned amount out is Q-2026-10-10-73. (4) Q-70, option (a): a small relaxing sub-slice lets `CHARGE_REDUCTION` (both forms) reduce an obligation that a Deposit application partly paid, within its unpaid rest; it comes after the allow-function hardening of owner answer 3 to Q-2026-10-10-69 (the correction's draft names the obligation; the row's correlation is the correction), built first in its own sub-slice (10c-3c-1, then 10c-3c-2). Order in the plan, before 10h: 10e (10e-1a, 10e-1b, 10e-1c), 10e-2, 10c-2b-5, 10e-3, 10c-2a-7, 10c-3c-1, 10c-3c-2, then 10f, 10g and the real-path tests.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-1a, a returned Payment with no closed Final Settlement relied on (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-PAY-042, BR-CREDIT-014, BR-AUTH-010; DB-PAY-006, DB-PAY-009; owner answers 1 and 5 to Q-2026-10-06-36, answers 2, 3 and 5 to Q-2026-10-06-37; migration `20261120_116`):** a returned Payment (a bounced transfer) is a draft of the new kind `PAYMENT_RETURN`: a `payments.reverse` holder records it with a reason and an evidence reference (`app.record_payment_return_draft`, `PaymentReturnService.record`); only a `financial.approve` holder confirms it (`app.confirm_payment_return`, `PaymentReturnService.confirm`; a person, READ COMMITTED, replay, stale P5S02 with the draft left pending). The draft lists every active allocation, every Customer Credit recognized from the Payment with its active applications and every active Deposit receipt. In one transaction the confirm writes the decision and one `app.payment_returns` row, then reverses the Credit applications, the Credits, the allocations and the Deposit receipts and then the Payment; the debt comes back on its obligations and stays with their Customers (BR-SETTLE-016); the Payment row and its Receipt never change. The slice 7 refusals (a moved allocation; a Credit released from a moved allocation) and the sub-slice 8g refusal (a Credit released to a Customer other than the payer) let a reversal of that Payment's record through only while its return is confirmed in the same transaction, so both lasting blocks end. Refused (55000): a record that a closed Final Settlement relied on (owner answer 5 to Q-2026-10-06-37: an allocation or Credit application on that Lease's obligations made at or before the closure, a Final Settlement Credit application, a record linked to a post-settlement correction or a Credit such a correction released, a Deposit receipt of that Lease); such a Payment is returned through the `RETURNED_PAYMENT` correction of that Settlement (sub-slice 10e-1b); a Refund paid from the Payment's value that is not fully returned and a Deposit it funded with an active forfeiture (Q-2026-10-10-72 points 1 and 2; before closure staff first reverse the forfeiture with the 6b draft) or an active Deposit application; a pending draft whose payload names the Payment or one of its records. Choices for the owner: (1) each reversal row names the approver and its table's fixed authorization string (`credits.applications.reverse`, `credits.reverse`, `payments.allocations.reverse`, `deposits.correct` for the receipt), as the correction writers do (slice 12 approver-naming item); the Payment reversal row, whose column has no fixed string, names `financial.approve`; the authority is the confirmed draft, recorded in the `payment_returns` row and the audit event; (2) a receipt reversal goes through the existing receipt reversal function and is stamped with the confirm's statement time, as the 8r confirm, so it is dated just before the return row; the result finds it by its command key; (3) a new preparer capability makes eight preparer capabilities, so the two capability-scoped draft reads (`app.financial_draft_resource_by_capability`, `app.list_financial_drafts_by_capability`) accept eight instead of seven (a relaxing exact-text patch; without it every `financial.approve` reader's draft read would be refused); (4) the Settlement preview is not changed: a Settlement may close while a `PAYMENT_RETURN` draft waits; when the closure relies on that Payment's records, the draft is then stale at its confirm (P5S02, the draft stays pending), and staff withdraw it and record the correction instead (as built after review round 1; corrected after review round 2).
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10e-1a):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261120_116` (27 assertions in 15 modules); only the expected revision changed. Two expectations of `test_lease_deposit_forfeiture.py` changed because `payments.reverse` is a new preparer capability (names kept, a comment on each): `test_capability_scoped_reads_fit_the_database_bound` now allows at most eight capabilities, and `test_draft_reads_pass_only_the_prepare_capabilities_the_reader_holds` expects `payments.reverse` among a `financial.approve` reader's capabilities. The new PostgreSQL module `test_payment_return_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`; six unit tests in `test_payment_return.py` cover the command and the service. No other committed test changed: a read-only search of every backend test found no other test that lists the preparer capabilities, the draft kinds, the app functions or their grants at head, and every forward-only test upgrades only to its own revision. (Correction: the search missed the full capability list in the second test above, written with double quotes; the first unit run found it.)
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-1a after review round 1 (independent and security; record ops/reviews/V1-056-slice-10e-1a-payment-return.md):** (1) the relied-on rule also covers a Credit that a Final Settlement or a correction used when that use was later reversed or released (a Settlement Credit application, an application or its reversal or release linked to a correction, a Refund of the Credit or a Refund correction linked to a correction), so a Credit whose value a correction left held is not reversed without a correction of that Settlement; (2) the recorder runs only at READ COMMITTED (55000), as the receipt reversal and forfeiture recorders; (3) the confirm locks only the targets of active records, the Late-payment Charges before the Invoices (as the Settlement confirm and the correction lock function); (4) the moved-Credit pass is keyed on the Credit's own source Payment; (5) a draft recorded before a closure that relies on its records is stale at its confirm (P5S02, the draft stays pending), not 55000 as the design said; (6) known limit: a draft over the 16,000-byte draft limit is refused (22023). Not changed, for later slices: the generic draft reads still show a confirmed `PAYMENT_RETURN` draft's decision `transaction_id` (11c strips it, as 11e for correction drafts); every 55000 reaches staff as one conflict message and the draft reason is copied onto each affected Customer's ledger (11c: stable reason codes, screen guidance); the pending-draft check matches ids in the payload text, the wrong-Unit pass relies on every writer setting its constraints immediate, the result finds reversals by time and actor, and the unused kind-only draft reads still accept five kinds (slice 12 items). Tests added: the owner's third-party case with the Invoice Customer's Credit applied to another of its Invoices; a Payment whose only record is an allocation made before the closure, with each relied-on kind read; a draft made stale by a closure; a Late-payment Charge allocation; a Payment whose records a REFUND correction released and re-posted; each refusal of the source state; a service identity cannot confirm (P4506); the recorder outside READ COMMITTED; a return row whose Payment is not reversed is refused at commit (23514).
- **Owner answers of 2026-10-10 to Q-2026-10-10-74, Q-2026-10-10-73, Q-2026-10-10-72 point 1 and the sub-slice 10e-1a report:** (1) Q-74, option (a), an owner exception as for Q-2026-10-05-31 and Q-2026-10-07-43: the three round-2 tests are added, the small lock-order change is made and the text is fixed; then the static checks, the migration-only check, one full backend unit run and one full PostgreSQL run, then review round 3; 10e-1a is committed only if the runs are green and round 3 has no blocker and no should-fix; if round 3 finds anything, the work stops for the owner. (2) `payments.reverse` as the eighth preparer capability is accepted; the bound of the two capability-scoped draft reads may go from 7 to 8; the two changed expectations in `test_lease_deposit_forfeiture.py` keep their names, and the independent reviewer compares them with HEAD and confirms that only the capability count changed. (3) The 10d refusal of a forfeiture reversal while another forfeiture of the same Deposit stays active is kept as a guard (implementer note under Q-2026-10-09-61). (4) Q-73, option (a): under pay back, a Lease Deposit forfeiture leaves out the returned amount of a pre-termination Pet Refund-review Refund that the Deposit still holds, because that money is the Customer's (built in sub-slice 10c-2b-5, an approved tightening of the forfeiture draft). (5) Q-72 point 1, a Refund already paid out from the bounced Payment's value: the Customer owes that amount back as an `EXTRA_CHARGE`, the same rule as the answer to Q-2026-10-08-57; it uses the new Refund correction kind of option (a) (a linked row that gives the unreturned amount back to the Refund's source on paper, with a linked charge of the same amount in the same transaction), so that no Deposit, Credit or Payment shows a negative balance (sub-slice 10e-1c). (6) Q-72 points 2 and 3 are not decided yet; after 10e-1a is committed each case is written for the owner in simple words, with a small example in dollars, the options and a recommendation. 10e-1b and 10e-1c wait for those answers; the work continues with the next sub-slices before 10h that Q-72 does not block, starting with 10c-2b-5 (owner instruction; it comes before 10e-2, which reuses the 10e-1c charge).
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-1a after review round 2 (owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-10-74; record ops/reviews/V1-056-slice-10e-1a-payment-return.md):** three tests added: a Credit applied before the real closure, released whole by a `REFUND` correction and brought back whole by a return-only `REFUND_RETURN`, keeps its Payment relied on only through the review-round-1 Credit records, and nothing else would refuse its return (independent SF-1); the recorder outside READ COMMITTED is refused by its own guard and message on a Payment with no waiting draft (independent SF-2; the round-1 test passed through the waiting-draft refusal too, so the round-1 claim was not proven until now); in one financial-role transaction that confirms the return of one Payment, the slice 7 and sub-slice 8g refusals still hold for other Payments (security S1). Lock-order change (independent N-1): the confirm locks the targets of the active records in two passes, as the Final Settlement confirm: every Late-payment Charge in id order, then every Invoice in id order (the Invoice targets and the Charges' source Invoices together), so two returns no longer lock a Charge's Invoice out of order. Text: choice (4) above now says stale (P5S02), the refusal list names the active Deposit application, the migration docstring names the round-1 Credit cases, and the round-1 record counts six Credit branches, not five.
- **Owner answers of 2026-10-10 (fresh session) to Q-2026-10-10-72 points 2 and 3, the point 1 detail, and Q-2026-10-10-75:** (1) Q-72 point 2, option (a): a kept (forfeited) Deposit paid by a Payment that later bounces is fixed after closure by one `RETURNED_PAYMENT` correction that reverses the forfeiture (a linked record through the existing reversal function, as sub-slice 10d), then the Deposit receipt, then the Payment (sub-slice 10e-1b). The Customer does not owe the Deposit amount, because no rule makes an unpaid Deposit a debt; no new charge rule is added. (2) Q-72 point 3, option (a): a Payment whose records two closed Final Settlements relied on is refused (55000), both as a `PAYMENT_RETURN` draft and as a `RETURNED_PAYMENT` correction of either Settlement, with a test. Known limit: such a Payment cannot be returned in the system; staff record the loss outside the system. A multi-Settlement correction (one draft that writes one correction per Settlement in one confirm, a change to DB-SETTLE-003 and the 10a header rules) is in the plan as possible later work. (3) Q-72 point 1 detail, confirmed: with no closed Final Settlement, the charge for a Refund already paid out from the returned value is an ordinary one-time Miscellaneous charge on the Lease the money came from, issued in the same confirm; a returned Payment whose Refund has no Lease to charge on is refused (a known limit); `EXTRA_CHARGE` is used only inside a correction of a closed Settlement (sub-slice 10e-1c). (4) Q-75 point 1, option (A): under pay back, the returned money is DL-032 money everywhere, before and after closure; any correction that runs the shared DL-032 step must apply or refund it (BR-SETTLE-008). (5) Q-75 point 2, option (b): any returned Refund that was paid before the termination is paid back, also when it came back before the termination; a forfeiture leaves it out (the Q-73 answer). BR-DEP-022 ("non-refundable according to the signed Lease") keeps what the Deposit held at the termination, read as not including returned Refund money.
