Implementer note: a Pet Refund-review Refund may be paid again after a return while the Lease is signed, so answer (b) needs a rule for which money such a later Refund pays (Q-2026-10-10-76, open; sub-slice 10c-2b-5 waits for it). (6) Standing instruction: when one sub-slice waits for an owner answer, the next sub-slice that does not depend on that answer may be built without asking; the report says so.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-1b, a returned Payment that a closed Final Settlement relied on (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-PAY-042, BR-CREDIT-014, BR-DEP-023, BR-AUTH-010; DB-PAY-009, DB-SETTLE-003, DB-SETTLE-005, DB-DEP-006; owner answers 1 and 5 to Q-2026-10-06-36, answers 2, 3 and 5 to Q-2026-10-06-37, answers (a) to Q-2026-10-10-72 points 2 and 3; migration `20261121_117`):** a bounced Payment whose records a closed Final Settlement relied on is returned by the `RETURNED_PAYMENT` correction of that Settlement: a `final_settlements.prepare` holder records it with a reason and an evidence reference (`app.record_settlement_returned_payment_draft`, `SettlementCorrectionService.record_returned_payment`); only a `financial.approve` holder confirms it (`app.confirm_settlement_returned_payment`, `confirm_returned_payment`; a person, READ COMMITTED, replay, stale P5S02 with the draft left pending). In one transaction the confirm writes the decision, the correction header and the `payment_returns` row naming the correction, then, each linked to the correction before it is written: the reversals of the Payment's Credit applications (a Final Settlement or correction Credit application passes the 8e-2 refusals only by that link), its Credits and its allocations, the reversals of the forfeitures, Deposit applications and receipts of every Deposit it funded, and the Payment reversal. The debt comes back on its obligations and stays with their Customers (BR-SETTLE-016); the Settlement row and its closure snapshots never change. Each funded Deposit ends empty, so there is no DL-032 step; a kept Deposit's forfeiture is reversed in the same correction and no charge is made for the Deposit amount (Q-72 point 2). Refused (55000): a Payment that is not RECEIVED; a Payment this Settlement did not rely on (the correction of the closed Settlement that did, or else the `PAYMENT_RETURN` draft, returns it; corrected after review round 1); a record that another closed Final Settlement also relied on; a Refund paid from the Payment's value that is not fully returned (sub-slice 10e-1c); a funded Deposit's forfeiture governed by another Lease; a funded Deposit's application of another Final Settlement; a funded Deposit that would still hold money after the return; a waiting draft that names the Payment or one of its records. **Known limit (owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-10-72 point 3):** a Payment whose records two closed Final Settlements relied on cannot be returned in the system: a correction of either Settlement and the `PAYMENT_RETURN` draft refuse it (55000), and staff record the loss outside the system; a multi-Settlement correction is in the plan as possible later work. Choices for the owner: (1) the receipt reversal is written by the correction confirm itself with the same `deposits.correct`, command record and `deposit.receipt.reversed` audit event as the receipt reversal function, because that function stamps the statement time, which would date the receipt reversal before the Deposit application reversals and make the Deposit's history negative (refused); the forfeiture reversal still goes through the existing function (statement time, before the application reversals); (2) the write steps are an internal helper (`app.post_settlement_returned_payment`) for sub-slice 10e-1c to reuse (10e-2 keeps the Payment, so it needs the helper split first; corrected after review round 1); the helper runs no refusal itself, so every caller runs the source and refusal check under the same locks first; the sub-slice 10e-1a confirm is not changed; (3) the lock order is the draft lock, then the Payment's return lock (as the 10e-1a recorder and confirm), then the 8e-3 order (the Settlement's draft lock, the Lease row, the correction lock function, which gains a `RETURNED_PAYMENT` branch and also locks the targets and Deposits that branch adds outside the Lease), then the funded Deposits' forfeitures; (4) relaxing exact-text patch: `app.validate_payment_return` accepts a `RETURNED_PAYMENT` correction draft with the correction it confirms (a `PAYMENT_RETURN` draft still takes none); (5) the `PAYMENT_RETURN` draft's refusal of a relied-on Payment keeps its message ("return it through a correction of that Final Settlement") also when two Settlements relied on it; the correction then refuses with the known-limit message.
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10e-1b):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261121_117` (27 assertions in 15 modules); only the expected revision changed. The new PostgreSQL module `test_settlement_returned_payment_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`; three unit tests in `test_settlement_correction.py` cover the command and the service. No other committed test changed: a read-only search of every backend test found that the tests reading the correction lock function's source count its single Payment pass, its single Credit pass and its intervals, which the patch keeps, and that every forward-only test upgrades only to its own revision.
- **Owner answer of 2026-10-10 to Q-2026-10-10-77 (sub-slice 10e-1b review round 1, independent SF-3):** option (a). The written option (a) of Q-2026-10-10-72 point 2 gave "a returned Pet Refund-review Refund" as an example of a Deposit that would still hold money after the return; that example does not happen, because a Refund paid and then fully returned cancels out and the Deposit holds exactly the receipt again, so the receipt reversal leaves it at 0. The correction keeps the amount check: a funded Deposit is refused only when its held value plus its active Deposit applications and forfeitures differs from the Payment's active receipts in it (for example a second receipt of another Payment). A test covers a funded Deposit whose Settlement Deposit Refund came back in full.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-1b after review round 1 (independent and security; record ops/reviews/V1-056-slice-10e-1b-returned-payment.md):** (1) independent SF-1: a Payment that only another closed Settlement relied on, recorded under this Settlement, is refused with "return it through a correction of the Final Settlement that did"; the known-limit message comes only when both Settlements relied on it (tested); (2) independent SF-2: the refusals of a funded Deposit's forfeiture governed by another Lease and of its Deposit application of another Settlement are tested on the real source with one field changed; (3) independent SF-3: Q-2026-10-10-77 above; (4) the confirm's forfeiture lock pass leaves out reversed receipts, as the lock function and the helper do; (5) text: the forfeiture reversal keeps the reversal function's statement time, so it is dated before the correction header (as in sub-slice 10d) and before the Deposit application reversals; every other reversal gets a strictly increasing instant and the Payment reversal is last (tested); the refusal lists and rule IDs are complete; the helper serves 10e-1c, while 10e-2 needs it split. Answered in the record, not changed: the sub-slice 10e-1a security note on the moved-allocation relaxation (revisited: no wider exposure, because the helper reverses only the returned Payment's records and the confirm sets its constraints immediate); the lock function reads the targets before its Payment pass (a change in between changes the source digest, so the draft is stale); the database trusts the actor and authorization it is given (the existing boundary). Tests added: a service identity cannot confirm (P4506); a record on another, open Lease is locked, reversed and linked. Slice 12 items: optional hardening of the three relaxed refusals for a correction's `payment_returns` row; optional refusal check inside the helper; a test of a correction's own Credit application of the Payment's Credit passing the 8e-2 refusal by the link; the lock function's `OR ... = ANY(...)` passes may stop using an index (rows unchanged).
- **Owner answers of 2026-10-11 to the sub-slice 10e-1b report and Q-2026-10-11-78:** (1) 10e-1b is accepted with its three design choices: the correction writes its own receipt reversal, so the Deposit history never goes below zero; the sub-slice 10e-1a confirm stays unchanged; the forfeiture reversal is dated before the correction header, as in 10d. The next report confirms that the correction's own receipt reversal makes the same checks as the receipt reversal function (permission, amount and state) and names the test that proves it; a missing check is added in the next sub-slice that touches that code, or put on the slice 12 list if it is only hardening. (2) Q-78, the three recommendations, under one plain rule: the person who received the refund money pays it back. A: for a Credit Refund, the charge is on the Lease where the money was used. B: when there is no Lease, or the person is not a Customer of that Lease, the charge is a one-time Miscellaneous charge with no Lease; nothing is refused (this replaces the "refused" detail of the owner answer to Q-2026-10-10-72 point 1). C: when a second Signer received the money, that Signer is charged directly. Limits: only existing charge types and records; a new Invoice type, a new charge rule or a new Customer record for a person who is not yet a Customer needs the owner first; the charge is the exact amount paid out, with no fee; inside a closed-Settlement correction it is an `EXTRA_CHARGE` in the draft, confirmed with `financial.approve`; an unpaid charge stays open (no write-off). (3) Next work: 10e-1c, then 10e-2; before 10e-2 the shared helper is split with the 10e-1a tests unchanged (a committed test that must change stops the work for the owner).
- **Owner answers of 2026-10-11 to Q-2026-10-11-79, Q-2026-10-11-80, Q-2026-10-10-76 and the 10e-1b receipt reversal check:** (1) Q-79, option (a): ending the "Refund not fully returned" refusal of a returned Payment follows from the Q-78 answer. Sub-slice 10e-1c changes only the three committed assertions that check that refusal (10e-1a `test_each_refusal_of_the_source_state_is_checked` and `test_refusals_stale_drafts_withdrawal_and_reads`; 10e-1b `test_refusals_stale_drafts_and_a_deposit_that_would_still_hold_money`); each changed assertion checks the new result exactly: who is charged, on which Lease or with no Lease, and the amount. (2) Q-80, option (a): ending the two refusals follows from the Q-53 and Q-70 answers. Sub-slice 10c-2a-7 changes only the expectation of `test_a_line_billed_from_an_inspection_superseded_before_closure_is_not_eligible` and sub-slice 10c-3c-2 only that of `test_an_invoice_paid_by_a_deposit_application_takes_no_charge_reduction_yet`; tests that show the limits still hold stay (a correction that is not to-pet on such a line is still refused; a reduction larger than the unpaid rest is still refused). (3) For Q-79 and Q-80: each changed test keeps its name unless the name would then say the wrong thing; then it is renamed and its ops/traceability.json entry is updated in the same commit; the independent reviewer compares each changed test with HEAD and confirms nothing else got weaker. (4) Q-76, option (a): a new Pet Refund-review Refund pays the returned money first, then the Deposit's own money, so a bank return changes nothing for either side (in the Q-76 example the Customer ends with $300 and the termination keeps $200); the 10c-2b-5 pay-back value is that running count. (5) The 10e-1b receipt reversal check is accepted: the correction's own receipt reversal passes the shared BEFORE INSERT check (`app.validate_deposit_receipt_reversal`) as the receipt reversal function does; 10e-1c adds the explicit "Deposit belongs to this Property" check to the helper and a test that this direct path is refused in a bad state (moved forward from slice 12, because it is cheap and on a money path). (6) New standing rule: when an owner answer ends a refusal and a committed test asserts that refusal, the implementer may change that assertion to the new expected result without asking; the report names each such test and the independent reviewer compares it with HEAD; any other change to a committed test still needs the owner (also in ops/V1_055_RUN_RULES.md).
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-1c, a Refund already paid from a returned Payment's value (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-PAY-042, BR-CREDIT-014, BR-REFUND-015, BR-AUTH-010; DB-REFUND-002 amended, DB-PAY-009, DB-SETTLE-005, DB-DEP-007; owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-10-72 point 1 and its detail, answers A, B and C to Q-2026-10-11-78, answer (a) to Q-2026-10-11-79, the owner check of 2026-10-11 on the correction's own receipt reversal; migration `20261122_118`):** a bounced Payment whose value paid a Refund that is not fully returned (a Refund of the Payment, of one of its Credits or of a Deposit it funded) is now returned. The `PAYMENT_RETURN` confirm (no closed Settlement relied on the Payment) and the `RETURNED_PAYMENT` correction confirm write, before their reversals, one `RECOVERED_BY_CHARGE` Refund correction per such Refund (its whole net amount back to the Refund's source on paper; the Refund stays, because the money really left) and one charge of the same amount to the person who received the refund money. The charge: an `EXTRA_CHARGE` inside the correction when its Lease is the correction's own closed Settlement's Lease and the person is its signed Customer (a second Signer is charged directly, owner answer C); an ordinary Miscellaneous one-time charge on that Lease, issued in the same confirm, when the Lease has no closed Settlement and the person is its signed Customer (also a second Signer); otherwise (no Lease, or the person is not a signed Customer of it) a Miscellaneous one-time charge with no Lease (owner answer B). The Lease the money came from: a Deposit Refund, the Deposit's Lease; a Credit Refund, the Lease of the Invoice or Late-payment Charge whose release created the Credit (none when that Invoice has no Lease), else the Lease the Payment names (owner answer A); a Payment Refund, the Lease the Payment names. The charge is the exact net amount still out (a part already verified returned is not charged), with the Property's one-time charge Due Date days and no fee; an unpaid charge stays open. Refused (55000): a Reservation outcome Refund and a Refund of a Deposit two Payments funded (owner answers 1 and 2 to Q-2026-10-11-81, below); a charge on a Lease whose closed Final Settlement this return cannot correct (the `PAYMENT_RETURN` draft: any closed Settlement; a correction: another Settlement's; the Q-2026-10-10-72 point 3 known limit, and for a Payment used after closure on a Lease whose Settlement did not rely on it, until sub-slice 10e-4); a source whose Refunds out differ from its planned recoveries. Also built: the sub-slice 10b `EXTRA_CHARGE` writes moved unchanged into the shared helper `app.write_correction_one_time_charge` (its committed tests unchanged); the correction's own receipt reversal moved into the shared writer `app.write_payment_return_receipt_reversal` with the explicit "Deposit belongs to this Property" check (owner check of 2026-10-11; a test shows that direct path refused in a bad state: a Deposit of another Property P0002, a blank reason 22023, a waiting Final Settlement draft 55000, a closed Settlement's receipt without a linked correction P5S01); the `RETURNED_PAYMENT` write helper runs the correction's source, digest and refusal check itself (the sub-slice 10e-1b optional hardening). Choices for the owner: (1) the recovery charge and row are written right after the `payment_returns` row, before the reversals that need the value back, each dated after every event of its Refund and source (a Payment Refund's recovery row after its own new charge, which the Payment's chronology reads); (2) the `PAYMENT_RETURN` confirm reverses the receipt of a Deposit whose Refund it recovered through the shared writer, dated after the recovery, because the receipt reversal function stamps the statement time, which would date it before the recovery and make the Deposit's history negative; its other receipts keep the function; (3) the Refund resource and the financial action context leave the new column out, so the staff screens keep their exact key list; the return results name each charge; showing the recovery charge on the staff Refund reads is a slice 11c item; (4) drafts recorded before revision 118 are stale (P5S02), because their source gained `recoveries`; staff record them again. **Known limit:** a recovered Refund whose money later comes back cannot take a verified return (its net is 0), so that money is handled outside the system. Slice 12 items (review rounds 1 and 2): a test of a Credit released from an Invoice with no Lease being charged with no Lease; a test that a waiting draft naming only a recovered Refund counts as waiting; a test of a `LEASE` or `NO_LEASE` recovery charge linked to a `RETURNED_PAYMENT` correction (a third-party payer, or another open Lease); BR-PAY-042 mapped in the 10e-1c traceability entries (the third-party test).
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10e-1c):** the head-revision assertions now expect `20261122_118` (27 assertions in 15 modules); only the expected revision changed. The new PostgreSQL module `test_payment_return_recovery_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`. Under the owner's standing rule of 2026-10-11 (an owner answer that ends a refusal lets the implementer change the committed assertion of that refusal) and owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-11-79, three committed assertions of the ended refusal "A Refund paid from this Payment's value is not fully returned" changed, names kept: (1) 10e-1a `test_refusals_stale_drafts_withdrawal_and_reads`: the third-party Payment whose released Credit refunded 30.00 to the Invoice Customer is now returned, and the 30.00 is charged to that Customer on the Lease of the released Invoice, issued and unpaid; (2) 10e-1a `test_each_refusal_of_the_source_state_is_checked`: its three synthetic "Refund out" rows are removed from the refusal loop and replaced, on real sources (review round 1), by a Payment Refund (300, no Lease), a released Credit Refund (3,000, the Invoice Customer on that Invoice's Lease) and a Pet Refund-review Refund (15,000, the Customer on the Lease), each passing the check with its planned recovery checked exactly; the same synthetic inputs without a planned recovery are now refused with "A Refund paid from this Payment's value has no planned recovery", asserted in the new module (`test_the_cases_a_return_still_refuses`); (3) 10e-1b `test_refusals_stale_drafts_and_a_deposit_that_would_still_hold_money`: the Pet Deposit's Payment, from which the Settlement refunded 5,400, is recorded with the recovery planned for the Deposit owner on this Lease inside this correction; the confirm is checked in the new module. Every other assertion of these tests is unchanged (the independent reviewer compared each with HEAD bb97083). The Credit ledger cursor (`credit.py`) accepts the new event family `REFUND_RECOVERED_BY_CHARGE`.
- **Owner answers of 2026-10-11 to Q-2026-10-11-81 (three returned Payments that sub-slice 10e-1c still refuses):** (1) a cancelled Reservation's Deposit Refund paid from a bounced Payment: the refusal stays. **Known limit:** while such a Refund is out, the Payment cannot be returned in the system (55000, "A Reservation Deposit Refund paid from this Payment's value is not fully returned"); staff record the loss outside the system; tested (`test_the_refused_cases_on_real_sources`); recovering it through the Reservation outcome path, with a no-Lease charge to the person refunded, is in the plan as possible later work. (2) A Refund of a Deposit two Payments funded: the refusal stays as a guard (today every Deposit has one receipt); tested in the same test. (3) A Payment whose money was used on a Lease whose closed Final Settlement did not rely on it: option (c); that Settlement's `RETURNED_PAYMENT` correction takes the Payment, and the charge is an `EXTRA_CHARGE` on that Lease, the exact amount, billed to the Customer whose debt the Payment paid. It is sub-slice 10e-4 in the plan, after 10e-3. The implementer's design charges the person who received the refund money (the Q-2026-10-11-78 rule); in the owner's example that is the same Customer, but the two can differ, so Q-2026-10-11-82 asks before 10e-4 is built. Until 10e-4 the refusal stays (tested).
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-1c reviews (record ops/reviews/V1-056-slice-10e-1c-payment-return-recovery.md):** round 1 (independent: 1 blocker, 4 should-fix; security: 2 should-fix), all fixed: (1) the blocker: the staff screen parser accepts exactly 17 Refund correction keys, so the Refund resource and the financial action context leave the new column out (tested; the recovery charge on those reads is a slice 11c item); (2) the `PAYMENT_RETURN` result lists the recoveries by the charges' audit correlation (the draft), not by the Payment's receipts; (3) the `RETURNED_PAYMENT` write helper re-checks the source digest before it writes; (4) every `invoice-lease:` lock is taken in one id-ordered pass before any row lock; (5) the planned recoveries are cross-checked against the source; every closed Settlement the Refund belongs to must link the row; a Credit released from an Invoice with no Lease is charged with no Lease; a waiting draft naming a recovered Refund counts as waiting; (6) the refusals and the third-party payer are tested on real sources; the 10e-1a refusal test checks the planned recoveries on real sources. Round 2 (independent and security): no blocker and no should-fix; its notes are answered in the record or listed for slice 12 (above): hardening of the recovery check (a Deposit two Payments funded is refused only by the plan check; the charge's audit correlation not bound to the return; a closed Settlement that paid a Refund but is not the charge Lease's Settlement is refused only at insert, 23514, a combination no real source reaches today because such a Payment is relied on), negative tests that change one condition of the recovery check at a time, and a refusal when a planned Lease was not in the pre-lock pass (a deadlock risk only, not an integrity risk).
- **Owner answers of 2026-10-11 to Q-2026-10-11-82, Q-2026-10-11-83, Q-2026-10-11-84 and Q-2026-10-11-85:** (1) Q-82 (sub-slice 10e-4): keep the design (the person who received a Refund paid from the bounced money pays it back, the Q-2026-10-11-78 rule), with one addition: when no one was refunded, the Customer whose debt the Payment paid is charged; the charge is the exact amount; a test for each case. Implementer reading: the correction reverses the Payment's allocation, so the debt comes back on its obligation and stays with the Customer whose debt the Payment paid (BR-SETTLE-016), for the exact amount; no second charge is written, because it would bill the same debt twice. (2) Q-83 (sub-slice 10e-2): (a) BR-CREDIT-002 is not amended: money freed by a `DEPOSIT_RECEIPT_CORRECTION` becomes Customer Credit only with the payer's authorization, as the rule says; until then it stays unallocated with the Payment, the way an overpayment works today; (b) a new function writes the right receipt, so the correction can move the money straight to the right Deposit, or record the right amount, without a Credit step; new functions only, no tightening; (c) a Deposit the Property kept (an active forfeiture) is refused until a `FORFEITURE_REVERSAL` correction runs first; if (a) or (b) turns out much bigger than the plan expects, or conflicts with another rule, the implementer writes it in ops/V1_056_QUESTIONS.md with a recommendation and stops that sub-slice. (3) Q-84 (sub-slice 10c-2b-5), option (a): in the `pet_noncompliance=True` branch of the committed fixture `reviewed_case` only, the Settlement refunds the $30.00 that came back before the termination and the Pet Deposit forfeiture becomes $50.00 (the Q-2026-10-10-75 and Q-2026-10-10-76 answers); both amounts asserted exactly. (4) Q-85 (sub-slice 10c-2a-7), option (a): the committed test keeps its setup and gains the full DL-032 steps its correction needs; a test that a correction that is not to-pet on such a line is still refused stays.
- **Owner standing rule of 2026-10-11 (replaces the standing rule of the Q-2026-10-11-79 answer):** when an owner answer changes a result, and a committed test or fixture expects the old result, the implementer may update it to the new result without asking; only the affected case or branch changes; the report names each change; the independent reviewer compares it with HEAD and confirms nothing else got weaker; a change that removes a check, or that the answer does not cover, still needs the owner. Also in ops/V1_055_RUN_RULES.md.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2b-5, pay back returned Pet Refund-review money after a pet noncompliance termination (BR-DEP-022, BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-013, DL-032, DL-053; migration `20261123_119`; owner answer pay back to Q-2026-10-09-61, (a) to Q-2026-10-10-73, (A) and (b) to Q-2026-10-10-75, (a) to Q-2026-10-10-76, (a) to Q-2026-10-11-84; record ops/reviews/V1-056-slice-10c-2b-5-pet-deposit-payback.md):** the money of a Pet Refund-review Refund that comes back to the Pet Deposit is the Customer's, whenever the return was verified; its amount is a running count (a verified return adds, a return reversal and every later Pet Refund-review Refund take out first, never below 0), and the pay-back value is the smaller of that count and what the Pet Deposit holds (0 for a Security Deposit). A Deposit with a forfeiture ground whose held money is above 0 and all pay-back money follows DL-032, also while its forfeiture stays active, so before closure the Final Settlement and after closure every correction that runs the shared DL-032 step applies it to open debt first and refunds the rest (BR-SETTLE-008); the Settlement preview shows `payback_usd_cents`. Approved tightening (Q-73 (a)): a Lease Deposit forfeiture takes only the held money beyond the pay-back value and is refused (22023, "The Deposit holds nothing to forfeit") when nothing is beyond it. Relaxing exception: the returned-Refund correction with a new Refund, which checks the path before the return, also accepts a Pet Deposit whose held money after the return would be all pay-back money. Choices: (1) a Settlement or correction Refund paid from pay-back money and its later return change only what the Deposit holds, not the count, so in a kept Pet Deposit every cent held stays pay-back money; (2) the disposition insert check still accepts any amount up to the held value, because the one-call Lease disposition is owner-only since slice 6 and the forfeiture draft reads the source (slice 12 item: the same limit in the insert check, a further tightening for the owner); (3) the count orders events by time, a return before a Refund at the same instant. Known consequence: a second active forfeiture of a Deposit (the sub-slice 10d guard) is no longer reachable through a forfeiture draft, because money that comes back after a forfeiture is pay-back money; the 10d guard stays and is tested on a hand-built state.
- **Updated tests (V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2b-5, owner standing rule of 2026-10-11):** the committed fixture `reviewed_case(pet_noncompliance=True)` (`test_settlement_pet_refund_return_migration.py`, its `pet_noncompliance` branch only, owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-11-84): the forfeitures take 10,000 of the Security Deposit and 5,000 of the Pet Deposit (both asserted exactly) and the Settlement refunds the 3,000 returned before the termination to the Customer (asserted exactly); `test_a_returned_pet_refund_off_the_dl032_path_is_only_held` is renamed `test_a_returned_pet_refund_after_a_pet_noncompliance_termination_is_paid_back`: the return with a new Refund is accepted and refunds 2,000 (was refused, 55000), and after the return-only correction the Pet Deposit holds 2,000 on the `DL_032` path (was `KEPT`) and the net Refund is 8,000; its forfeiture after closure is still refused (P5S01); `test_held_pet_deposit_money_off_the_dl032_path_waits_for_the_owner` (`test_settlement_held_value_refund_migration.py`) is renamed `test_held_pet_deposit_money_after_a_pet_noncompliance_termination_is_paid_back`: the held-value Refund is recorded and confirmed and refunds 2,000 (was refused, 55000); the 10d fixture `twice_forfeited_case` (`test_settlement_forfeiture_reversal_migration.py`): its second forfeiture is now refused as a draft (22023, checked) and is written with the owner's one-call disposition, so the 10d guard test keeps its state and assertions. Traceability entries renamed and noted. The head-revision assertions now expect `20261123_119` (27 assertions in 15 modules); only the expected revision changed. The new module `test_settlement_pet_payback_migration.py` runs last in `scripts/database-test.sh`.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10c-2b-5 review round 1 (independent and security; record ops/reviews/V1-056-slice-10c-2b-5-pet-deposit-payback.md):** no blocker. Fixed: (1) both reviewers: the "only on the DL-032 path" refusal of the returned-Refund and held-value forms had no test left after the two committed tests changed; a new test refuses both on a kept Pet Deposit that holds its own money with pay-back money (a state built with the owner's one-call disposition) and checks the exception is false for a returned amount of 0 or none and for a Security Deposit; (2) independent: the before-and-after-closure matrix gains the kept Pet Deposit after closure with open debt and the empty Pet Deposit before closure with no debt; (3) the exception also requires the Deposit to hold money after the return (it matched an empty result before; it failed closed later); (4) the least-privilege test checks SECURITY DEFINER and `search_path` of the new functions; (5) docs/004 states the invariant the rule rests on and when `payback_usd_cents` has an effect, and amends the "any active disposition makes it KEPT" sentence. Slice 12 items: the same pay-back limit in the disposition insert check (a tightening for the owner; the 10d fixture would then need another way to build its state); the 10d guard's docstring and message ("would not put the Deposit on the DL-032 path") read partly wrong now (the guard refuses on other active forfeitures, so it still fails safe); a test of the `RECOVERED_BY_CHARGE` exclusion and of a Settlement Refund's return not counting; the invariant above is a check item in the 10e-3 and 10h pre-code designs; the comment "(Q-2026-10-09-61 is open for the others)" in migration `20261114_110` is stale (an accepted migration, not edited).

Slice 12 items: BR-DEP-022, BR-DEP-026 and BR-DEP-027 in docs/002 carry no pay-back amendment line yet (docs/002 amendment lines stop at Q-2026-10-08-49, a gap older than this slice); the pay-back exception helper does not read the forfeiture ground (equivalent today, because a Pet Deposit's Lease forfeiture is only ever BR-DEP-022, and the confirm re-checks the real state); the forfeiture source subtracts a pay-back value read at `infinity` from held money read at the statement time (equal unless a Deposit event were dated in the future, which no writer does); the Security Deposit case of the helper test fails on the Refund and Deposit mismatch rather than on the Deposit type (test strength only).
- **Owner answers of 2026-10-12 (fresh session) to Q-2026-10-11-86 and the sub-slice 10c-2b-5 report:** (1) Q-86, option (a): sub-slice 10e-2 (`DEPOSIT_RECEIPT_CORRECTION`) is built now without the new receipt: as linked records the correction reverses the Deposit's applications, recovers each Refund paid from the Deposit by a charge to the person who received it, and reverses the whole wrong receipt; the freed money stays unallocated with the Payment (the Q-2026-10-11-83 point 1 answer: it becomes Customer Credit only with the payer's authorization). The right receipt (the right Deposit or the right amount, Q-83 point 2) becomes a later sub-slice 10e-2b. **Known gap until 10e-2b:** after a `DEPOSIT_RECEIPT_CORRECTION` the money stays with the Payment instead of moving into the right Deposit or being recorded for the right amount; the gap must close before V1-056 ends (the owner's Q-2026-10-08-56 answer). (2) For 10e-2b, the three receipt rules that stand in the way (a Deposit takes only one receipt from each Payment, even after a reversal; a Security Deposit's first receipt must equal the amount its Reservation accepted; no function writes a Pet Deposit receipt today) are asked as one question, Q-2026-10-12-87; 10e-2b waits for it. (4) The slice 12 tightening from sub-slice 10c-2b-5 is approved: returned money is the Customer's, so no path may forfeit it, including the owner-only one-call disposition; the Lease Deposit disposition insert check takes the same pay-back limit as the forfeiture draft (a forfeiture of at most the held money beyond the pay-back value). It is built as a small sub-slice before 10h (placed in the plan as sub-slice 10c-2b-6, right after 10e-2). When it lands, `twice_forfeited_case` changes so its second forfeiture uses the Deposit's own money, under the owner standing rule of 2026-10-11; if that is not possible, the implementer tells the owner.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-2, a wrong Lease Deposit receipt after closure (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-CREDIT-002, BR-AUTH-010; DB-DEP-007, DB-SETTLE-005; owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-08-56, answers 1 and 3 to Q-2026-10-11-83, answer (a) to Q-2026-10-11-86, the Q-2026-10-11-78 rule; migration `20261124_120`):** a Lease Deposit receipt that is wrong while its Payment is real (meant for Rent, the wrong Deposit, the wrong amount) is fixed after closure by the `DEPOSIT_RECEIPT_CORRECTION` variant of the `SETTLEMENT_CORRECTION` draft: staff record it under `final_settlements.prepare` with a reason and an evidence reference; only `financial.approve` confirms. In one transaction, each record linked to the correction before it is written: each Refund paid from the Deposit that is not fully returned is recovered by a `RECOVERED_BY_CHARGE` row and a charge of its exact net amount to the person who received it (an `EXTRA_CHARGE` on the Lease when that person is its signed Customer, else a charge with no Lease; the charge says "paid from a corrected Deposit receipt"); the Deposit's applications are reversed, newest first, so the debt comes back on its obligations and stays with their Customers (BR-SETTLE-016); then the whole receipt is reversed and the Deposit ends empty. The freed amount stays unallocated with the Payment: it becomes Customer Credit only with the payer's authorization (BR-CREDIT-002, not amended), and staff may allocate it to the debt that came back, as any allocation after closure; there is no Payment reversal, no Credit and no DL-032 step (as `RETURNED_PAYMENT`). Refused (55000): a receipt already reversed or of a Deposit of another Lease; a Payment that is not RECEIVED (a guard); a Deposit with an active forfeiture until a `FORFEITURE_REVERSAL` correction runs (Q-83 point 3); a Deposit application of another Final Settlement (a guard); the sub-slice 10e-1c recovery refusals (a Reservation outcome Refund, a Deposit two Payments funded, a charge on a Lease whose closed Settlement this correction cannot correct); a Refund of the Deposit with no planned recovery; a Deposit that would not end empty; a waiting draft that names the receipt, the Deposit, its Payment or a recovered Refund. A receipt correction, a `PAYMENT_RETURN` and a `RETURNED_PAYMENT` draft of one Payment share the `payment-return:` lock. Relaxing changes: `app.refund_recovery_allowed` also accepts a recovery inside a `DEPOSIT_RECEIPT_CORRECTION` confirmed by the same approver in the same transaction whose receipt, not yet reversed, is in the Refund's Deposit; `app.write_payment_return_recoveries` words the charge for this variant (the sub-slice 10e-1a and 10e-1b texts unchanged); `app.lock_settlement_correction_plan` gains its branch (the receipt's Payment in the Payment pass, the targets of the Deposit's active applications FOR UPDATE); the Deposit-application reversal loop of `app.post_settlement_returned_payment` moved unchanged into the shared internal writer `app.write_correction_deposit_application_reversals`. **Known gap until sub-slice 10e-2b (Q-2026-10-12-87):** the correction does not yet write the right receipt (the right Deposit or the right amount); until then the money stays with the Payment.

No other committed test or fixture changed; the 10e-1a, 10e-1b and 10e-1c tests run unchanged after the helper split and the text change.

- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-2b-1, the right receipt in a Deposit receipt correction (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-016, DL-032, BR-AUTH-010, BR-CREDIT-002; DB-DEP-002, DB-SETTLE-005; owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-11-83, answer (a) to Q-2026-10-11-86, answers (a), (a), (a) to Q-2026-10-12-87; migration `20261126_122`):** a `DEPOSIT_RECEIPT_CORRECTION` may name one or two right receipts (distinct Deposits of the Settlement's Lease, together at most the corrected receipt) and a DL-032 plan (both or neither; neither is sub-slice 10e-2 as built). After the 10e-2 writes the confirm writes each right receipt from the same Payment's freed value, linked to the correction (DB-SETTLE-005 `deposit_receipt_id`) before it is written, with its own `deposit.receipt.recorded` Deposit audit event, then runs the shared DL-032 step: the Customer's Credit first, the Deposits in the DL-032 order, only the rest refunded, every new cent applied or refunded (BR-SETTLE-008). The recorder checks the plan with an overlay of the correction's effect (each obligation whose Deposit applications are reversed at its open amount plus those applications; each named Deposit's held value after the correction, absolute, above 0 only on the DL-032 path); the confirm-time step compares the recorded result with the real state (stale P5S02, the draft stays pending). The money the receipts do not take stays unallocated with the Payment (BR-CREDIT-002). Rule 1: the key `UNIQUE (deposit_id, payment_id)` is replaced by a check in `app.validate_deposit_receipt` (at most one active receipt of a Payment in a Deposit, 23505; a reversed receipt no longer blocks). Rule 2: a Security Deposit's first active receipt may differ from the accepted amount only when a correction of the Lease's closed Final Settlement, confirmed in the same transaction, links it; never above the required amount, which does not change. Rule 3: `app.write_correction_deposit_receipt` (internal, no grant; it writes only a receipt its correction's draft names, from that draft's Payment) is the only Pet Deposit receipt writer. Refused (55000 unless named): an unknown Deposit (P0002); a Deposit of another Lease; a kept Deposit (owner rule 3; Q-83 point 3 for a Security Deposit); a Deposit with a forfeiture ground (until sub-slice 10e-2c, Q-2026-10-12-88); more than the corrected receipt frees; a Payment that cannot fund the Deposit; another active receipt of the Payment in it; more than the required amount takes; a correction that also charges a recovered Refund on this Lease (until sub-slice 10e-2b-2); a plan that leaves new money unused. Relaxing exact-text patches: `app.validate_deposit_receipt`, `app.settlement_correction_plan_result` (the optional overlay key `deposits_after`), `app.lock_settlement_correction_plan` (a receipt correction with a plan locks its plan's targets), and the 10e-2 recorder, write helper and result; the 10e-2 input function accepts the optional receipts and plan. The 10e-2 result and its audit event now also list `new_deposit_receipts`, `credit_application_ids`, `deposit_application_ids` and `refund_ids` (empty for a correction without receipts; slice 11e item for docs/005).

- **Owner answers of 2026-10-12 (fresh session) to the two points of the sub-slice 10e-2b-1 report (recorded as Q-2026-10-12-89):** (1) A `DEPOSIT_RECEIPT_CORRECTION` that writes the same receipt again into the same Deposit (the same Payment and the same amount as the corrected receipt) is refused. A receipt correction must change the Deposit or the amount. If the receipt is right, a change to how the Deposit paid the Lease's debt belongs to another correction kind, not this one. An approved tightening; built in sub-slice 10e-2b-2, which already changes this code. (2) Pet Deposit receipts: the table must require a correction link (owner rule 3 of Q-2026-10-12-87 held by the table, not only by the single internal writer). An approved tightening; built in sub-slice 10e-2b-2, or in its own small sub-slice before 10h if it is not small. Implementer finding (read only, head `20261126_122`): point (2) is not small. No function writes a Pet Deposit receipt outside a correction, so the committed PostgreSQL fixtures give a Pet Deposit its money by a direct insert before closure (for example the Pet Deposit helper of the Final Settlement fixtures in `test_final_settlement_migration.py`, which 34 modules import; `seed_pet_deposit` in `test_deposit_api_migration.py`; `joint_qualified` in `test_refund_signer_recipient_migration.py`; the forfeiture test in `test_lease_deposit_forfeiture_migration.py`). A table rule for every Pet Deposit receipt refuses all of them, and the answer does not say how a fixture then gets Pet Deposit money (a fixture that turns the check off would weaken a test, which needs the owner). Point (2) is therefore its own sub-slice 10e-2b-3 before 10h, and Q-2026-10-12-90 asks how the fixtures get Pet Deposit money (recommendation: the table rule applies once the Lease's Final Settlement is closed).
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-2b-2, recovery charges as placeholder lines in a Deposit receipt correction's plan (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-016, DL-032, BR-AUTH-010, BR-CREDIT-008; DB-SETTLE-005, DB-DEP-002; the owner instruction of 2026-10-12 with the answers to Q-2026-10-12-88; owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-12-89; migration `20261127_123`):** a `DEPOSIT_RECEIPT_CORRECTION` with right receipts may also charge back a Refund paid from the corrected receipt's Deposit on this Lease (the 10e-1c rule: the person who received it pays it back); the 10e-2b-1 refusal of such a correction is lifted. The draft names each such recovery charge as a placeholder line (`md5('deposit-receipt-recovery:' || draft || ':' || refund)`, also computed by the service helper `deposit_receipt_recovery_line_id`) in the plan overlay's `new_obligations` with its Refund, Customer, Lease and exact amount, so the approver confirms real numbers and the plan may pay it in the DL-032 order (the Customer's Credit first, also the Credit of a joint Signer whom the preview does not name before the charge's Invoice exists). At the confirm the shared step first checks that each real recovery charge equals its placeholder exactly (Customer, Lease, amount; one placeholder per Refund and per charge; P5S02, the draft stays pending), then every step on a placeholder pays the real line (the step, its Deposit applications, the Credit step writer and the three checks that match a correction's Credit or Deposit application with its plan step). Owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-12-89 (an approved tightening): the same receipt again into the same Deposit (same Payment, same amount) is refused (55000, also at the confirm). Hardening carried from the 10e-2b-1 review: the receipt writer also requires its correction's decision confirmed in the same transaction by the same approver. No table or column changes; three new internal functions (no grant); nine functions patched by exact text.

Q-2026-10-12-92 proposes where to build it (recommendation: a sub-slice 10e-2b-4 after 10e-2b-3, before 10h); nothing is built before the owner answers.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-2c, a right receipt into a Deposit with a forfeiture ground (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-008, DL-032, BR-DEP-019, BR-DEP-022, BR-DEP-023, BR-DEP-026, BR-AUTH-010; DB-SETTLE-005, DB-DEP-002, DB-DEP-004; owner answers to Q-2026-10-12-88, point 1 (b) and point 2 (c); migration `20261128_124`):** the 10e-2b-1 refusal of a right receipt into a Deposit whose termination ground says the Property keeps it ends. A Deposit with a ground that was never forfeited: the same `DEPOSIT_RECEIPT_CORRECTION` forfeits the new receipt's money on that ground, as a record linked to the correction (new DB-SETTLE-005 link `deposit_disposition_id`); staff choose nothing. A Deposit whose forfeiture was reversed first: each right receipt carries the staff's choice `outcome` (`DL_032`, the 10e-2b path, or `FORFEIT`, forfeited again) with `outcome_reason`, and the `financial.approve` approver confirms it with the correction; any other Deposit takes no choice. The draft shows each forfeiture (`deposit_receipt_forfeitures`: Deposit, type, amount, ground rule, reason, basis) and leaves its money out of the plan; the confirm computes the forfeitures again under the locks (P5S02) and writes each, after the right receipts and before the shared DL-032 step, through the new internal writer `app.write_correction_deposit_forfeiture` (no grant; only what its draft names, only inside its correction's confirm; its own disposition context and Deposit command record; `occurred_at` after the receipt, because the slice 6 writers date a forfeiture at the statement's start, before the correction's records). Refused until the owner answers Q-2026-10-12-93: a forfeiture that would take other money than the new receipt's (a pay-back count, or other money held after the correction). The forfeiture rows carry the authorization reference `deposits.dispose` that the tables require, as the slice 6 draft confirmed by `financial.approve` does; the audit event names `financial.approve` and the correction.

It is the same rule as the owner answers to Q-2026-10-10-73 and Q-2026-10-10-76: returned money belongs to the Customer. It is built where it fits best before 10h. - **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-3, a return recorded by mistake after closure (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, DL-032, BR-REFUND-015, BR-CREDIT-008, BR-AUTH-010; DB-REFUND-002, DB-SETTLE-005; owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-08-57 and the Q-2026-10-11-78 rule; migration `20261129_125`):** a new variant `RETURN_REVERSAL` of the `SETTLEMENT_CORRECTION` draft kind (`final_settlements.prepare` records with a reason and evidence; only `financial.approve` confirms) names one return that an earlier `REFUND_RETURN` correction of the same closed Settlement recorded by mistake. In one transaction, each record linked before it is written, it undoes the uses of the returned Refund's own source (its Credit, or its Deposit) by that correction and by every `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` naming it (their Credit and Deposit applications from that source reversed, newest first, so the debt comes back and stays with its Customers), recovers each of their Refunds from that source by a charge of the exact net amount to the person who received it (the 10e-1c writer), then reverses the return (a linked `CORRECTION_REVERSAL` row). Implementer's reading of "paid from the returned value": only records paid from the returned Refund's own Credit or Deposit are undone; what those corrections paid from the Customer's other Credits or from other Deposits was real money paid to real debt and stays. Refused (55000): a return already reversed; a Refund to recover that has its own return (reverse that later return first); a returned value another path used (undo that use first); uses to undo that are more than the returned amount (a `REFUND_RETURN` with a new Refund also paid out money its source already held, found by review round 1), until the owner answers Q-2026-10-12-95 (recommendation: charge back only what the mistaken return brought back); a Refund to recover paid to someone other than the Deposit's own Customer, until the owner answers Q-2026-10-12-94 (the plan's older text says the Deposit owner pays; the Q-78 rule says the person who received the money; recommendation: the person who received it). No DL-032 step. The general Refund correction check's reversal branch now reads a Deposit Refund's source by `source_deposit_id` (the 10c-2b-2a note), so an over-reversal of any Deposit Refund is refused; before this sub-slice only the Pet Refund-review Refund could reach that branch, so no committed result changes. A reversed return leaves its earlier correction nothing, so a `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` naming it is then refused (a state no path reached before).

- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-3b, who pays back and how much in a return reversal (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-REFUND-015, DL-032; DB-REFUND-002, DB-SETTLE-005; owner answers (a) to Q-2026-10-12-94 and (a) to Q-2026-10-12-95; migration `20261130_126`):** a `RETURN_REVERSAL` now charges a Deposit Refund paid to an agreed second Signer back to that Signer (an `EXTRA_CHARGE` on the Lease when that person is its signed Customer, else a no-Lease charge). When the uses of the returned value are more than the returned amount and the Refunds paid out at least that amount, the returned amount is shared over the Refunds, the oldest first, and only that share is charged back; the rest of each Refund stays paid, and every Credit or Deposit application stays. So a `RECOVERED_BY_CHARGE` row inside a `RETURN_REVERSAL` may recover less than the Refund's net: exactly the share its draft recorded (DB-REFUND-002 amendment). Every other recovery is still the whole net. Review round 1 additions: a later recovery of a partly recovered Refund gets a numbered charge reference, so its rest can be charged back later (for example when the Payment that funded its Deposit bounces); the writer takes a share only inside a `RETURN_REVERSAL`; the recovery-amount function also requires the draft's confirmation in this transaction by the correction's approver; on the shared path, a Refund of the uses with its own return (also one returned in full) is refused. Still refused (55000) until the owner answers Q-2026-10-12-96: the Refunds paid out less than the returned amount, so the rest paid debt. Not built, owner confirmation asked in Q-2026-10-12-97: the reversal does not change the left value of an earlier return-only correction.

- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-4, a returned Payment used on a closed Lease after closure (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-PAY-042; DB-PAY-009, DB-SETTLE-005; owner answer (c) to Q-2026-10-11-81 point 3 and the owner answer to Q-2026-10-11-82; migration `20261201_127`):** the `RETURNED_PAYMENT` correction of a closed Final Settlement also returns a Payment that no closed Settlement relied on when the Payment names that Settlement's Lease or its money is used there (an active allocation, or an active application of one of its Credits, on an Invoice of the Lease or a Late-payment Charge of such an Invoice; or a Refund paid from its value that is charged back on that Lease). Its records are reversed and linked as in 10e-1b, so the debt comes back on its obligations and stays with their Customers; each Refund out is charged back to the person who received it by the unchanged 10e-1c rules (on the Lease the money came from, for a Payment Refund the Lease the Payment names, when the recipient is its signed Customer: an `EXTRA_CHARGE` when that Lease is this Settlement's; otherwise an ordinary charge on that Lease or a charge with no Lease). So a Payment that names no Lease, used on the closed Lease through an allocation, whose rest was refunded to the payer, gets a no-Lease charge (Q-2026-10-13-98 asks the owner to confirm; nothing waits). The way in through the named Lease has no time or use condition. With nothing refunded no charge is written: the reversed allocation puts the debt back on the Customer whose debt the Payment paid (implementer's reading of the Q-82 addition, stated in the Q-82 record). A Payment another closed Settlement relied on, and a recovery charged on another closed Settlement's Lease, stay refused. The `PAYMENT_RETURN` draft keeps refusing a Payment whose Refund would be charged on a closed Settlement's Lease; its message now names the returned-Payment correction (implementer's wording, from the design the owner kept; the service reports the generic conflict until slice 11c shows it; slice 12 item). Not changed: the `PAYMENT_RETURN` draft still returns a Payment whose only use on a closed Lease after closure is an allocation or Credit application with nothing refunded (no closed Settlement relied on it); closing that path would be a tightening (note for the owner; 10h decides the one-call changes after closure). Known limits (slice 12 items): a Payment used after closure on two closed Leases can be returned by either Settlement's correction, which also reverses and links the other Lease's records (nothing either Settlement relied on changes; the `PAYMENT_RETURN` draft does the same unlinked); a Payment with recoveries on two closed Settlements' Leases is refused by every correction although the `PAYMENT_RETURN` message points to one. A `RETURNED_PAYMENT` draft recorded before revision 127 is stale (P5S02; its source gained `used_on_lease`): staff reject it and record it again.

- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-3c, only the mistaken amount comes back when returned money also paid debt (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, DL-032; DB-SETTLE-005; owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-12-96; migration `20261202_128`):** a `RETURN_REVERSAL` whose uses are more than the returned amount while its Refunds paid out less than it is no longer refused. Each Refund is charged back whole; the newest Credit or Deposit applications of the uses are reversed whole until the rest of the returned amount (that amount less the Refunds' nets) is reached; the part of the last of them that was not returned money is applied again, in the same correction, to the same obligation from the same Credit or Deposit, right after the return is reversed and linked before it is written. In the owner's example ($300 returned only, then $1,700 with a new Refund; the $2,000 Credit pays a $1,500 charge and refunds $500) the $500 Refund is charged back, the $1,500 application is reversed and $300 applied again: the Customer owes $500 and $1,200 of the charge, exactly the mistaken $1,700 (the test runs it at 1/100 scale, 300 cents for $300; the rule does not depend on scale). The source names the part (`reapply`, at most one entry, empty in every other case); the recorder stores a one-step `plan` from it, which the Settlement Credit writer reads and the Deposit application check matches (internal `app.settlement_return_reversal_reapply_steps`, no grant); a Deposit part keeps the reversed application's stage and target and is numbered after the Settlement's highest application number (the design said "order"; implementer's reading). The design's fallback writer for a Credit part was not needed: the existing writer's basis rules accept the restoration (tested on charge lines). Fail closed (review round 1): the part must be a listed application of the returned Refund's own Credit or Deposit, with the same Credit or Deposit, the same obligation kind and, for a Late-payment Charge or a Deposit's line, the same target, only in that case, above 0 and below the application; the reversal brings back exactly the returned amount when the uses are more than it, never more; the confirm accepts only the plan built from its source and a payload with exactly the recorder's keys. The result and audit event name the part (`reapplied`). Known limits (the confirm refuses, no money moves): a Credit part on a Rent or Service Invoice without designation is refused (23514) if the Rent-first proposal no longer gives its line; a Deposit part is refused when the Deposit's path is no longer DL-032 or the first Pet Deposit stage's line is no longer pet damage; a Credit part is refused when the Credit's Payment is no longer received; the Deposit writer dates the part by the clock only, so a reversal row dated ahead of the clock could make it refused (not seen). A `RETURN_REVERSAL` draft recorded before revision 128 is stale (P5S02) and keeps a new draft of the same correction waiting: staff reject it and record it again. Lock order (security review note): the lock function does not lock the re-applied target's Invoice lines in its passes; the Credit writer locks them after the Invoice row, as every other writer does, so no new lock cycle. For sub-slice 10e-3d (independent review note): the re-applied application uses money an earlier return-only correction brought back but is linked to the reversal correction, so 10e-3d must count it against that earlier correction's left value.

- **Owner answers of 2026-10-13 (fresh session) to the 10e-3c report, and to the stop for a blocked command in the same session:** (1) Sub-slice 10e-3d (the Q-2026-10-12-97 answer): the read-only findings are the start of the design. Process the Settlement's corrections oldest return first. Count only uses made by corrections; ordinary Credit spending outside a correction stays the known limit of owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-09-63. Limit a reversal of an earlier return to that return's own left value. The pre-code design is written and committed first; a design choice that changes who pays or how much goes to the questions file with a recommendation and waits for the owner. (2) The Credit Refund outside a correction (the ordinary Refund draft with no link to the Settlement), which 10e-3d will not count, is named in the plan's 10h entry, because 10h decides one-call changes after closure. (3) "Report the Rent-line refusal as a conflict" (the Settlement Credit writer's 23514 refusal of a part a `RETURN_REVERSAL` applies again) moves from the slice 12 list to 11c, where the other business-state refusals become 409. (4) The blocked secret scan: the second try with the same term was the mistake; no harm came of it. Never repeat a command that a guard hook or the auto-mode checker blocked. The standard secret scan (ops/V1_055_RUN_RULES.md; the implementer's wording, approved with two changes after the later stop) searches the staged diff case-insensitively for password, passwd, secret, token, api key, private key and a URL that carries a user and a password, never with a file-name term in the pattern, checks `git status` by eye for env files, and scans every saved run output the same way. Later in the same session a read-only search for the earlier scan's form was blocked by the guard hook because of a file-name term in its grep pattern; it read no secret file; the implementer stopped without a retry, which the owner confirmed as right. Exception (owner, same day): if the guard hook blocks a scan only because of a word inside the search pattern, and the command reads only the staged diff or saved run outputs, that word may be removed and the scan run once more, with the word named in the report; anything else blocked means stop and tell the owner. (5) Review agents are told to write no files; if one does, the report says so. (6) Context is reported from the session's own token counter, as a share of the 1M-token context window (used tokens divided by 1,000,000), never from an estimate. (7) Signature tests and the clock (ops/V1_055_RUN_RULES.md): if a signature test fails with `Signature evidence is invalid`, check the clocks first as in check K1 of 10e-3c (an image already on the Mac, `--pull=never`); in step, that run may be rerun once under `caffeinate -dims`; not in step, or the rerun fails the same way, stop and tell the owner. Also: the pending rename of `test_a_right_receipt_into_a_deposit_with_a_forfeiture_ground_waits_for_10e_2c` is done the next time its file is touched. Order: 10e-3d, 10c-2a-7, 10c-3c-1, 10c-3c-2, 10f, 10g, 10e-2d, 10e-2b-3, 10e-2b-4, then 10h; a large sub-slice may be split into smaller committed ones without asking.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-3d-1, every correction's use of returned money counts (BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-CREDIT-014, DL-032; DB-SETTLE-005; owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-12-97 and its detail of 2026-10-13; migration `20261203_129`):** 10e-3d is split (owner allowed): 10e-3d-1 builds the count and the reversal limit; 10e-3d-2 (how a reversal charges money another correction paid out, and other Settlements' corrections) waits for Q-2026-10-13-100, because it changes who pays. A new internal count walks the Settlement's corrections oldest first, each with what it did at its own time; what a correction drew from a Credit or Deposit (applications, Refunds and reversed returns, and for a Deposit its receipt reversals and forfeitures, less the applications it reversed, the Refunds it recovered by a charge and, for a Deposit, its own receipts and forfeiture reversals, so it pays out the money it released first; a correction that reverses the Credit draws all the Credit still holds; the receipt, forfeiture and Credit reversal terms since review round 2) takes first from its own return into it, then from the earlier correction it names (a `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` or a `RETURN_REVERSAL`), then from the other returns, oldest first. The left values (`app.settlement_held_deposit_value`, `app.settlement_held_credit_value`) are that count's, so a sweep by a correction that does not name the earlier correction now lowers them; in the Q-97 case X1's $1,000 is used by X2's Refund, X1 has nothing left, and a held-value Refund naming X1 is refused also after X3 brings money back. Implementer's reading (not a free choice): a correction's own return is taken first, because the committed 10e-3b and 10e-3c reversal of a correction with a new Refund charges back exactly its own returned amount; taking the older return first would count the same money twice. A reversal of an earlier return takes only its own left value: a positive part of its money that another correction paid out (`swept_usd_cents`) is refused (55000, "Another correction paid out this return's money; reversing it waits for the owner") until sub-slice 10e-3d-2 builds the owner's answer (a) to Q-100; a missing part is refused as an incomplete source. Since review round 2 the source also names the earlier correction's `left_usd_cents`, and a reversal that would take more than it from the source is refused ("The returned value is no longer available; undo its other use first"), for example when a Refund of the uses came back through a later return. Before 10e-3d-1 such a reversal was refused only when the source held too little, and otherwise took money a later return brought back. Only uses made by the Settlement's corrections count; ordinary Credit spending stays the Q-2026-10-09-63 known limit. The `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` Credit cap still leaves out the confirming correction's own records (the `except` argument; committed tests unchanged). A `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` or `RETURN_REVERSAL` draft recorded before revision 129 whose source now reads differently is stale (P5S02).

Its name stays true; docstring and traceability note follow. `test_settlement_return_reversal_migration.py::test_the_database_guards_and_a_reversal_of_uses_beyond_the_return`: the last refusal's message changes the same way (X2's Refund paid out X1's money). In the same module the built sources of `test_the_shared_path_refusals_fail_closed` and `test_the_part_applied_again_fails_closed` gain `"swept_usd_cents": 0` and, since review round 2, `"left_usd_cents": 0`, the new fail-closed keys (as 10e-3c added `"reapply": []`); their cases are unchanged. No fixture changed.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-3d-1 review round 1 (independent and security; record ops/reviews/V1-056-slice-10e-3d-1-returned-value-count.md):** independent: one blocker, fixed: the first count read each correction's records as they are now (applications not reversed, Refunds less every recovery), so a `REFUND` correction that releases an application (reversed whole, its rest written again) and refunds the released part was counted as using the returned money, although it paid out only the money it released (and a later reversal of a counted application made the earlier correction's use smaller while its release stayed). The count now takes each correction's net draw at its own time: what it paid out (applications, Refunds, reversed returns) less what it brought back (its reversals of applications from the source and its recoveries by a charge), never below 0; a `RETURN_REVERSAL` names its earlier correction, so its return reversal empties that entry. Tested on the real path (an ordinary application of 1,500, then a `REFUND` correction of 500: the return keeps 1,800 left and nothing swept). Two should-fix: the order rules are now proven by a test (three returns of 300, 400 and 200; a held-value Refund naming the 200 one pays 500 of debt and refunds 200: the named 200 first, then the oldest 300, then 200 of the 400); "The Deposit holds no returned money to refund" lost its only tests, because the counted paths can no longer reach it (a counted sweep now empties the left value first): it stays as a backstop for the known limits (records the count leaves out, such as a Deposit receipt reversal by a 10e-1b or 10e-2 correction), and its Credit sibling "The Credit holds no returned value to refund" is tested after ordinary spending. Notes answered: the known limits are named in docs/004 (ordinary spending, other Settlements' corrections, receipts, receipt reversals, forfeitures and their reversals, Credit reversals); the claim that a reversal without a sweep takes at most the left value holds for the counted uses; any value other than 0 is refused. Security: no blocker and no should-fix; notes: the walk orders by the correction's time, which depends on the clock (slice 12 item: a per-Settlement monotonic order); a fractional `swept_usd_cents` fails its cast (22P02), still closed; the cost per call is acceptable (indexed lookups; corrections need an approver each). Later state (review round 2): the count also takes a correction's receipts, receipt reversals, forfeitures and their reversals and its Credit reversal, so the known limits left are ordinary Credit spending and, until 10e-3d-2, other Settlements' corrections; "The Deposit holds no returned money to refund" stays a backstop.

- **Owner answers of 2026-10-13 (fresh session) to Q-2026-10-13-103 (sub-slice 10e-3d-2, three cases the Q-100 answer does not decide):** (1) Point 1, option (a): when a mistaken return's money left the Credit or Deposit through a bounced Payment (a `RETURNED_PAYMENT` correction's Credit reversal) or a wrong receipt (a `DEPOSIT_RECEIPT_CORRECTION`'s receipt reversal), the return's reversal charges that money to the person who received the Refund the earlier correction charged back less (a second charge-back of the same Refund), so that person pays back the whole Refund in total. This is the same rule as the answers to Q-2026-10-11-78 and Q-2026-10-13-100: the person who received the money pays it back. A case that does not fit that rule is written as a question and that part is not built until the owner answers. Built in sub-slice 10e-3d-2c. (2) Point 2, option (a): the case of returned money kept by a forfeiture of a correction stays refused. Sub-slice 10e-2d should make it unreachable; every forfeiture path is checked again when 10e-2d is built, and the refusal stays as a backstop. (3) Point 3, option (a): when a return's reversal undoes a payment on another Lease (the Q-100 point 2 answer), a companion correction of that other Lease's Settlement shows the undo. It is recorded and confirmed together with the reversal, in one step, so one never exists without the other; the refusal that protects a closed Settlement does not get wider. Built in sub-slice 10e-3d-2d. Also (owner, same day): the pending rename of `test_a_right_receipt_into_a_deposit_with_a_forfeiture_ground_waits_for_10e_2c` is done the next time its file is touched. Order: 10e-3d-2a, 10e-3d-2b, 10e-3d-2c, 10e-3d-2d, 10c-2a-7, 10c-3c-1 (the hardening of owner answer 3 to Q-2026-10-10-69), 10c-3c-2, 10f, 10g, 10e-2d (owner answer to Q-2026-10-12-93), 10e-2b-3, 10e-2b-4, then 10h; a large sub-slice may be split into smaller ones that each end in a commit; a sub-slice that waits for an owner answer is passed over for the next one that does not depend on it.
- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-3d-2a, a return's reversal undoes the money another correction paid out (BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-CREDIT-014, BR-REFUND-015, DL-032; DB-SETTLE-005, DB-REFUND-002; owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-13-100 point 1; migration `20261204_130`):** the 10e-3d-1 count becomes one internal walk that the count (same results) and a new internal takes function both read. For an earlier return-only correction E, the takes are the records of each other correction S (neither E nor one naming E) that paid out part of E's money: inside S the money S brought back pays out first, then S's Refunds from the source, the oldest first, then S's applications, the newest first (the owner answers to Q-2026-10-10-76 and Q-2026-10-12-95: a new Refund pays the returned money first). E's reversal now undoes those parts too: a Refund part is charged back to the person who received that Refund (a share, the 10e-1c charge: this Lease when that person is its signed Customer, else no Lease), an application part reverses the application whole and applies the rest again to the same obligation from the same Credit or Deposit, linked (as 10e-3c, now one part per such application). Together with the own uses they undo exactly E's used money, so the reversal takes only E's left value. In the owner's example as tested (Pet Deposit, scaled to the fixture): X1 brings back 2,000; X2 brings back 500, the Customer's Credit pays 300 of a 1,500 charge, the Pet Deposit 1,200, and 1,300 is refunded; X1's reversal charges 800 to the Refund's recipient and makes 1,200 of the charge owed again. Implementer's readings (fail closed): an S that also paid out through a reversal of the Credit, a reversed return, a Deposit receipt reversal or a forfeiture is not split, and its part is refused with its own message (10e-3d-2c, or the forfeiture refusal of owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-13-103 point 2); an application reversed since waits for 10e-3d-2b; a Refund part more than the Refund's net is refused ("undo its other use first"). The own uses are undone against the part of E's money they used (E's used less its swept part). The source's parts must add up and be listed exactly, else it is incomplete. A `RETURN_REVERSAL` draft recorded before revision 130 is stale (P5S02).

- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-3d-2b-1a, a return's money on a payment a later return reversal changed (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-CREDIT-014, DL-032; DB-SETTLE-005; owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-13-100 point 1; migration `20261205_131`; design in the plan, 10e-3d entry, "10e-3d-2b pre-code design"):** a return reversal reverses a payment whole and puts back exactly the part that was not its own return's money, so another return's part of that payment is in the put-back with the same amount; that return's later reversal now follows its part there (through further put-backs too), reverses the put-back whole and puts its rest back, so the debt its money paid is owed again. New internal `app.settlement_returned_value_successor` finds the put-back from the reversing correction's stored source (the n-th `reapply` entry is the n-th application linked to it) and checks it (same source, obligation and stage, the entry's amount); any other change of the payment (a `REFUND` correction's release, a pet-damage reclassification: the new Q-2026-10-13-107) or a put-back that does not check stays refused with the 10e-3d-2b message. Implementer's reading (no new question): the put-back carries the other returns' parts unchanged because the 10e-3c and 10e-3d-2a put-backs are defined as the payment less exactly the reversed return's part.

- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-3e, a return reversal's Credit put-back goes back on its own line (BR-CREDIT-006, BR-CREDIT-008, BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016; DB-CREDIT-002; owner answers (b) to Q-2026-10-13-104 and (a) to Q-2026-10-13-106; migration `20261207_133`; design in the plan, 10e-3e entry, "10e-3e pre-code design"):** a Credit put-back of a `RETURN_REVERSAL` correction on an Invoice with Rent or Service lines without a Customer designation is written on its own line (the reversed application's) with the new label `PUT_BACK_BY_CORRECTION` on the row and its detail and no Rent-first proposal, and every put-back's detail trace names the payment it restores. The database allows the label only on a row linked to a correction of its Final Settlement confirmed in the same transaction, or on the re-posted rest of a row that has it (the release re-post of migration 67, which a later `REFUND` correction's confirm writes after closure, migration 100); the deferred check holds a return reversal's put-back to the payment the same correction reversed (same Credit, target and line, together at most its amount there) and the label to exactly that case. The sub-slice 10e-3d-2a Rent-first refusal and the sub-slice 10e-3c known limit end. Implementer's readings (no new question; they change neither who pays nor how much): the label is used only where none of the existing labels is true (an Invoice with Rent or Service lines without a designation), so a designated part keeps the Customer's designation, a Late-payment Charge `NOT_APPLICABLE` and any other Invoice `OTHER_APPLICABLE_OBLIGATION`, as before; the restored payment is read from the draft's stored source (the n-th plan step is the n-th part applied again), so no plan and no committed plan expectation changes; the re-posted rest of a put-back keeps the label, because none of the other labels is true for it either. The frontend part waits for the new Q-2026-10-14-109 (corrected after review round 1: the re-posted rest of a put-back is an ordinary `credits.apply` payment with the label, so the one-call reversal screen's label list shows a general error for it; recommendation (b), the label in that screen's lists, as sub-slice 10e-3e-2 after the answer).

- **V1-056 signature fixture clock sub-slice (owner instruction of 2026-10-14; test set-up only; TEST-019; record ops/reviews/V1-056-signature-fixture-clock.md):** cause of the `Signature evidence is invalid` failures (a full rerun in four sessions; 13 failures in one stretch at the end of 10e-3e run P3): `app.record_lease_signature` (and `app.sign_lease`) refuses a signing time later than the database's statement time with no tolerance, the Lease signature fixtures took that time from the host clock, and the database runs in the Docker VM, whose clock can lag the host clock; a lag longer than the few milliseconds between the fixture's reading and the database statement fails the signature, far below the about 80 ms resolution of the K1 clock check, so the clocks looked in step afterwards. Fix, test set-up only: `database_now()` in `backend/tests/test_lease_lifecycle_migration.py` reads `clock_timestamp()` from the database (as `test_utility_baseline_migration.py` already does); the default of `signature_command` and every Lease signing time in the database tests use it, and the lifecycle test's termination time too, because a termination may not be dated before the signing (seven modules, 22 host-clock readings: 21 signing times and 1 termination time); the concurrent signing test reads the time once before its threads start (security review round 1 note: a failed read must not leave one thread waiting at the barrier). No assertion changed; the refusals of a signing time one day ahead and one microsecond before the last signature are now measured from the database's clock. The app's signature check is unchanged. The plan's slice 12 item on the time source of the signature helpers is done; a new slice 12 item: the business date helper `reservation_business_date` still reads the host clock (it matters only within the clock lag of midnight in Asia/Phnom_Penh, and not for the signature check).

- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-3d-2b-2, a return's money on a payment a later Refund correction or pet-damage reclassification changed (BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-CREDIT-014, BR-DEP-027, DL-032; DB-SETTLE-005; owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-13-107 and the owner's acceptance of 2026-10-14; migration `20261208_134`; record ops/reviews/V1-056-slice-10e-3d-2b-2-return-reversal-changed-payment.md):** each part of a return's money keeps its place in its record (new internal `app.settlement_returned_value_placed`; `app.settlement_returned_value_parts` calls it), and a payment part is followed by the new internal `app.settlement_returned_value_follow`: through a return reversal's put-back with that return's money cut out, and over the Refunds (the oldest first) and payments (the newest first) of a `REFUND` correction or `PET_DAMAGE_RECLASSIFICATION` that reversed it, in the order that correction paid the money it took back out again; a Refund part is charged back to its recipient, a payment part makes its debt owed again; anything the function cannot follow stops open with the new message "A later correction changed a payment made with this return's money in a way this reversal cannot follow yet; reversing it waits for the owner". Implementer's readings, beyond the written design: (1) a reversed return's stretches on a payment its reversal changed are its placed parts followed up to that reversal, so a put-back of a put-back is followed (the committed chain test `test_a_part_follows_a_chain_of_put_backs` needs it; the design had said such a put-back stays open); (2) the put-back must belong to this Settlement (the rest a Refund correction re-posts carries no Settlement link, migration 100), instead of the reversed payment's Settlement; (3) the recovery scope `app.settlement_return_reversal_refund` also accepts a Refund the source lists in `own_parts` (relaxing; needed so an own part on a changing correction's Refund is charged back; the design had listed no other function); (4) the planned "a Refund correction of the 1,200 put-back, then X1b's reversal" test is built in the other order (Refund correction first), because in the planned order the Refund correction's released Credit first pays the 300 a reversal made owed again. Review round 1 hardening (fail closed): a part not inside its payment stops open, and while following a reversed return's stretches only changes by strictly earlier corrections are followed, so the recursion ends with a clean refusal.
