- **V1-056 signing time from the database clock (owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-14-110; BR-AUDIT-003, API-036, API-038, UI-038, UI-040; no migration; record ops/reviews/V1-056-signing-time-database-clock.md):** a Signer's signature time and the Lease signing time are the database's own time at the command. The two signing request bodies (`PATCH /lease-signers/{signer_id}`, `POST /leases/{lease_id}/sign`) are `event_id` and `reason` only; a body that still sends `signed_at` is refused with 422 (the models keep `extra="forbid"`). The routes pass no time; the service passes `COALESCE(%s::timestamptz, statement_timestamp())` to `app.record_lease_signature` and `app.sign_lease_with_reservation`, the same statement time the unchanged database checks compare with, so a device clock can neither refuse a signing nor make one look earlier. A retry with the same Idempotency-Key replays the first result (the database replays before it reads the time). The staff screen sends no `new Date()` time for either action. Implementer's reading (both round 1 reviewers found it acceptable): the service commands keep an optional explicit time, which no route passes, so the committed database tests keep checking the database refusals they check today (a Signer signature time later than the statement time; a Lease signing time earlier than a Signer's signature or later than the statement time) through the service; the unchanged "not before the Signer was added" check has no committed test. Docs: docs/002 BR-AUDIT-003, docs/005 API-036 and API-038, docs/006 UI-040. New Q-2026-10-14-112 (other times the screens take from the device clock, for example the termination `occurred_at`; the database-level limit that the server's own role can still pass an earlier time; nothing waits).

- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-3d-2b-3, a tie in the newest-first order broken by write order (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, DL-032; DB-SETTLE-005; owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-14-111; migration `20261209_135`; record ops/reviews/V1-056-slice-10e-3d-2b-3-tie-break.md):** within the newest-first order of one correction's payments from a Credit or a Deposit, a tie in time is broken by the order the correction wrote them (`settlement_correction_effects.effect_sequence`, the higher the newer), then by the id; a Deposit payment keeps `application_order` before it. Relaxing exact-text patches of `app.settlement_returned_value_placed`, `app.settlement_returned_value_follow` and, in `app.settlement_return_reversal_source_state`, the two list orders (migration 125) and the newest-first walk of the 10e-3c part applied again (migration 128); no refusal added, no grant changed. Finding for the owner (independent review round 1, confirmed): the premise of Q-2026-10-14-111 does not hold on the real path; the rests a Refund correction writes again do not share one stamped time, because the database already refuses a Credit application that is not later than every earlier event of its Credit (`app.validate_credit_application_source`, migration 41) and a Deposit application that is not later than the Deposit's latest balance event (`app.validate_deposit_application`, migration 86), whose `application_order` is also unique per Final Settlement. So the change is defence in depth: no stored result changes and, in practice, no waiting draft becomes stale. Implementer's design change, confirmed by the round 1 independent reviewer under the run rule of 2026-10-14: the planned "owner's example on the real path" cannot be built, so the tests make the tie by hand on a real correction's two Credit payments, in an owner transaction that is always rolled back (user triggers disabled only inside it), with the lower id written last so write order and id order disagree; a Deposit tie cannot be made even by hand (the unique `application_order`), so the Deposit form is pinned by the exact-text check only.

- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-3d-2c, money of a return that left through a bounced Payment or a wrong receipt (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-REFUND-015, BR-CREDIT-014, DL-032; DB-SETTLE-005, DB-REFUND-002; owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-13-103 point 1; migration `20261210_136`; record ops/reviews/V1-056-slice-10e-3d-2c-return-reversal-returned-refund.md):** a `RETURNED_PAYMENT` or `DEPOSIT_RECEIPT_CORRECTION` correction C recovers the Refunds paid from the money it takes back, reverses the payments made from it and then the source, while it charges an earlier return E's returned Refund R's recipient only R's net, which E had lowered. E's `RETURN_REVERSAL` now charges every part of E's money that left with C's reversal of the source (C's own take; a stretch on a payment C reversed; a Refund part above its net that C's recovery covers) to the person who received R, in one charge-back of R, and nothing else; a part that cannot be followed stays open and is refused with the reworded message "A Credit reversal or a Deposit receipt reversal paid out this return's money in a way this reversal cannot follow yet; reversing it waits for the owner". Implementer's design changes, confirmed by the round 1 independent reviewer under the run rule of 2026-10-14 (none changes who pays or how much): (1) the general Refund correction check counts R's listed charge-back as available for the reversal of E's return (after C the source holds nothing and a Credit's Payment is reversed); (2) R's charge-back takes the reversal's exact time, written right after it, because the deferred Credit and Deposit history checks group events by time and R's net cannot go below 0 first (targeted run T1 failed on both history checks before this); (3) the design's refusal of an R with another active return is not built: the committed test the design names as allowed has one (the later correction's real 500 return), and C charged only the net every return had lowered, so each reversal charges back only its own money, capped by R's net after the reversal; (4) an own use's Refund that such a C charged back sends the source down the walk path, so it is charged back the same way (before: refused as no longer available); (5) the follow function hands such a stretch back with no record id and the source names R. R's charge-back row is dated a few microseconds before the charge Invoice it names (nothing reads that order). Known text item: the charge reads "Recovery of Refund N paid from value whose return was recorded by mistake" (the writer's fixed sentence; wording for slice 11e or the owner).

- **V1-056 sub-slice 10e-3d-2d-2a, a return reversal undoes another Lease's correction's Credit payment through a companion correction (BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-CREDIT-006, BR-CREDIT-014, BR-AUTH-010, DL-032; DB-SETTLE-005; owner answers (a) to Q-2026-10-13-100 point 2 and Q-2026-10-13-103 point 3; migration `20261212_138`; record ops/reviews/V1-056-slice-10e-3d-2d-2a-return-reversal-companion.md):** 10e-3d-2d-2 split into 2d-2a (this), 2d-2b (Refund parts of another Settlement's corrections and another Settlement's bounced Payment take) and 2d-2c (the Q-2026-10-15-113 answer). A `RETURN_REVERSAL` R whose earlier return's money a correction of another closed Final Settlement B paid out through a Credit application reverses that application whole and puts back the rest on its own line, every record on B linked to a companion correction of B: a `SETTLEMENT_CORRECTION` draft of B, variant `RETURN_REVERSAL_COMPANION`, id derived from R's draft and B, written in R's confirm under `final_settlements.prepare` with R's reason, prepared and confirmed by R's approver (implementer's reading of "recorded and confirmed by the same approver"; nothing in it is chosen by the approver: it is B's slice of R's confirmed source), never pending; R and the companion name each other (R's source, result and audit event; the companion's payload and its own audit event on B's Lease). B's own rules pass unchanged. The walk treats a companion as part of its reversal. Lock order: the Settlement draft locks of R's and every companion's Settlement in id order, then their invoice-lease locks and Lease rows in Lease id order, before the lock function. Still refused: a take whose correction also refunded from the Credit (`OTHER_SETTLEMENT`, 2d-2b), another Settlement's Credit reversal or return reversal, and B's own Final Settlement confirm's use (2d-2c). Known limit (fail closed): a payment that a correction of another Settlement, a companion included, changed stops the follow function, so a later reversal of another return whose money sat in it is refused. Implementer's changes to the written design, confirmed by the reviewers of round 2 under the run rule of 2026-10-14 (none changes who pays or how much): (1) the source's `companions` entries carry no derived ids (the confirm derives them from its draft, so nothing in the payload names the companion); (2) the placed function does not merge companions (a reversal taker is always opened by its return reversal record; the merge with an OR join timed out deep follow chains in P1); (3) a new internal `app.settlement_credit_application_owner` decides which Settlement a listed Credit application belongs to (its own, else the Final Settlement of its target's Lease; review round 1 blocker: the rest a `REFUND` correction writes again has none); (4) the successor and follow functions rank only R's own Settlement's `reapply` entries against R's linked put-backs (review round 1); (5) R's and the companions' records are dated after every companion header (review round 1); (6) the companion's audit events are written after `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE`.
